Heimdall · heimdall.com.ua

How Heimdall works

An unofficial air-threat map for Kyiv and the oblasts around it. One Python process reads the official alert data and the channels that track drones and missiles, stores every post before it reads it, rebuilds the map on every pass, and serves the pages and the pushes. This manual is the repository's own documentation, as of version 1.58.60, 27 October 2026: the map first, then everything in full.

Runs
One process on Fly.io, standard library plus cryptography
Reads
3 official alert feeds, 24 Telegram channels, Open-Meteo
Keeps
SQLite on a 1 GB volume, beside the Sentinel-2 tiles
Serves
Tactical /m, Light /light, the guide, the dashboard
Sends
Web Push to a ~10 km cell, never a place

The system on one page

Left to right: what the server reads, the threads that read it, what it keeps, what it works out on every pass, what it serves and sends, and where that lands. Every box opens its section of the reference.

Three journeys

The same machinery, followed through one post, one alert and one bulletin.

A post becomes a mark

§7 · geo.py, State.markers()

  1. Read. The previews every 30 s (10 s in a missile threat); three channels through the Telegram API every 15 s. §7.2
  2. Stored first. Into feed before anything reads it, so a fix to the rules re-reads the old posts too.
  3. Parsed. geo.parse_for_channel: weapon, place, count, and a course, height or speed only when stated. §7.3
  4. Placed in context. The channel's report before and after picks between places that share a name; "Вибух" or "500" alone goes to its last report. §7.3b
  5. Chained. A report continues a track only at a speed the target can fly: 350 km/h, a jet drone 650. §7.4
  6. Aged. Grey once no new report comes, then gone; Kyiv's official all-clear ends every position in the city.
  7. Tagged. #n, Heimdall's number for the target tonight. §7.8
  8. Logged, served, pushed. Once into marker_log; out by /api/markers and the live line; a push to the phones whose cell it nears. §9

An alert becomes a colour

§6 · UkraineAlarm, AlertsInUa, Ubilling

  1. Three feeds. The raion feed (siren.pp.ua or the keyed ukrainealarm API), alerts.in.ua with a token, and the ubilling mirror while the raion feed is down. §6.1
  2. Normalised. One shape per region and alert type; start and end events kept for the replay. §6.3
  3. Reconciled. One source decides by precedence; a source that stops answering stops deciding. §6.5
  4. Coloured. Red, yellow or nothing, raion by raion. No Telegram post can start or end it. §6.6
  5. Shown and sent. The pages paint the raions; the start and the end are pushed to the phones that asked.

A window becomes a bulletin

§7.11 · Bulletins, AIReader

  1. On the clock. The last hour every 20 minutes, the last 24 hours every hour, only while something happened. §7.11
  2. The record. activity_summary: counts, places, times, heights, courses, missiles, weather. Never a post's text, a channel or a reader.
  3. One call. OpenRouter writes it in Ukrainian, English and French, within the month's budget.
  4. Cleaned. No markdown, links, channel names or card numbers; cut at a full sentence.
  5. Kept. Every one for 90 days with its record, then one an hour and one a day for good.
  6. Shown. In the app only with the dashboard's switch on, and only while the server still stands by it.

The ground rules

Each is pinned by tests and argued in the safety rules.

  • COLOUR
    Only the official data sets an alert's colour, raion by raion. Telegram cannot start or end an alert.
  • FACTS
    Never a trajectory, ETA, threat type, altitude or position a post did not state.
  • IMMEDIATE
    Ballistic, Kh-22/32 and MiG-31K concern the whole region: no radius, no countdown.
  • STALE
    A target with no new report turns grey, then goes. Stale is never shown as live.
  • NEWS
    A press release, a tally or reported speech is not an observation of the sky.
  • BANDEROL
    A jet drone, never a cruise missile: in parsing, tags, chaining and every language.
  • PLACES
    Home, Work, Kids and Pin never leave the phone; a push subscription keeps a ~10 km cell.
  • UNSTATED
    A weapon nobody named is "probably a drone", or "drone or missile" while missiles are reported.
  • TRACE
    Every mark leads back to its post, its channel, its time and the rule that read it.
  • SECRETS
    Keys are the owner's Fly secrets; config, VAPID keys and databases are never committed or printed.

Part II · The safety rules

  • The app is never the primary warning
  • No liberties with trajectories
  • Stale is never shown as active
  • A missile is not aged like a drone
  • A new source is on trial until a person trusts it
  • A village is read only where it is (1.43)
  • A shared text says what the post said, and nothing about who shares it (1.54.9)
  • The private channel says what the map says — and, since 1.58.7, what the map shows became of a target (1.58)
  • The bot writes about a place because it was given one (1.58.38)
  • A target quotes the words it was read from (1.58.39)
  • Only where it is heading, and no alert there: not on the map (1.58.7)
  • An emoji is not a weapon (1.58.42)
  • A weapon nobody named is "probably a drone" — never "unknown", never "a drone" (1.54.8)
  • Two channels' reports are one target only when they could be (1.43.1)
  • A post in Russian says the same thing (1.43)
  • A regional warning is for that oblast (1.43)
  • A missile post is for the oblasts it names (1.56.6)
  • A shelter is the city's, a distance is a straight line (1.57)
  • The nuclear event cannot be switched on by accident
  • A warning is only raised when something was actually reported
  • A sound for what concerns this reader, once (1.58.44)
  • The approach signal follows the posts, not the target (1.58.56)
  • A 3D model says what it is, never where it is going (1.58.45)
  • Feedback asks for no place, and the jar never asks while something is in the air (1.58.46)
  • Alert colours must match the official app
  • A number of drones is an estimate, and says so (1.58.20)
  • A limit never stands between a reader and the alert (1.58.17)
  • A hromada's alert is the hromada's (1.58.16)
  • Altitude is read, never guessed
  • A threat type is never invented
  • A fire is not an explosion, and it does not last all night
  • A carrier is never a mark
  • A reply is its own message
  • What was carried is as old as where it came from
  • A bridge with nothing reported is not a safe bridge
  • The card says what is known about your place, and nothing more
  • The heat map is statistics, and says so
  • A ballistic report is not an official alert, and a retraction ends it
  • The replay is the past, and looks like it
  • The guide says what the official guidance says
  • The history layer never buries the live picture
  • What was destroyed decides what it means
  • A press release is not an observation of the sky
  • A name that merely starts the same is not a match
  • A stated part of an oblast is not the oblast centre
  • A side of a town is not the town (1.45.1)
  • "From the north" is a course south — wherever the words are (1.58.41)
  • Where it is, and where it is going, are different facts
  • A glyph points where the post said, or it does not point at all
  • A heading over a list is not a sighting
  • The weapon belongs to the line that names it
  • A tally of the night is not a sky
  • One weapon, one symbol — and it never turns
  • An outcome with no place named belongs to the region, not to its centre
  • An explosion is a moment, not a state
  • In Chernihiv oblast a drone faces into Ukraine (1.56.3)
  • "Heading to your village" is a thing the post has to say
  • A push that cannot be acted on costs the ones that can
  • The one row that could say where somebody sleeps
  • What the app said is kept, not just what it was told
  • "High confidence" says how sharply it was read, not that it is true
  • Nothing is drawn where the source said it is clear
  • Overlapping explosions are grouped, never stacked
  • Every number is traceable
  • The AI writes bulletins, and puts nothing on the map (1.54)
  • A language model is a second opinion, never a source (1.50)
  • A target only the AI reader found (1.52)
  • The AI reads every 10 minutes and writes up the night (1.53)
  • A target's tag is a number, not a designation (1.51)
  • A pin on a street, found on the phone (1.56)
  • Where drones usually go is the dashboard's, not a reader's (1.55)
  • Operational security
  • When in doubt

Part I · The reference

Heimdall — technical documentation

Documented version: 1.58 · last updated 2026-10-27 (1.58.60)

The repository is Dread92/Heimdall-map. The app's address is https://heimdall.com.ua (1.58.34, 18 October 2026); the Fly app keeps its first name, kyiv-air-watch-gb, and its first address keeps working (§15 has the move).

This is the complete technical reference: what runs, where the data comes from, how a Telegram post becomes a mark on a map, how an official alert becomes a colour, what is stored, what is sent, and how to change any of it safely. It is updated with every patch — see §18. tests/test_docs.py fails the build when this file, APP_VERSION and CHANGELOG.md disagree, or when a route, a config key, an environment variable or a database table exists in the code but not here.

The rules the code follows are in SAFETY.md. This document says how; SAFETY.md says why not otherwise. Deployment walkthrough: DEPLOY.md. Strings and languages: I18N.md. The regression corpus: CORPUS.md. French quick start: DEMARRAGE-FR.md.

1. What it is#

An unofficial air-raid and drone tracker for Kyiv city and Kyiv oblast first, and the oblasts switched on around them (the six around Kyiv, then Odesa, Kharkiv, Dnipro, Mykolaiv and, since 1.45, seven more — §7.1). One Python process — standard library only, plus cryptography for Web Push — reads:

  • the official «Повітряна тривога» alert data (which raions are under alert, at which level),
  • 25 public Telegram channels that report where drones and missiles are: the four Kyiv live trackers, three country-wide channels (the Air Force among them) and one live tracker per oblast — two for Dnipropetrovsk oblast since 1.58.25, @nikalert for Nikopol (§7.1) — most through their t.me/s/ previews, three through the Telegram API,
  • the Air Force's summaries and the carriers' take-offs and launches, read from those posts (§7.5, §7.6), and the nights' weather (Open-Meteo, §7.6),

and serves its pages from the same data — and, with the owner's key, has an AI model write a bulletin of the last hour and the last 24 hours from what it recorded (§7.11):

Page Address For
Tactical /m (also /, /k, /kyiv) The full map: every mark with its evidence, the feed, alerts, statistics, display modes, layers.
Light /light (also /l) One place (one of the four pins, named by their owner): its raion's official status, what is within 30 km, what is heading there.
Guide /guide What to do, weapon by weapon and situation by situation — the official guidance, in three languages, offline (1.35, §10.5).
Privacy /privacy What stays on the phone, what the server receives and keeps, what only an opt-in sends — in three languages; the address Google Play asks for (1.58.59, §11).

Both install as separate PWAs (manifest ids /m and /light — unchanged by the 1.29 rename, so Android offers the new name and icon as an update of the installed app; an iPhone keeps an installed app's name and icon until it is added again). Production runs on Fly.io as kyiv-air-watch-gb (one performance-1x machine — a dedicated CPU — with 2 GB since 1.41.1, a 1 GB volume for the database; [[vm]] in fly.toml, which every deploy applies).

2. Principles the code enforces#

Summarised here, argued in SAFETY.md, and each pinned by tests:

  • The official alert decides the colour. Red / yellow / nothing comes only from the official data, by raion. A Telegram post can neither start nor end an alert (OFFICIAL_ALERTS_FROM_TELEGRAM = False).
  • Nothing is invented. No trajectory, ETA, threat type, altitude or position the post did not state. A course is drawn only if the post reported it; an ETA is a band, only toward the watched place, only for a stated course, never for ballistic / Kh-22 / MiG-31K.
  • Weapons that outrun a radius ignore the radius. Ballistic, supersonic (Kh-22/32) and MiG-31K are "immediate": they concern the whole region (150 km), with no distance and no countdown.
  • Stale is never shown as live. A target with no new report turns grey, then disappears.
  • A news item is not an observation of the sky. Press releases, tallies and reported speech never become marks.
  • Banderol (S-8000) is a jet drone, never a cruise missile — in parsing, tags, chaining and every language.
  • Places stay on the phone. The four watched places are never sent to the server; a push subscription stores a ~10 km cell, never a village; the Light page works out the oblast and raion on the phone.
  • Every mark is traceable to the post, the channel, the time and the rule that read it.

3. System overview#

 sources                          app/server.py (one process)                                 out
 ───────                          ───────────────────────────                                 ───
 official alerts ─────────▶ UkraineAlarm / AlertsInUa / Ubilling ──apply_snapshot──┐
  (siren.pp.ua or ukrainealarm,       every 10–30 s                                 │
   alerts.in.ua, ubilling mirror)                                                   ▼
                                                                         State: alerts → level by raion ──▶ /api/state
 24 Telegram channels ────▶ Telegram (t.me/s previews, 30 s; 10 s in a missile threat)        │
  (3 Kyiv trackers, 3 country, ─▶ TelegramAPI (chyste_nebo …, 15 s; polls, never subscribes)  │
   1+ per oblast)                    │ ingest(): stored BEFORE it is read                      │
                                     ▼                                                        ▼
                               Store (SQLite on the Fly volume): feed, alerts, events, marker_log, outcomes,
                               targets, target_points, track_moves, af_reports, air_reports, weather, ai_bulletins, kv …
                                     │
                                     ▼
                     State.markers(), re-read on every pass:
                       geo.parse_for_channel (gazetteer + villages of the oblast)
                       → _marks_from_posts (the channel's report before / after: shared names, a height alone)
                       → _chain_and_prune (one target only at a speed it can fly: 350 / 650 km/h)
                       → _tag_marks (#n for the night) → log_markers ──────────▶ /api/markers, /api/stream (SSE)
                                     │                                            /api/replay, /api/trail
                                     ├──▶ Pusher, proximity, bridges ──▶ Web Push ──▶ phones (~10 km cell only)
                                     ├──▶ activity_summary ──▶ bulletins (OpenRouter, 3 languages) ──▶ /api/bulletin
                                     └──▶ patterns, hourly: target_points → track_moves ──▶ /api/admin/patterns 🔒
 Open-Meteo ──▶ Weather ─────────────┘        Translations (DeepL / Google) ──▶ feed in EN / FR
 Sentinel-2 file on the volume ──▶ /tiles/sat · Esri (owner's key) ──▶ /tiles/esri
                                                                pages: /m Tactical · /light Light · /guide · /admin 🔒

Everything that arrives is stored before it is interpreted: a parsing bug is always replayable against the real post, and a fix to the rules applies to posts already in the database.

4. Repository layout#

Heimdall-map/
├── app/
│   ├── server.py           HTTP server, API, SSE, source pollers, SQLite store, marker pipeline, push
│   ├── geo.py              Ukrainian parsing: gazetteer, declensions, types, headings, counts, phases, altitude
│   ├── translate.py        offline UK→EN glossary; DeepL / Google machine translation with a circuit breaker
│   ├── ai_reader.py        1.50: the AI reader in shadow mode (OpenRouter) — prompt, schema, checks, comparison (§7.7)
│   ├── chanpost.py         1.58: the owner's private Telegram channel — a post per report, its outcome, the all-clear (§9c); not started since 1.58.38, its words and picture are the bot's
│   ├── tgbot.py            1.58.38: the owner's Telegram bot, one to one — each user's places, a post naming one or a target within 10 km (§9d)
│   ├── mapshot.py          1.58: its picture — the map around one target, drawn with Pillow in the app's style (§9c)
│   ├── areas.py            1.58.7: which official alert area a point is in, and is it under alert (`held`, §7.4; §9c)
│   ├── limits.py           1.58.17: the public API's abuse limits — per client, on what costs the server (§12.1)
│   ├── adminauth.py        1.58.21: the dashboard's login — sessions, the sign-in limits, its security log (§12.2)
│   └── push.py             Web Push: VAPID ES256 JWT + RFC 8291 aes128gcm, on `cryptography` only
├── static/
│   ├── kyiv.html           Tactical page (one self-contained file)
│   ├── light.html          Light page (one self-contained file)
│   ├── i18n.js             every UI string in EN / UK / FR, place / raion / river / channel names
│   ├── glyphs.js           the weapon silhouettes (G, GLYPH_BY_TYPE), shared by both pages
│   ├── models3d.js         the threats in 3D on the Tactical page: each weapon built from shapes, photographed into a
│   │                       strip of frames (1.58.45; an ES module, loaded on demand)
│   ├── vendor/             three-m3d.min.js — three.js 0.185.1 (MIT, three.LICENSE), only the classes models3d.js uses
│   ├── jar3d.webp          1.58.47: the donation jar in 3D (models3d.js's jar), a strip of 36 frames — scripts/render_jar.js
│   ├── threats3d.webp      1.58.55: the guide's five weapons turning (models3d.js's spin), a row each — scripts/render_models.js
│   ├── kyiv-map.json       27 oblasts + all raions, in km around Kyiv (geoBoundaries)
│   ├── light-map.json      raions of the 8 oblasts + nearby oblast outlines, rivers, roads, towns (built)
│   ├── light-map-x.json    raions of the 11 oblasts farther out — fetched only for a place there (built, 1.43)
│   ├── map-ua.json         rivers, main roads, airports and power plants of the whole country (built, 1.45)
│   ├── towns-ua.json       raion centres and towns of 10,000+ of every oblast, for finding one's way (built, 1.49)
│   ├── kyiv-districts.json the 10 city districts of Kyiv (OSM)
│   ├── hromadas/           1.58.16: each official hromada's outline, `<oblast uid>.json` (OCHA COD-AB, built) — fetched for an oblast with a hromada alert
│   ├── hromadas.js         1.58.16: their test on the phone (is this place in that hromada?), shared by both pages (§6.4)
│   ├── streets/            1.56: every settlement and named street of each oblast, `<uid>.json` (OSM, built; LICENSE.md: ODbL)
│   ├── streets.js          1.56: their search, on the phone — loaded by both pages' pin pickers when they open (§10.6)
│   ├── shelters.json       1.57: the shelters Kyiv city lists (KMDA, ІАС «Майно»; built by scripts/build_shelters.py)
│   ├── shelters.js         1.57: the nearest ones, on the phone — loaded by both pages for a pin in Kyiv (§10.7)
│   ├── lightmap.js         1.57.2: the Light radar's satellite / map background — loaded when asked for (§10.2)
│   ├── ukraine-map.json    oblast outlines in an affine lon/lat projection (Light page fallback lookup)
│   ├── admin.html          private dashboard: usage, source health, channels, lead time (/admin, ADMIN_KEY)
│   ├── admin-login.html    1.58.21: its sign-in page (EN / UK) — the key typed here, never in the address
│   ├── index.html, mobile.html   older desktop / mobile views (/desktop, /ua)
│   ├── guide.html          what to do, weapon by weapon (/guide, 1.35): self-contained, content in its JSON block
│   ├── privacy.html        the privacy policy (/privacy, 1.58.59): three languages, the same facts — §11
│   ├── sw.js               service worker: push display, notification clicks, the pages and the guide kept for when the server is unreachable
│   ├── manifest.json, light.webmanifest   the two PWAs
│   ├── heimdall-*.png      the app icons (any / maskable / iPhone), the favicon; logo-hd-badge.png Android's badge
│   └── logo*.png (NGO 07300), bf-logo*.png (Black Flame Studio), ukraine-map.LICENSE.md
├── data/
│   ├── ua_regions.json     official region id → oblast / parent raion / map shape (built)
│   ├── regional_places.json the villages and city districts of the oblasts with a tracker (GeoNames, built, 1.43)
│   ├── extra_places.json   the villages of Kyiv oblast (built, 1.52; read by the rules too since 1.54.7)
│   └── corpus.jsonl        regression corpus of real posts and their expected readings
├── scripts/
│   ├── build_geo.py        rebuilds data/ua_regions.json and static/light-map.json (`--light`: the Light maps only)
│   ├── build_three.sh      rebuilds static/vendor/three-m3d.min.js from three-m3d.entry.js (npm three + esbuild; 1.58.45)
│   ├── render_jar.js       renders static/jar3d.webp from models3d.js's jar (Node + Playwright; 1.58.47)
│   ├── render_models.js    renders static/threats3d.webp, the guide's weapons turning (Node + Playwright; 1.58.55)
│   ├── build_hromadas.py   rebuilds static/hromadas/ from OCHA's ukr_admin_boundaries.gdb (pyogrio, shapely; 1.58.16)
│   ├── build_places.py     rebuilds data/regional_places.json from GeoNames (1.43)
│   ├── build_extra_places.py rebuilds data/extra_places.json from GeoNames (1.52)
│   ├── build_mapdata.py    rebuilds static/map-ua.json from Natural Earth (1.45)
│   ├── build_towns.py      rebuilds static/towns-ua.json from GeoNames (1.49)
│   ├── build_streets.py    rebuilds static/streets/ from the 27 regional OSM extracts (pyosmium, shapely; 1.56)
│   ├── build_shelters.py   rebuilds static/shelters.json from the city's shelter list saved in a browser (1.57)
│   ├── build_imagery.py    builds the satellite layer (imagery/sat-ua.mbtiles) from ESA WorldCover / Sentinel-2 (1.47)
│   ├── upload-imagery.bat  puts imagery/sat-ua.mbtiles on the Fly volume (1.47)
│   ├── set-esri-key.bat    one-time: the owner's Esri key for the closest zooms, into Fly's secrets (1.48)
│   ├── set-admin-key.bat   1.58.22: a new dashboard key, made on the PC, shown once, into Fly's secrets (§12.2)
│   ├── set-openrouter-key.bat  one-time: the owner's OpenRouter key for the AI reader, into Fly's secrets (1.50)
│   ├── set-telegram-channel.bat one-time: the owner's bot token and private channel, into Fly's secrets (1.58, §9c)
│   ├── build_manual.py     rebuilds docs/manual.html — the whole documentation as one page, with the system map (1.54.9)
│   ├── manual/             its template (template.html) and the system map, drawn by hand (map-svg.html)
│   ├── corpus.py           add / review / pull corpus cases
│   ├── release.bat         ships a patch: checks all is committed, fly deploy, git push, shows the live version
│   ├── telegram-login.bat  one-time Telegram sign-in for the API reader (runs telegram_login.py)
│   ├── github-push.bat     creates the private GitHub repo and pushes (browser sign-in via GitHub CLI)
│   └── start.sh, dev.sh, tunnel.bat
├── tests/                  pytest suites (see §14) and tests/fixtures/
├── docs/                   this file, SAFETY, DEPLOY, I18N, CORPUS, DEMARRAGE-FR, PLAY (Google Play: listing, data safety, beta)
├── .github/workflows/      ci.yml (tests on 3.9 and 3.12), fly-deploy.yml (optional auto-deploy)
├── Dockerfile · fly.toml · render.yaml · deploy-fly.bat · start.bat
├── config.example.json     template for config.json (config.json itself is never committed)
└── CHANGELOG.md · README.md · CONTRIBUTING.md · SECURITY.md · LICENSE

5. Runtime, configuration and secrets#

5.1 The process#

python app/server.py (--demo for fake alerts, --port N). One ThreadingHTTPServer and these threads:

Thread What it does Cadence
UkraineAlarm official alerts by raion (keyless proxy or keyed API) /alerts/status every 10 s, full list on change or every 30 s
AlertsInUa official alerts from alerts.in.ua, when a token is set 15 s
Ubilling oblast-level mirror; applies only while the raion source is down 30 s
Telegram reads the channels' t.me/s/ previews (all but those the API reads) 30 s; 10 s while a missile threat is open or a threat is within 12 km of a Kyiv bridge (1.34)
TelegramAPI reads telegram_api_channels (chyste_nebo) through the Telegram client API — polls the channel, never subscribes to updates last 20 posts every 15 s (8 s in a missile threat)
Vitals 1.41: the process's own CPU, the CPU the host took away (steal — on a shared Fly vCPU, throttling), memory, how late its own clock woke it; every request over 2 s (path and time only); every start with its build (kv.boots, last 100). On the dashboard's Live tab (/api/online → process) 10 s 1.58.3: each start says how the run before it ended — a stop signal (last-exit.json beside the database, written by the SIGINT/SIGTERM handler: a deploy, a secret set, Fly moving the machine), the watchdog, or nothing written (a crash, an out-of-memory kill); the server panel counts the week's starts by cause (restarts_7d)
Watchdog the database and alert-state locks must be free within 15 s; a held lock logs every thread's stack, five in a row (~2 min) → exit, Fly restarts it; a starved process is only logged (load, not a deadlock) 20 s
Digests not started since 1.58.37 (the owner: "the nights and weeks tab can go away"; §8b) — the AI's report on a period of nights (§7.10) does it from the full record; the ones written stay in digests —
Translations machine-translates feed posts into a language somebody reads on demand, ~3 calls/s max
bulletins 1.54, with OPENROUTER_API_KEY: the AI's bulletins (§7.11) — on the clock, the last hour and the last 24 hours once an hour at :00 (1.56.1; the last hour was every 20 minutes), only while there is activity, one call each in three languages → ai_bulletins; or one the dashboard asks for. (Until 1.53 an ai-reader thread read the posts — §7.7; retired) 20 s; ≤ ai_reader_monthly_usd a month
night-reports 1.53: after ai_report_hour (Kyiv), the AI's report on the night before (§7.10) → ai_reports; or a night the dashboard asks for; 1.58.37: a period of up to 31 nights the dashboard asks for → ai_period_reports 5 min
Pusher sends Web Push: one notification at a time, each to all its phones at once through push.Sender (32 workers, one kept-open HTTPS connection per push service per worker, VAPID signed once an hour per service) queue
streams every open page's live line (StreamHub, §5.4): one thread, non-blocking sockets (epoll), the data sent as changes events + every 15 / 5 / 2 s
proximity per-subscriber distance checks → push continuous
warm 1.58.3: 45 s after a start, /api/stats's 14 days once in the background — the first ask after a start parsed four days of posts for the explosions while a reader waited (16.6 s after the 1.58 deploy) once
bot-in · bot-watch · bot-out 1.58.38, with TG_BOT_TOKEN: the owner's Telegram bot, one to one (§9d) — bot-in reads its private messages (getUpdates, a 25 s long poll) and answers them; bot-watch compares the marks with every user's places; bot-out sends, at most 25 a second. (1.58–1.58.37: the thread channel, the private channel of §9c, no longer started) long poll · 5 s · 50 ms
AlertsInUa.backfill a month of history for the favourite regions once at start
AFBackfill the Air Force summaries of the last 14 days that af_reports lacks: first from the stored feed, then from t.me/s/kpszsu?before=… pages (at most 240, stops once nothing is missing); since 1.33 also the carriers (parse_air_report) in every stored post, once per parser version (kv.air_parsed) 60 s after start, then daily
bridges every 5 s: a Kyiv bridge whose state rose (nothing → near → at, §9b) is written to bridge_events and pushed to the phones that asked for the bridges (topic bridges, in their language), at most once per bridge every 10 min — a "near" turning "at" excepted 5 s
patterns 1.55 (§7.12): each tagged target's moves, from one reported position to the next (target_points → track_moves, kept for good): the last two days' nights again every hour, 1.58.37 every night kept whose moves were never built (Patterns.gaps), and once per MOVES_VERSION every night whose points are all still kept 90 s after start, then hourly
Weather the last nights' weather at the launch areas and Kyiv from Open-Meteo (WEATHER_POINTS; 92 nights the first time, then 10) into weather 90 s after start, then every 6 h (1 h after a failure)

One computation for everybody (State.cached): /api/markers, /api/state, /api/feed, /api/stats, /api/impacts are built once per change (the publish sequence seq) and at most every few seconds, by one thread while the others wait, serialised and gzipped once; every page gets the same bytes (_send_cached). The proximity pushes use the same marks (markers_now()). Nothing in a request path calls the network: the marks' English text is the offline glossary (machine translation is the Translations worker's alone). The listen backlog is 128. Measured on a 200-post window: ~1,000 requests/s, p95 ≈ 50 ms, where 1.23 managed 3/s.

poll_gap() shortens the official-source and Telegram intervals while a missile threat is open. The live line beats faster with it (msl 0 → every 15 s, 1 → 5 s, 2 → 2 s); a page whose line is down falls back to asking /api/version at 15 / 5 / 1 s, as before 1.29.

At start the process raises its open-file limit to what the system allows (up to 65,536): every open page is a socket, and Linux's default of 1,024 would stop the server long before the hub does.

5.4 The live line (1.29)#

Before 1.29 each open page held one server thread in /api/stream, and every new post made every page download the marks, the feed and the alerts again — five requests per page per post. On the 256 MB machine of the time Linux allowed ~2,000 threads, so ~1,700 open pages was a hard stop, and the CPU cost grew with pages × posts.

Now Handler._stream sends the headers and hands the socket to StreamHub (the connection is not closed: Server.shutdown_request skips it). The hub writes to every page from one thread, each message serialised once. What it sends:

  • the old events, unchanged, for a page that asked for them (ev=1, the default): start, end, threat, update, feed, eradar, tr, notice — the Tactical page raises its toasts and sounds from them;
  • hello on connecting: {v, msl, hb, now, h: {part: hash}} — which version of each part is current;
  • sync after a burst of events (150 ms to gather it) and at least every hb seconds (15 / 5 / 2 by missile level — the heartbeat): {v, msl, hb, now} plus, for each part that changed and that the page asked for (sync=mk,st,fd), what changed:
    • mk (the marks, by id) and fd (the feed's 150 posts, by post_id): set = changed entries, same order; pre + n = new entries at the head (a new post, a new mark); ids = a new order; all = the whole list when there is nothing to diff against;
    • st (the alerts state): act = the active alerts as a list (by location_uid|alert_type), sub / unset = keys of oblasts and sources that changed, set = other values whole. (1.46: sources is {name: {ok}} — the time of each reading changed at every poll and was resent to every page at every beat; the detail is the dashboard's, /api/health.) Every part carries b (the version it starts from) and h (the version it leads to). The full answers (/api/markers, /api/state, /api/feed) carry the same h. A page applies a change only when it holds exactly b (static/live.js, LiveSync.part), otherwise it fetches that part in full — nothing is guessed. tests/test_live.py checks that the deltas rebuild exactly what the server serves.

The pages: while the line beats (a message within 3 × hb), they ask nothing; the Tactical page checks /api/version once a minute (build, safety) and does a full reload every 10 minutes. A silent line is reopened, and the page asks at its old cadence until it beats again. The Light page takes sync=mk,st without events (p=light&ev=0); the Tactical page in blackout mode takes mk,st and still fetches its 25-post feed.

A page that stops reading is dropped once 512 KB are waiting for it (its EventSource reconnects). 1.58.24: a page keeps one line at a time (opening one closes the one before), and a line that keeps failing is opened again later and later — 3 s (Light: 5 s), doubled at each failure up to 30 s, ±25 % so the pages do not come back on the same second after a restart — while the pulse keeps the page current; a line that said hello starts again from 3 s. Nothing about a reader is kept: a connection is a socket, a language and which page it is; /api/online counts them.

Measured on the demo feed (one core, 2026-09-28): 9,000 open pages — 8 threads, 62 MB, ~80 ms of CPU per second, a change reaching the last page ~0.1 s after the first; ~25 KB per page per minute under the demo's heavy churn. The same pages on 1.28 needed 9,000 threads and ~45,000 requests per post.

5.2 Configuration keys#

config.json is created from DEFAULT_CONFIG on first run and is git-ignored. config.example.json is the committed template.

Key Default Meaning

5.3 Environment variables#

Environment variables win over config.json. On Fly they are set as secrets (fly secrets set …).

Variable Meaning
PORT Listening port (Fly sets it).
DB_PATH SQLite file (Fly: on the volume).
SAT_TILES The satellite layer's MBTiles file (1.47). Default: sat-ua.mbtiles next to DB_PATH — on Fly, /data/sat-ua.mbtiles on the volume.
CONFIG_PATH Alternative config.json location.
ANDROID_CERT_SHA256 1.57.6: the SHA-256 fingerprint(s) of the Android app's signing key (§10.8), as keytool or apksigner prints them, several separated by commas — set with scripts/set-android-key.bat. Public, not a secret. Unset: /.well-known/assetlinks.json is an empty list.
ANDROID_PACKAGE 1.57.6: the Android app's identifier, default com.blackflamestudio.heimdall.
ADMIN_KEY Protects /admin and its APIs only. The map stays public. 1.58.21: typed at the sign-in page (§12.2), or sent by a script in an X-Admin-Key header — never in an address.
FLY_APP_NAME Set by Fly itself. 1.58.17: without Fly's Fly-Client-IP header nobody is limited (the peer would be Fly's proxy); 1.58.24: that header is believed only when this is set (§12.1); 1.58.21: the dashboard's session cookie is Secure.
ACCESS_KEY Puts the whole app behind a key. Never on the public app. 1.58.35: a sign-in page and a 180-day session (hacc), never the key in the address; X-Access-Key for scripts (§11.1).
ALERTS_IN_UA_TOKEN alerts.in.ua token.
UKRAINEALARM_KEY Official ukrainealarm API key.
DEEPL_KEY DeepL key (…:fx = free plan → api-free.deepl.com).
GOOGLE_TRANSLATE_KEY Google Cloud Translation key.
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY Turns on the written summaries of each night / week on the dashboard (ai_key).
AI_MODEL Overrides ai_model.
ESRI_KEY Turns on the satellite layer's closest zooms (Esri World Imagery, esri_key, 1.48). Set only by scripts/set-esri-key.bat.
OPENROUTER_API_KEY Turns on the AI reader in shadow mode (openrouter_key, 1.50, §7.7). Set only by scripts/set-openrouter-key.bat.
AI_READER_MODEL Overrides ai_reader_model.
TRANSLATOR google (default) allows the free Google endpoint as last resort; anything else disables it.
TG_API_ID Telegram API app id (my.telegram.org).
TG_API_HASH Telegram API app hash.
CANONICAL_HOST The app's own address (heimdall.com.ua since 1.58.34). Pages opened elsewhere show the move banner (§9); the channel's posts link there (§9c). Set by scripts/set-domain.bat.
TG_SESSION The signed-in Telegram session (a key to that account). Set only by scripts/telegram-login.bat, via fly secrets import.
TG_BOT_TOKEN 1.58: the owner's bot (@BotFather) that posted in his private channel (§9c); 1.58.38: the bot that writes to its users one to one (§9d). Set only by scripts/set-telegram-channel.bat, via fly secrets import; never logged.
TG_CHANNEL_ID 1.58: that channel's id (-100…), found by the same script. 1.58.38: no longer read (the channel is not started); fly secrets unset TG_CHANNEL_ID removes it.
PUBLIC_URL 1.58: the address the channel's posts link to (/m). 1.58.34: unset, it is https:// + CANONICAL_HOST, else https://heimdall.com.ua (chanpost.public_url).

Secrets live only in config.json (git-ignored) or in Fly secrets. They are never committed, never printed by the scripts, and .dockerignore keeps config.json out of the image.

5.5 What the process spends its CPU on (1.46)#

Measured on 7 Oct 2026 with py-spy on a copy of the service, the channels read at the ballistic cadence (every 5 s), the feed of that evening loaded: 87 % of the CPU went into building TLS contexts. urllib.request.urlopen() without a context makes a new one per request and loads the system's ~150 root certificates into it — 15–25 ms of CPU each time, for each of the ~20 channel previews and the alert sources, every round. One shared context (server._SSL_CTX, translate._CTX, passed to every urlopen) took the process from 10 % of a core to 1.6 %; with 500 pages on the live line and a full page load every second, 4.1 % and 113 MB of memory. This is very likely what spent a shared vCPU's burst balance on attack nights (§15, "throttling") before 1.41.1 moved to a dedicated CPU.

Also in 1.46: the pages' state carries whether each source works and nothing more (§5.4); the oblasts a post names are read once per post (State._named_cache, they were ~40 % of each rebuild of the marks); /api/places is built once an hour per set of oblasts and sent gzipped (State.cached), and the Tactical page asks for oblast=all only when the villages layer is turned on (loadVillages); SQLite runs synchronous=NORMAL (with WAL: a sync at each checkpoint, not at each commit).

Not changed, already economical: the live line (one thread, one serialisation per combination of parts, deltas), the 304s on static files, Web Push's pooled connections.

5.6 Zooming the map (1.49)#

The map is one SVG in kilometres; the view is its viewBox. Measured in Chromium with the CPU slowed 4× (a mid-range phone), a pinch of 200 frames: every change of the viewBox restyled every element sized by --k and laid out every label again — 50 ms a frame (20 fps), the main thread busy 100 % of the time (style 49 %, layout 28 %). A change of scale lays the SVG's text out again even when it is only a CSS transform of the SVG or of an element around it. So, while a pinch or the wheel goes on (zoomAt(…, true) → fastVB), the map drawn last is moved and scaled on the screen with a CSS transform (svg#map, will-change:transform, from its top-left corner), the names of places are hidden for that moment (svg.zoomg :not(.mk)>text; the marks and their labels stay), and 150 ms after the last movement the new view is drawn once (applyVB()). The view's arithmetic uses the map's box without the transform (SR()). Result: 16.7 ms a frame (60 fps) on the slowed phone and on a computer, the main thread busy 20 %; the redraw at rest costs one frame of ~70 ms. A one-finger drag still moves the viewBox (no change of scale: cheap).

1.54.3, the owner: "sometimes the map flickers when I zoom in or out" — black patches over the map, the left column blank. The scaled map is one GPU picture only while nothing inside it moves; every mark's halo, a blinking glyph, a marching course ray made the browser draw the whole map again at every frame, at the scaled size — and a long wheel zoom scaled it to many times the screen, more than the GPU keeps. Now, while the gesture lasts, every animation inside the map is paused (svg.zoomg *{animation-play-state:paused}), and a scale past 3× or below ⅓ is drawn again at its own size at once (fastVB → applyVB()), so the scaled picture never grows past three screens. The glow of an oblast with a target somewhere in it no longer breathes (a large clipped area repainted 60 times a second for nothing a reader needs).

Tiles: the tiles of the zoom before stay under the new ones until all of these have loaded or failed (tileSweep, 4 s at most), so a change of zoom never shows the black background between two pictures.

Zoomed in, nothing large is dashed or animated (svg.deep, past ~90 px a km). Chrome draws a dashed line dash by dash along its whole length — the 50 km ring around Kyiv is 25,000 dashes at 1 km across — and an animated one again at every frame: 1.48 could stall a computer's page ("page not responding") at the closest zooms. There the rings, the zones, the occupied areas' outline, the region rings, the reach and course rays and the tracks are drawn solid and still, the occupied areas in a plain tint.

Lengths sized by the zoom keep their digits (kx(), KDIG): with two decimals, at 1 km across a drone's scale(0.0007) was written scale(0.00) and the mark vanished; marks are placed to the metre.

6. Official alert data#

6.1 Sources and precedence#

Source Granularity Key Role
api.ukrainealarm.com — the backend of the government app oblast, raion, hromada; red/yellow level; official reason text UKRAINEALARM_KEY Primary when a key is set.
siren.pp.ua — keyless read-only proxy of the same API same none Primary without a key (production today).
alerts.in.ua oblast, raion, hromada, city; level; threats ALERTS_IN_UA_TOKEN Primary when a token is set (the raion source then only reports health).
ubilling mirror one on/off per oblast, usually no start time none Stand-in only, while the raion source has not answered for 90 s.

The mirror lights an oblast as soon as any raion in it is under alert. That is why it can only stand in: it painted the whole of Kyiv oblast red while Boryspil raion had been clear for ten minutes (24 Sep 2026). When it stands in, the Light page says "official data for the whole oblast only — your raion is unknown", and an alert with no known start shows no "since" (since_known: false).

1.58.31, standing in says only what it knows (Ubilling.stand_in). The owner, 15 Oct 2026, 10:17: "everything is still red while the official app has lifted the alert in several raions" — siren.pp.ua had not answered since 09:39, and the mirror painted Kyiv, Cherkasy and Chernihiv oblasts whole and red (it gives no level) while the government app showed four raions of Kyiv oblast, yellow. Now:

  • an oblast the mirror says is clear has no raion under alert either: the raion and hromada alerts the silent source left there end (a second apply_snapshot over those levels, scope = the clear oblasts);
  • an oblast the mirror says is under alert, once the raion source has answered at least once (last_ok), is unsure, with raions_as_of (that source's last answer) and a note saying so: the raions as it last showed them stay as they were, and the oblast's summary is "in part" (P), not whole. The Tactical page leaves such an alert off the oblast's fill: the raions under a known alert keep their colour, every other raion of the oblast is hatched (.rn.Q, .nb.oQ, pattern #hatchQ) — unknown, never red, never clear; a watched place there reads "alert in part of the oblast" (P), and the band adds "official raion data unavailable since 09:39: the raions as they were then, the others unknown" (b_unsure). The Light page reads such a place as P with "your raion is unknown". The city of Kyiv is one unit: the mirror is exact there, never unsure. With no raion source ever heard (a boot with it down), the whole oblast, as before.
  • Production reads the raion data through the keyless siren.pp.ua; a key of the API itself (UKRAINEALARM_KEY) or an alerts.in.ua token (ALERTS_IN_UA_TOKEN), set by the owner as Fly secrets, removes that single point of failure.

The ukrainealarm reader polls /alerts/status every 10 s and reads the full /alerts list when the change index moves — and at least every 30 s regardless, so a level change on an alert already on (yellow → red) is never more than half a minute behind (it was up to 2 min before 1.19).

6.2 The official levels#

Since 11 Sep 2026 each alert carries the government app's danger level:

Level Official meaning Shown as
red massed drone, missile, or drone-and-missile threat red; "AIR RAID ALERT"
yellow drone threat yellow; "ALERT · YELLOW LEVEL"
none (mirror) unknown treated as red — never as "maybe"

The official reason ("Дронова загроза (жовтий рівень)") is kept word for word in the alert's notes and shown as-is in Ukrainian; EN/FR show the level's definition.

6.3 The normalised alert#

Every source is normalised to one shape (dicts in State.active, rows in alerts):

key, source, location_uid, location_title, location_title_en, location_type (oblast | raion | hromada | city), oblast_uid, alert_type (air_raid | artillery_shelling | urban_fights | chemical | nuclear | info), alert_level (red | yellow | null), started_at, finished_at, notes, threats[] — plus, from the official raion source, raion_uid, raion_key (the map shape), raion_title; and from the mirror, since_known.

started_at for the official source is the earliest of the alert's lastUpdate and its levels' createdAt, normalised to YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ.

6.4 From an official id to a place on the map#

The API names a raion or hromada by a bare numeric id. data/ua_regions.json, built by scripts/build_geo.py from the official region list, maps:

  • states: official oblast id → this app's oblast uid (Crimea 9999 → 29);
  • districts: raion id → name, oblast, shape key on the map (boryspil, korostenskyi …);
  • communities: hromada id → name, parent raion (cities listed at the top level, like "м. Харків та … громада", are hromadas of their oblast, not the oblast);
  • ignore: the API's test region.

The hromadas' outlines (1.58.16). static/hromadas/<oblast uid>.json (scripts/build_hromadas.py) holds the outline of 1,456 of the 1,457 official hromadas, keyed by the official id, with its parent raion's shape key — from OCHA's COD-AB for Ukraine, admin level 3 (ukr_admin_boundaries.gdb, edition v05 valid 2025-09-01, CC BY 3.0 IGO; the raion outlines are an older edition of the same data set), matched by the Ukrainian names of the hromada and its raion in its oblast (a typo or a cut-short official name — "Гостомелська", "Новояворівськ" — matches its one close neighbour; two of one name in one raion are told apart by whether the hromada's centre is a city, as the official list says "м. Покров та Покровська …"; the Sumy raion's two Миколаївська are not, and keep the raion rule). In the map's kilometres, simplified as one coverage to 0.25 km (neighbours keep one shared line), 0.1 km precision; about 30 KB per oblast gzipped. A page fetches an oblast's file only once an official alert there names a hromada (static/hromadas.js, HROMADA.need); until it is here, and for a hromada with no outline, the alert colours its raion as before. The server reads them too (areas.py: hromada_of, in_hromada): alert_fill gives a hromada with an outline as hr:<oblast>:<id> instead of its raion, so the held rule (§7.4) and the channel's picture (§9c) follow the hromada as the pages do — the picture filling every ring of it even-odd as the pages do (1.58.24: a hromada in two pieces, Borshchahivska or Brovarska, was tinted on the first alone). A place within 1.5 km of a hromada's outline belongs to it (BORDER_KM, the Light page's raion rule).

An id not in the table is kept, never dropped, placed as well as its name allows, and logged once ("rebuild ua_regions.json"). Oblast uids follow alerts.in.ua: 31 Kyiv city, 14 Kyiv oblast, 10 Zhytomyr, 25 Chernihiv, 20 Sumy, 19 Poltava, 24 Cherkasy, 4 Vinnytsia.

6.5 Reconciliation#

State.apply_snapshot(source, alerts, authoritative_levels) diffs a source's full list against the active set and emits start, update, threat and end events (stored in events, published on SSE). A source only closes its own alerts or alerts at levels it is authoritative for — so the mirror (authoritative for oblast) can never close a raion alert, and the raion source (authoritative for every level) takes back what the mirror painted when it returns. snapshot() merges duplicates and computes each oblast's status: A (an oblast-wide alert), P (only raions / hromadas), N.

The history of the alerts (1.58.4): State.alert_rows(start, end) — one row per alert, in the alerts table's shape, rebuilt from the start / end events (_alert_intervals, the replay's own reading: a restart closes what was not started again within five minutes of it; an alert with no end written and not on now is left out, never counted to now; one over within a minute is a source's flicker, not an alert — an official channel read from 15 to 24 September 2026 flipped Kyiv's alert 1,290 times, ~17 s each). The stats (/api/stats), the dashboard's analytics (Kyiv's minutes, the starts, the oblasts' hours), the lead times, the nights' summaries, the AI's records (bulletins, night reports) and the digests read it. Until 1.58.4 they read the alerts table, whose one row per region kept the region's first start: Kyiv city was "under alert" 1,440 minutes a day from mid-September and "the longest alert" ran from 24 September to 12 October (20,131 minutes).

6.6 What decides a colour, page by page#

  • Tactical: each raion shape is coloured by alerts with its raion_key (fallback: name stems); an oblast-wide alert colours all its raions. 1.58.16: a hromada's alert colours the hromada (#hromg, over the raions, under their names; alertMap), not its raion — left out where the raion is already painted as strongly — once its oblast's outlines are here; until then, or with no outline, its raion. The band counts and names hromadas as hromadas ("Kyiv oblast · 1 hromada red", hrom_red_*), no longer as raions. The banner uses the watched oblast pair (city ⇄ oblast) plus the home's own raion (found by point-in-shape on the map's outlines). The calm line never says "no alert in the oblast" while any raion of it is under one.
  • Light: see §10.2.

7. The Telegram feed and the map marks#

7.1 The channels#

1.58.36 — readers are not shown them. The owner: "the sources, we are not going to show them." Until then a mark, a post and an outcome named their channel (channel, oc, ch), every id began with it (kpszsu/123#0), the state listed the channels read and their health, the pages mapped the names to labels and linked to the post. Now:

  • what readers get (/api/state, /api/markers, /api/feed, /api/impacts, /api/replay, /api/trail, the live line's syncs and events) passes through State.public(): a channel name becomes a token — c + 8 hex characters of an HMAC keyed with kv.pub_salt, made once for this deployment, so that a token is the same after a restart and cannot be matched against a list of likely names (pub_ch); an id keeps its tail (c1a2b3c4d/123#0); the state's sources fold every tg:/tga: entry into one, channels: {ok, n, n_ok}; config.channels is dropped;
  • a flag comes back with the token: real_id puts the channel back (the map token → channel is rebuilt from the feed table after a restart) and the flag is stored with the real name, as before;
  • the real objects stay for the service itself (markers_now, state_now, feed_now — the bridges, the proximity push, the private channel) and for the dashboard (/api/health, flags, the corpus); the public copies are cached beside them (markers:pub, state:pub, feed:N:pub, with their own h);
  • the pages show the time of a report where they showed its channel; the "view the post" link, the map of display names, the channel in a shared text, a toast and the strip, and the channel named in the legend and the Stats tab are gone; the Air Force rows of the Stats tab keep their figures and lose their link.

The defaults are in code (AUTHORITATIVE_CHANNELS — deployed configs list 33 channels, and a config whitelist would have changed nothing on any machine; telegram_channels in a config is still ignored). Since 1.31 the dashboard manages the list on top of them (State.channel_list, stored in kv.channels, so it survives restarts and deploys):

  • each channel is Map (its posts place marks and can raise banners and alerts), Trial (its posts are shown in the feed with their place tags only and a "trial" chip — no threat tag, no mark, no banner, no toast, no push, not in any count or summary) or Off (not read, and its posts leave the feed);
  • read via the web preview (t.me/s/…) or the Telegram API (when the TG_* secrets are set);
  • added channels start on Trial — a channel nobody has vetted must not be able to put a drone over somebody's house; the admin promotes it after judging what it posts and the share of it the app reads;
  • the defaults can be switched off, never deleted; a deleted channel stays off for 6 h so its recent posts stop counting at once; at most 40 channels.

How: Store.feed_since (every computation — marks, the missile level, statistics, summaries) keeps only Map channels; Store.feed (what the pages show) drops Off ones and strips a Trial one's threat tags; the feed event of a Trial post carries tags: ["trial"] only. Both readers take the list afresh each round (State.channels_read). A web channel is checked for readable posts when it is added. The defaults:

rocket_danger_lviv, tlknewsua 1.58.43: added by the owner from the dashboard (on trial until he puts them on the map; their home oblast, Lviv 27 / Kharkiv 22, set there — not in code). @rocket_danger_lviv, "РАКЕТНА неБЕЗПЕКА": missiles over the west, "🚀 Теребовля / 🚀 Збараж", "Львів 3 хв" _parse_live(·, "27"); its 🚀 is a missile (geo.ROCKET_IS_MISSILE, the owner's one exception to 1.58.42): an unstated target of a 🚀 post is unspecified_missiles with msl_weak — a missile while one is reported (1.58.31), else a probable drone; the towns of Ternopil oblast it names are in the gazetteer (1.58.43; a name villages elsewhere share is "X (Тернопільщина)"); "Франик" is Ivano-Frankivsk
@tlknewsua, "TLk News", Kharkiv, in Russian: "КАБ на Приколотное/Бурлук", "БПЛА … курсом на Липцы", "Упал", "Не фиксируется" _parse_live(·, "22"), RU_CHANNELS ("Упал" = впав, after a KAB at a place an impact — 1.44; after one only heading somewhere, nothing); "РСЗО" — rocket artillery — is not the sky (ARTY_RX)
vseok450 "4.5.0", live tracker of Kyiv and its oblast, 12K readers (1.57.3, the owner's choice) — on trial by default (CHANNEL_MODE_DEFAULT): its posts in the feed, flagged, nothing on the map, until the owner puts it on the map from the dashboard parse_kyiv_airdef; its posts are short lists of places and counts ("Яготин / Богуслав / 3 Чернігівська", "1-й до Чабанів", "Обухів/Українка 2 з півдня"), no weapon named (read as "probably a drone"), "Відбій" (never ends an alert), "Цього нема." (the previous one is gone). On 11 Oct the rules read the places of a bare list and "до X" but not yet the counts, "з півдня" after a pair of places, or a list under a sentence — to be taught from its posts on trial. 1.58.43, parse_vseok450: "1 мінус / 1 до Узина / 1 в Чорнобильську" was read whole — one target shot down at Uzyn. A list with one of its own shapes is read line by line: a place line as the Kyiv trackers'; a count in an oblast or in the Chornobyl zone ("3 Чернігівська", "В Чернігівській 1+1", "Чорнобильська 2", _VS_AREA_RX) names no place — no mark, as before; an outcome with no place in a list is no mark (which target, the post does not say); "Ці в Житомирську" — those already reported — none. Any other post is read as before

A channel's mode until the dashboard sets one (1.57.3): map, or CHANNEL_MODE_DEFAULT — a new tracker starts on trial; the dashboard's choice, once made, is kept (kv.channels).

The oblasts as compartments (1.43). Every channel belongs to the compartment of the oblast it speaks for (its home): an oblast uid, 31 (Kyiv city and Kyiv oblast) or ua (the country-wide channels) — CHANNEL_HOME_DEFAULT for the defaults, chosen when a channel is added, changed from the dashboard. The dashboard shows the channels oblast by oblast and switches a whole oblast on or off (State.region_set, kv regions, which stores only what differs from the default). An oblast switched off leaves the map — SCOPE_UIDS, read by in_scope() — and its channels are not read (channel_mode() answers off for them); 31 cannot be switched off. By default the map covers Kyiv, the six oblasts around it, and the four farther out with a tracker of their own: Odesa, Kharkiv, Dnipro, (1.44) Mykolaiv and (1.45) Kherson, Kirovohrad, Khmelnytskyi, Ternopil, Rivne, Lviv, Volyn (DEFAULT_SCOPE). 1.44: every oblast on the map except Kyiv's has its own tracker — Chernihiv, Cherkasy, Odesa, Kharkiv, Dnipro (1.43), Sumy, Mykolaiv, Poltava, Vinnytsia, Zhytomyr, and (1.45) the seven above. The pages follow: state.scope decides which oblasts are painted raion by raion (scopeRegs()), the place lists ask /api/places?oblast=scope, and the Near list keeps to NEAR_MAX = 350 km of the reader's place (a ballistic mark is "the whole region" only within REGION_R = 150 km). A channel's home is also how its posts are read (geo.CHANNEL_HOME, §7.3b), and the posts of a regional channel carry home in the feed: the Tactical page raises a regional channel's ballistic or MiG-31K report only for a reader whose oblast — picked, or of a watched place — is that one.

1.56.6: a missile post is for the oblasts it names. The Air Force's "🚀Швидкісна ціль на заході Донеччини, курс на північ" raised "ballistic — shelter now" in Vyshenky and turned the Voronkiv target into "drone or missile" (the owner: "the banner alarms the user for nothing"). missile_obls(post_id, channel, text, tags) gives, for a post tagged ballistic_missiles or cruise_missiles, the oblasts it names: geo.oblasts_named() (the oblasts written out) and the oblast_uid of every mark geo.parse_for_channel() reads in it (a town's oblast), 31 folded into 14, ua dropped; cached by post id and text (an edited post is read again). Store.feed adds them to the post as obl. The warning strip (threatStrip) skips a post with an obl none of the reader's oblasts (myOblasts(), Kyiv city counting as its oblast) is in — a MiG-31K take-off excepted, its missiles reach everywhere; a post with no obl warns everyone, as before. State._missile_active() skips a post whose obl misses the oblasts on the map (SCOPE_UIDS), Kyiv and the favourites: the level sets every reader's cadence, so it follows the map, not one reader. The official alerts are never filtered this way — only the official data sets an alert.

A channel whose preview shows no messages is marked failed with "web preview disabled by the channel", so "no drones reported" and "cannot read this channel" never look the same.

7.2 From page to stored post#

Two readers, one path. Telegram.poll() parses a channel's preview HTML; TelegramAPI re-reads a channel's last 20 posts every 15 s (8 s in a missile threat), with receive_updates=False: a personal account's update stream (every chat it is in) took the 256 MB server down within minutes on 25 Sep 2026. ingest() runs in a worker thread (asyncio.to_thread), off the client's event loop. Both hand (post_id, time, text) to Telegram.ingest(), so a post read through the API is stored, tagged, parsed and shown exactly like one read from a preview. The preview reader skips a channel the API is reading (api_channels).

Telegram.ingest() skips posts already stored, runs tag_feed_text() and is_relevant() (news, fundraising, culture → dropped), stores the post with the offline English (§8) and publishes a feed event. Tagging order matters: forecasts first (FORECAST_RX — "оцінка загроз", "може відбутись у будь-який момент", and since 1.41.2 what the enemy "plans": "ворог планує запустити …" — never raise anything), then the news guard (geo.looks_like_news: strong news words, reported speech and consequences, length, summaries, and since 1.41.2 looks_like_explainer — a live tracker talking about the sky: "до прикладу", "розповім про балістику", "по цій темі", "я описую ціль", "якщо ціль знищена …", "ворог планує"), then threat tags. parse_for_channel applies looks_like_explainer to every channel's parser, so none of it is ever a mark. 1.58.60: a warning for hours or days ahead — "дали попередження на 24 години щодо застосування ворогом балістичного озброєння по м. Київ та Київщині" (@kyiv_airdef, 27 Oct), "протягом доби існує загроза …", "найближчу добу можлива …", "у найближчі дні ймовірна …" — was two ballistic marks (Kyiv, its oblast) and the ballistic strip. geo.drop_span_warnings takes out every sentence (geo._sentences: "м. Київ", "н.п.", "12.30" end none) that names a span of three hours or more, a day or days (SPAN_WARN_RX) with a warning word (SPAN_WORD_RX) and nothing in the sky (SPAN_LIVE_RX: a launch, a course, a flight, an outcome, "негайно"); parse_for_channel, parse_post and tag_feed_text read what is left. A post that is only that is tagged alert + forecast — kept in a tracker's feed as a warning, raising nothing; "Балістика на Київ! Попередження на 24 години продовжено" still warns of its missile. The city administration's "threat" line (parse_kmda) ignores a day's notice too; an alert start is the official data's and is unchanged. The AI reader's rule 12 says the same (PROMPT_VERSION 6), and the offline glossary reads it ("A warning was issued for 24 hours regarding the enemy's use of ballistic weapons on Kyiv and Kyiv region"). 1.42.1: a retraction comes before all of it (is_retraction()): a glitch of the system ("лаг системи", "збій системи", "хибне спрацювання") on its own, or "not confirmed" / a false alert about something missile-shaped (a speed, a launch, a rocket, a MiG) — "Хибна ціль" is not one, that is a decoy, which flies. It is tagged retract and nothing else, kept (is_relevant), and it ends what the channels said before it: a ballistic or supersonic mark of the 20 minutes before (_drop_retracted(), also in the replay), the missile mode (_missile_active() stops at it), and the warning strip's channel-raised ballistic / MiG-31K warnings. Never an official one — only the official data ends an alert. "… не підтвердився" about anything else is an explainer: no mark. banderol_missiles removes cruise_missiles and unspecified_missiles.

1.58.31: a missile only while one was reported. The owner, 15 Oct 2026, 08:58 — "Ракети ↓ знижується" over Troieshchyna from @kievinfo_kyiv's "останній троя, тец-6🚀 знижує висоту", under the state's yellow (drone) level: "even propeller drones, jet drones or super high-speed UAVs are still DRONES, not missiles … if the parser hasn't found any messages in the channels about missile launches, ballistics, or MiG-31K takeoffs, the system shouldn't have the right to put up a «Missile» marker at all … any disputed or high-speed target should default to a drone"; and "«останній» in the masculine is always a drone (a Shahed); «остання» in the feminine is a missile".

  • Words. geo.missile_said(text, channel): "named" — the post names a missile (MSL_NAMED_RX: ракета, КР, крилата, балістика, Калібр, Х-101 / 555 / 59 / 69 / 22 / 32 / 47, Іскандер, Кинджал, надзвукова, a MiG-31K, a Tu-95 / Tu-160 / Tu-22M3), or it is the Air Force's "швидкісна ціль" (MSL_AF_CHANNELS = kpszsu: its jet drones are "🏍 Реактивний БпЛА"); "weak" — it only suggests one (MSL_WEAK_RX: "швидкісна ціль" from anyone else, the feminine "остання"; 🚀 until 1.58.42 — no emoji suggests anything now); a Banderol's "реактивна ракета" names no missile (a jet drone). TYPE_RX: "останній / останнього / останньому" is a drone (not "останній раз / день / вибух / відбій …"); "остання" is a weak missile (not "остання група / хвиля / інформація …"); "КР" is a cruise missile (not "Кр.Ріг"), counted ("2 КР"). parse_for_channel flags a missile mark of a "weak" post msl_weak.
  • The rule (State._msl_context, State._msl_gate, in _marks_from_posts, the live map and the replay alike): a msl_weak mark is a missile while a missile was reported — a "named" post of any channel, any oblast, within MSL_CTX_POST_MIN (90) minutes before it or MSL_CTX_AFTER_MIN (3) after; a carrier airborne or launching (air_reports "up" / "launch": MiG-31K, strategic bombers, Tu-22M3, Kalibr carriers) within MSL_CTX_AIR_MIN (180); or, live, an official alert's missile threat (MSL_OFFICIAL). A retraction ("лаг системи") drops the reports before it. Otherwise it is unknown with likely: "drones" — "probably a drone" on every page — and its evidence says why (confidence low). The evidence's translation is cached by post, mark and type (_ev_cache).
  • The feed tags such a post msl_weak, not a missile type: no missile chip, no missile mode (_missile_active), no ballistic strip from it alone — the Air Force's own post raises those.
  • The official level's colour is never touched by a post (§6.2); with this rule a "missile" over a yellow, drone-threat level appears only when a missile was reported.

7.3 From post to mark (geo.py)#

parse_for_channel(channel, text) picks the channel's parser; each returns candidates with an evidence block for every value:

  • The words it was read from — segment (lowercased by the parser). 1.58.39, the owner: "when the post tied to a drone names several places, it must absolutely be trimmed to where the drone is — the drone we placed at Hnidyn gets ONLY the Hnidyn part, not the whole post": geo.post_part(text, segment) finds them in the post as written and gives part — the post's own lines, with the heading they come under ("Хмельниччина:", found up the lines' block, never across a blank line from another block; a heading alone is no part) — and lines, their numbers (None for words inside a one-line post). A mark read from the whole post (or nearly) gets none. The server adds both to the evidence (State._marks_from_posts, cached with its translation in _ev_cache); the pages take the same lines of the post's translation when it kept the post's lines, else the glossary's translation of the segment (postPart, i18n.js).

  • Type — TYPE_RX: drones (Shahed / Geran / "мопед"), jet drones, cruise, ballistic, supersonic (Kh-22/32, before cruise), banderol_missiles (Banderol / S-8000 / "реактивні ракети з БпЛА"), KAB, tactical and strategic aviation, MiG-31K. No type word → unknown (drawn as an upright ⚠, never guessed). 1.58.44: "БР" — the Air Force's and the trackers' балістичні ракети (BR_RX, a word of its own: not "бр." of a brigade before a name) is a ballistic missile, in TYPE_RX, MSL_NAMED_RX, the tagger and COUNT_RX ("2 БР"): "3х БР на Київ" was a probable drone, "Пуск БР з Брянщини" nothing. "Без подальшої фіксації" (and "без дальнейшей фиксации") is lost. An outcome with no place that names a missile (_outcome_here, type_stated; from any channel, a short post) goes to that channel's last report of that weapon within 10 minutes (last_of_kind, an approach included) — never to a drone it reported since; with none, no mark. "Без подальшої фіксації 3х БР." ends the three ballistic missiles of "3х БР на Київ" as lost. A post that only says how targets ended (geo.ended_only) raises no missile level; the official alert still decides when it is over. 1.58.42: words only. The owner: "stop using the emojis to determine the nature of the threat, because it is very common for the rocket / cruise missile emoji to be used for jet drones" (picked: no emoji at all). TYPE_RX holds no emoji any more (🚀 was unspecified_missiles, 💣 a KAB, 🛵 🛸 🏍 🚁 🅿 a drone), nor MSL_WEAK_RX (🚀); the arrows channel (parse_arrows) reads its lines' words (_words_type), not its icons (ARROW_TYPE, gone: ✈ 🛵 🏍 a drone, 🚀 cruise, 💣 KAB, 🛩 aviation, 🎯 ballistic), else unknown with likely: "drones"; the bare-name channels' ✈️ is no drone (_PLANE_RX, gone). WEAPON_SIGN_RX (🚀 💣 🛸 🛵 🏍 🚁 🅿) keeps what the signs did besides: a post that carries one still reports a target (_parse_live's untyped-target test, its height-line and empty-line tests, the Nikopol channel's own posts), of no stated type — the same posts make marks as before. The AI reader too (prompt 5, check 6): a weapon is quoted in words (validate), EMOJI_KIND is gone. A tracker's sign-only report is now a bare name like any other: it continues its own channel's drone track (1.58.29) but not another channel's. 1.58.43, the owner's one exception: on @rocket_danger_lviv (ROCKET_IS_MISSILE) 🚀 is a missile — msl_weak, so one only while a missile is reported.

  • Position — the gazetteer with declension-aware stems (hyphen lookbehind: Коцюбинське ≠ Михайло-Коцюбинське); aliases like "Велика Димерка" ← "димерк"; a stated part of an oblast is not its centre. 1.56.3: the core gazetteer's coordinates (geo.PLACES, written by hand) checked against OpenStreetMap's settlements (static/streets/): 82 of 379 matched names were more than 1.5 km off and are where OSM puts them now — Гнідин was 5.8 km east of the village (the owner: "Hnidyn is never reported there"), Березань 11 km, Ольшаниця 12, Мигалки 14, Котельва 19; Красилівка and Ясногородка, several of each, are the one near Brovary and the one near Makariv.

  • Heading — "курсом на", compass words, "→", "A - B" routes (bearing()); a heading is marked stated only when the post stated it. A compass word after "з / зі / із / від" is where it comes from ("з півночі" = flying south), after the destination and (1.58.41) before "курсом" too (_FROM_COMPASS). 1.58.41: "із" was not in it — "курсом на Київ із заходу" was a course west; and before "курсом" the compass word was read as the course itself — "з півночі курсом на Київ" was a course north, drawn on the wrong side of Kyiv, pointing away from it. A compass word with no "from" before it ("на південь курсом на Обухів") is still the course.

  • Where it is vs where it is going (_place_role): a place after "на", "в район", "до", "в напрямку", "в бік" is the destination; after "від", "з", "повз", "над", "біля", "в районі" it is where the target is. After "на", a place in the locative ("на Позняках", "на Троєщині") is where it is. Only a destination named ("на Васильків з північного заходу", "курсом на Київ"): the mark is drawn at the destination as an approach (approach: true, place: "→ X", low confidence) — also when the post's only other place is its own oblast heading. On live channels, "Від Глевахи на Васильків" is at Hlevakha heading for Vasylkiv; "йдуть на Васильків" alone (dest_only) stays at the channel's previous report (≤ 8 min) turned toward the destination — 1.58: only a report of the same weapon (same_weapon: the same type, or a drone and a weapon not named) —, or else is an approach. In tracking, a later approach about a target already on the map gives it its destination and course instead of moving it; an approach point is never a step of a track. 1.58.6: a part of the oblast the post placed it in ("на сході Київщини, в напрямку столиці") is where it is — the oblast centre shifted ~70 km to that side, with its quad — and the destination is its course (side_stated); the side is never read as the course.

  • Count, altitude (only when written: "знижується", "низько", metres "висота 2200", a bare "1600, Вороньків" in a short live post, kilometres "висота 4,4км", 1.58.9: "на 300 метрах" / "на 400 м" — not "на 300 м від …", a distance), phase (PHASE_LADDER: prep → launch → entering…, with a per-sentence hedge guard: "може відбутись" is preparation, not a launch).

  • The oblast, then its towns (1.42.1): "курсом на Київщину, Макарів / Наливайківку" — a town the post names right after the oblast it is in is the destination, not the oblast's centre.

  • The next target (1.41.3, _split_next) — an outcome followed by a sentence starting "наступний", "ще один", "другий" or "інший" ("1 знищено.\nНаступний Нивки") is two reports. The outcome keeps only its own words (on a live channel the previous position, needs_context; otherwise no mark); the place after the word is the next target's position, never an outcome. Read whole, it was a confirmed shoot-down at Nyvky, shown 24 h in the history layer.

  • Immediate types — IMMEDIATE_TYPES = {ballistic, supersonic, mig31k}, REGION_ALERT_KM = 150.

7.3b The regional trackers: their villages, their Russian (1.43)#

A regional tracker names villages and city districts post after post ("2 реакти від Куликівки на Лихачів", "Стрюково", "БпЛА на Лесное - Пятихатки"). data/regional_places.json holds every populated place of the oblasts that have a channel of their own (Chernihiv, Cherkasy, Odesa, Kharkiv, Dnipro — about 6,700; 1.44: and Sumy, Mykolaiv, Poltava, Vinnytsia, Zhytomyr — about 13,800; 1.45: and Kherson, Kirovohrad, Khmelnytskyi, Ternopil, Rivne, Lviv, Volyn — about 19,000 in all), built by scripts/build_places.py from the GeoNames dumps (https://www.geonames.org, CC BY 4.0): Ukrainian name, coordinates, kind (city section / raion or hromada centre / place), population, raion, and every Ukrainian and Russian name GeoNames has, plus the Russian-looking spellings of the Ukrainian name (і as и / е / о, 'я as е, no apostrophe — _ru_variants). Kharkiv city's districts are named by hand in the builder (CURATED_BY_NAME).

  • They are answered by PLACES[name] like any place (_Gazetteer) but never iterated: not in the regex index, not in the map's town labels, not in /api/places. They are matched only in their oblast's context — a post from a channel whose home is that oblast, or a sentence that names the oblast (_find_places(text, ctx_uid) → _find_regional). "Іванівка" alone, from Kyiv, is nothing.
  • Words that are also a village's name are never read as one (REGIONAL_STOP: the compass, the border, "new", 1.43.1 "буде / будет" and the alert levels "жовтий / червоний" …; _REG_PAIR_STOP: a verb is never half of a two-word name — "новий буде" is not Нова Буда; an oblast's adjective "Київська / Черкаський" is never the village "Київське", _ADJ_NOT_NEUTER_RX); REGIONAL_WORDS gives a bare word its oblast's meaning ("Водосховище" from Chernihiv = the Kyiv reservoir).
  • 1.43.1: a core town of the next oblast against hamlets of its name — "На Таращу" from the Cherkasy tracker is the town of Тараща (Kyiv oblast), not the hamlet in Cherkasy oblast: when every village of the channel's oblast with that name is a small place (kind P, < 2,000), the name becomes an ambiguity token with the core town as its default and the villages as the other candidates (so the channel's previous report or the post's other places can still pick the village).
  • A name several villages share becomes one ambiguity token (_ambig_token) with its candidates: the one near another place the same post names (≤ 60 km) wins at once; otherwise the server takes the one nearest the channel's report of the last 30 minutes (≤ 60 km, _marks_from_posts); with neither, the clear default — the only city district, the only raion/hromada centre, or a population three times the next — or no mark at all. A destination nobody can tell apart gets no course drawn to it.
  • Routes: "Любеч-Славутич", "Сновськ-Березна-Куликівка", "Лесное - Пятихатки" — two places joined by a dash are at the first, heading for the second (_route_arrows writes "→"), unless the joined word is one place ("Новгород-Сіверський") or it follows "в районі / між" ("в районі Котельва - Коломак" is an area, not a course).
  • One line, several reports: a count of weapons after the words of the previous report starts a new one ("2 реакти … 1 реакт …"), so does ", 1 …" ("1 Райгород, 1 Буки на Бузівка, 1 на Привітне").
  • Words: "реакт / реакти" (a jet drone, JET_RX), FPV, "Молния", "Ланцет" (small strike drones).
  • 1.44: an oblast named as the origin — "Реактивний БпЛА з Дніпропетровщини на Світлогірське" from the Poltava tracker: after "з / із / від", the oblast is where it came from, and the sentence's villages are read in the channel's own oblast (parse_post, home). "в сектор / секторі A - B" is an area, not a route (_AREA_PRE). "високо / низько / далі" are never villages (REGIONAL_STOP); "Не фіксується" at the start of a sentence is a lost target (not "загроз не фіксується"). A channel's signature line — "X | Підписатись", "💪 Телеграм канал …" — is cut before the post is tagged or parsed (translate._SIGN_LINE), never its first line.
  • 1.44.1: a list after "в бік / курс" ("в бік Велика Рибиця/Мала Рибиця/Осоївка", "на Димерку, Бровари") is a list of places it may be heading for: every name joined by /, ,, "або / чи / і" to a destination is a destination (_LIST_SEP_RX in _parse_live) — read as a bare name, the second one became where the target was. 1.56.4, the owner: "Jet UAV to Kyiv/Irpin/Bucha — the post is talking about Bucha, yet the drone is put in the middle of the city": a target heading for a list and nowhere stated is drawn, as an approach, at the one geo.approach_pick chooses — in Kyiv and its oblast not the city when its suburbs are named with it, and of the rest the farthest from the centre (a target heading for all of them reaches the outermost first); elsewhere the first named, as before — and the mark keeps the whole list (targets), which the pages say as written ("heading for Київ / Ірпінь / Буча", apprTo in i18n.js). Four-letter names keep their last vowel in the stem, so "на Бучу", "у Бучі" were no place at all: their cases are written out in ALT (Буча, Ічня, Кілія; Мена as "Мену" only — "мені" is "to me"). A segment that names no place and says the target left ("вилетів з області") or is coming from outside ("підлітає до нас / до області") is no position (_EXIT_RX), nor is "Дорозвідка". "В. Михайловка" / "М. …" are Велика / Мала (_REG_ABBR_RX); a Russian name declined like an adjective is matched in its cases ("Раздельная" → "Раздельной", "Раздельную"). "Буялык" is Великий Буялик (CURATED_BY_NAME).
  • 1.44.1: the weapon a channel named last (weapon_by_channel in _marks_from_posts, 15 min): "Пуски КАБів в нашому напрямку" names no place and the reports after it name no weapon; a "Впали" placed at the channel's previous report is then an impact of guided bombs, never a shoot-down. Guided bombs keep the standard clock (5 / 15 min), not the regional one.
  • 1.43.1: our own aircraft (geo.drop_friendly): a sentence about "наш / свій БпЛА, дрон, борт, літак …" ("Акустика нашего БпЛА над Харьковом") is dropped before parsing and tagging; a post with "то наш / це наші / это наши" as a sentence of its own is about nothing hostile and is not kept. "Без фиксации", "Больше не фиксируется" (більше не фіксується) are a lost target (STATUS_RX).
  • 1.45: a town it is heading for is never the oblast centre. "Чернігівщина: … / 3 реакти на Славутич з півночі" put three jet drones in the middle of Chernihiv oblast: a sentence that names where it is going and nowhere it is is drawn as an approach to that town (parse_post: in_obl or (target and cm and not from_obl)), whatever oblast heads the post. Only an oblast it comes from ("БпЛА з Чернігівщини курсом на Київ") is still the place — and such a place is a region on the map, not a point (§7.4). "вектор / вектором" is a course word ("2х вектор Велика Димерка/Бровари"). The lines of a multi-line post stay lines (_drop_clear_sentences keeps the separators), and a line that names no place, no oblast and no weapon ("‼️Увага!") is not a report of its own.
  • 1.45.1: a side of a town is not the town (_rel_settle): "на півночі від Миколаєва", "північніше Харкова", "на північному заході від Броварів" name a reference and a side. With a distance ("за 20 км на північ від …") the mark is moved that far that way (medium confidence). Without one, a side of an oblast's capital (_OBL_CAPITAL) is the oblast — place == "область", drawn on the region (§7.4) — and a side of any other town stays at the town with low confidence. Either way evidence.position carries rel (degrees) and ref, and the pages write "north of Mykolaiv" (oblWhere, rq / rq_of). The offline translation reads "на півночі від X" as "north of X".
