  • 1.49: Kyiv named and no place in it is the city, not a point (_city_settle): "над Києвом", "біля Києва", "на околицях правого берега Києва" were drawn at the city's centre, "town in gazetteer, high confidence, ±5 km" — a city 30 km across, and a resident read a drone over the centre that nobody had put there. Kyiv has its own outline, so it is marked like an oblast (place == "область", oblast_uid 31, low confidence; §7.4), with the bank when the post names one (evidence.position.bank: "right" / "left" → "right bank · Kyiv"). A district, a street, a landmark, a distance or a side keep their point; a course to Kyiv ("→ Київ") stays an approach. The pages say "somewhere in the city" for it (city_somewhere, city_noplace).
  • 1.45: raions. "Шепетівський район", "у Самбірському районі", "в напрямку Самбірського району" are read as the raion (_RAION_RX → RAION_KEYS): one pseudo-place per raion of a tracked oblast ("Самбірський р-н"), at the raion's centre town (the core gazetteer's first, never a power plant; _load_raions), with low confidence: a raion is an area. With a town it is heading for ("залітає у Гайсинський район … курсом на Теплик") it is an approach to that town, and no course is drawn from the raion's centre (_raion_settle). The pages write it "Sambirskyi raion" (pn). A raion alert notice ("Тривога — Вараський район", "Відбій — …") is a notice, not a mark (ALERT_MSG_RX).
  • 1.45: the oblast a tracker just named (near): a tracker follows its target into the next oblast — "Це він на Хмельниччині", then "Далі на Плужне" (@rivne_radar). The oblasts a channel named in its last 30 minutes (NEAR_CTX_MIN; near_by_channel in _marks_from_posts, Telegram._near_ctx when tagging) are passed to geo.parse_for_channel(channel, text, near); a post that places nothing in the channel's own oblast is read in them. And the far filter: a regional tracker's place more than 300 km from its oblast, in an oblast neither the post nor near names, is a misreading and dropped ("Велика Бобровиця", a village by Rivne, was the Бобровиця of Chernihiv oblast).
  • 1.45: names. An exact alias wins over a stem ("Іванівка" is the one Іванівка by default where one is the hromada centre); a word declined like an adjective matches its town ("Кропивницького"); GeoNames' Soviet-era names are never the displayed one (build_places.py, SOVIET); "маякну" and other verb forms are not villages (_VERB_TAILS); "велика / мала / стара / верхня …" alone are never a village (REGIONAL_STOP). Artillery and mortar fire is not in the sky (ARTY_RX), and a report of the injured ("47-річний … поранення") is news. 1.58.43: in Russian too — "РСЗО", "артиллерия", "миномёт" (@tlknewsua's "РСЗО на Проходы/Липцы" was a target at Lyptsi). And the plausibility test (_plausible) reads a name without what tells it apart ("Збараж (Тернопільщина)").

Posts in Russian. The Kharkiv and Odesa trackers write in Russian. geo.prepare(text, channel) turns a post from RU_CHANNELS, or one that looks_russian() (ы, э, ё, ъ, or a Russian-only word), into the Ukrainian words the rules read (ru_to_uk, _RU_MAP: the weapons, the outcomes, the prepositions, the courses, the compass, the oblasts — "Нико обл" is Mykolaiv oblast). Place names are not translated: the gazetteers hold their Russian names (Харьков, Днепр, Чернигов … in ALT; the villages' in the regional file). The tagger reads the same Ukrainian reading (tag_feed_text); the stored and shown text is never changed.

Kyiv oblast's villages for the rules (1.54.7). data/extra_places.json (1.52) was the AI reader's alone, and since 1.54 the AI reads no post: "Кулажинці" (@chyste_nebo, 10 Oct, 01:21, a drone at 400–500 m north-east of Brovary) drew nothing. _load_kyiv_villages() puts the ~825 villages the core gazetteer does not have into the regional index of oblast "14" (REGIONAL, _REG_IDX["14"], kind P, raion as the key's suffix when two share a name), with KYIV_VILLAGE_STOP for the ones that are everyday words ("Озеро", "Ферма", "Селище", "Кулі" …). They are read like any regional channel's villages — in a Kyiv tracker's post (_find_places(text, "31") reads oblast 14's villages with the city's: the one place of a name no town of the gazetteer has, or the candidates when several share it) and in a post about Kyiv oblast — never in a long sentence from elsewhere. A district of Kyiv and villages with its name — "лук'янівка" (01:12) was the village east of Brovary, not Lukyanivka in Kyiv — are candidates with the district as the default (_ambig_token); the server takes the one next to the channel's report before (≤ 30 min) and its report after (≤ 15 min, State._reports_ahead: the posts already read on this pass), the sum of the two distances, among the candidates the target could have reached (reach_km, §7.4) — recomputed every pass, so the mark moves to the village once "Кулажинці" is in. A height alone ("500", "400 м", "Увага 500", geo._height_only) is the height of the target the channel reported last, within 15 minutes, at that report's place and time and of its kind (height_only: carried by _marks_from_posts like an outcome line) — never a place of its own. 1.58.26, the owner: "there are often duplicates of the height like this" — so it is no step of its own either where a track is written (the card, the tooltip, the night's trail, the Light row): reports at one place in a row, 10 minutes at most, are one step (trackSteps in static/i18n.js) — "Фастів 01:56–01:58 (4,4 км)", "Таборів 01:50–01:51 (1,7 км → 5,4 км)", where it read "Фастів 01:58 → Фастів 01:58 (4,4 км)" — its heights in the order said, one said twice in a row once; the count is still of posts. The height trend (altTrend) drops a height said again at once: "700", "700" is no trend. 1.58.28, the owner: "there must be an error here" — "300", then "6700" ten seconds later: a height the target could not have reached since the last one stated for it (alt_reachable: a climb of 50 m/s, a dive of 150 m/s, 300 m of slack) is another target's, and which one the post does not say — it is not drawn on it (alt_by_channel, per stream; a new place starts again).

A target that came in or went out is at the oblast's edge (1.58.28). "Лукʼянівка зайшов звичайний" (@chyste_nebo, 03:19) was drawn on the Kyiv district, 3 km from the centre: a post saying the target came in or went out ("зайшов", "вийшов", "залетів" — geo.CROSS_RX, not when it names the city) crossed the edge of the tracker's oblast there, so a district of Kyiv is no candidate (geo._cross_settle); between the villages left, the channel's reports before and after decide as above, and then — new — the places its targets were said a moment before to be heading for (dest_by_channel, DEST_CTX_MIN 5 min, within 60 km): "На Яготин" made it the Lukyanivka of Brovary raion, 36 km from Yahotyn. Nothing to go on: it is not drawn. "Звичайний" alone is a Shahed (the trackers' word, against "реактивний"). A destination farther than 150 km (DEST_LINK_KM) from the channel's last report is not that report's target going there: "Реактивний на Годунівку" (Hodunivka, east of Yahotyn) had drawn a jet drone at Musiiky, near Ivankiv, 160 km away.

A weapon not stated, in the app (1.54.8). The owner: "'unknown' is badly worded — it is most likely a drone (a jet Shahed); the only exception is when we know ballistic or cruise missiles are also coming". A mark of type unknown is named by what else is in the air (mslCtx(m): a missile on the map — MSL_TYPES — or a MiG-31K / ballistic / strategic-aviation / cruise warning in the strip, TS_TOP; local since 1.56.6: a missile mark within MSL_NEAR_KM = 150 km of the target — MSLP, the missiles' projected points, set by mslPaint() where the marks are painted — or a strip warning, which is the reader's oblast's, with the target within 300 km of the reader's place; a missile in Donetsk oblast no longer turns the drones around Kyiv into "drone or missile"): "probably a drone" on the map's label, the card's title ("Probably a drone — type not stated", th_unk_dr), its note (unk_note_dr: no missile reported right now, so most likely a strike drone, a Shahed or a jet one), the Near list (k_unk_dr) and the counts (lb_unk_dr); "drone or missile" (unk_mix, th_unk_mix, unk_note_mix, k_unk_mix, lb_unk_mix) while missiles are reported. The ⚠ sign stays: the post did not say what it is. The Light page names it the same way (lMsl). 1.54.9, drawn as a drone (the owner: "instead of the triangles, Shahed icons by default — outside a ballistic or cruise missile context these threats are to be treated as drones"): asDrone(m) — an unstated mark with no missile context (mslCtx(m); until 1.56.6 one switch for the whole map, MSLX) — takes the drone's silhouette (glyphKey, silSvg, the oblast badges), colour (KIND_COL) and class, turns to a stated course (orientOf), is counted with the drones, and its label is "probably a drone" without the ⚠; the hover card and every other reader of THREAT.unknown (a getter) say "🛸 Probably a drone". No ETA is worked out for it (speedBand has none for an unstated type): a jet drone flies 2.4 times as fast, and a countdown on a guess could be late. While missiles are reported near it it is the red ⚠ "drone or missile" again. Light: lDr(m), with lMsl(m): a MiG-31K or strategic aviation on the map, or mslNear(m, marks) (i18n.js: a missile mark within 150 km).

7.3c @nikalert: Nikopol and the oblast's monitors (1.58.25)#

The owner: "we'll add t.me/nikalert for Dnipro — straight on the map" (Nikopol / Dnipro). "ТРИВОГА Нікополь DNiPRO" (~17 K readers) posts some 30 times an hour, two streams told apart by their first sign — read by geo.parse_nikalert (registered in CHANNEL_PARSER, and called before the generic artillery rule and the "next target" split, so a list is read whole):

  • 🪖 — Nikopol and its raion, across the Kakhovka reservoir from the occupied bank: FPV and strike drones, shelling. "Нкпл:" is Nikopol, "Мгнц" Marhanets, "Нов🍅павлівка" Novopavlivka (its own spelling). A threat is a list, newest line first — "🪖⛔️ Загроза ФПВ / ◼️ 17:43 с. Покровське / ◻️ 17:39 Покровська ТГ": its kind is the lines above the list, its place the newest line's place the gazetteer knows, within 5 minutes of the newest (NIK_LIST_MIN); a hromada line ("Покровська ТГ") is an area, never a place; a landmark of the city ("Яхтклуб", "3-я Дільниця") is passed over for the next line. One mark per list — the list is one threat, not a count. A post of its own without a list is read with its heading's town when it names no other: "Нкпл: 🟠 Увага! Атака ударних БпЛА" is at Nikopol, "Нкпл: Падіння дрону без детонації в с. Капулівка" a drone down at Kapulivka; "Детонація дрону" an explosion. Its own posts name a weapon when they report one: a bare direction ("Напрямок р-н 33 квадрати") places nothing.
  • ⭕️ — the same list once the threat is over ("🪖⭕️ загроза фпв / ◻️ 17:19 Дрон впав з детонацією / ◻️ 17:18 Автошкола / … / ◻️ 17:10 Нікополь"): where the drone went, ending "Дрон впав", "Сигнал втрачено", "Без загроз". A recap is never a target (read whole, it was a drone shot down at Nikopol, "×37" from its time, 17:37) — only how it ended (_nik_recap): its newest line as the outcome the map knows ("Дрон впав з детонацією" an explosion, "Дрон впав" down, "Сигнал втрачено" lost, "Без загроз" clear) at the list's newest place the gazetteer knows, so the target it ends is closed rather than left to fade; a shelling recap ("обстріл") places nothing.
  • Ⓜ️ — monitors relayed: Dnipro city ("Dнпр:"), Kryvyi Rih ("Кр.Ріг"), Zaporizhzhia ("Зпр:"), Kherson ("Хрсн"), the oblast's Shaheds (🛵) and Banderols. The label says whose report it is, not where the target is: it is dropped before reading ("Dнпр: Обухівка 🛵" is at Obukhivka, not at Dnipro). A threat it says was lifted ("Загрозу балістики зняли") is no threat. "КР «Бандероль»" is a Banderol — a jet drone, never a cruise missile.
  • No mark: shelling and artillery ("Обстріл", "загроза артобстрілу"); its news, history and service posts (🗞, 🗺, 📣, a "Forwarded from", its "Тривога 🇺🇦НІКОПОЛЬ | Підпис…" footer — not even stored: is_relevant); the official alerts it relays (🅰️, ✅ — an alert's colour comes from the official data alone).
  • Dnipropetrovsk oblast only: what it relays from Zaporizhzhia or Kherson oblast is not placed there (those oblasts are not covered yet); a hamlet named like a city ("Запоріжжя", Kryvyi Rih raion) read for the city is the city — and so not placed.
  • A name several places share is the one near the channel's town (_nik_near, 45 km): Nikopol for 🪖, Dnipro or Kryvyi Rih for their monitors — the Покровське of Nikopol raion, not the one of Synelnykove raion 160 km away. The gazetteer had merged that hromada centre into the other (geo._load_regional merged a village file's place into the core town of its name in the same oblast whatever the distance; 1.58.25: only within 25 km, _MERGE_KM — Нова Січ, its other name, now finds it).
  • Each stream is its own previous report (geo.context_stream: "/nik", "/m:<label>"): the server's "this channel's previous report" — where a "на Дніпро" target is drawn, which way a target turned — is the stream's, so a jet drone "на Дніпро" from the Dnipro monitor is not moved onto the FPV reported a minute before at Pokrovske.

7.4 Scope, chaining, staleness#

State.markers():

  1. parses the recent feed into candidates; merges identical ones from the same post (a count). A live-tracking channel's bare line ("На Таращу", "Вибух", "Не спостерігається") is placed at that channel's last reported position (last_by_channel, at most 8 min old). 1.38: that position keeps its own time — the mark carries pos_ts, and a carried position never restarts the clock (only a newly named place does); the channel's own down / lost / clear line ends it; and the official all-clear for Kyiv city ends every position held inside the city (State.kyiv_city_all_clears: an end of the city's air-raid alert that left no other source's alert running). A live tracker's bare place with no course gets the bearing from its previous report (last_by_channel, ≤ 8 min, ≥ 0.4 km) — 1.56.3: only when that post and this one each report one target (n_here, the ctx's 4th field: the previous post's Kiptiv → this post's Liubech drew a target heading NW, back to Belarus — two targets, not a course), and never one that points back across the border (outward, below);
  2. drops what is outside the scope: in_scope() = oblast uid in SCOPE_UIDS (1.43: the oblasts switched on — Kyiv, the six around it, Odesa, Kharkiv, Dnipro, 1.44 Mykolaiv, 1.45 seven more by default) or, with no oblast, within SCOPE_KM = 330 km of Kyiv. REGION_UIDS (Kyiv and the six around it) still decides the feed's "region" tag;
  3. _chain_and_prune() links consecutive reports of the same target (same family, time and distance windows, roughly ahead of the earlier heading) into a track with history — each entry keeps its own report's stated height (alt_m, alt_state), so the pages can show the heights the posts gave in sequence; marks stale after track_stale_minutes; drops an unknown after 5 min, others after max(stale × 3, 15) min — all counted from the position's time (pos_ts, else the post's); drops everything in an oblast with no active alert of any kind once 3 min old, and, in Kyiv city with no alert running, every mark reported before the last official all-clear there; missiles age on their own MISSILE_TTL_MIN; 1.43.1, the chaining windows (_chain): a report continues a track of its own channel within 30 min, of another channel within 15 min; two channels' unspecified threats (bare place names) are one target only at a drone's speed (7 km/min, not 15); a report naming only the oblast (place == "область", drawn at the oblast centre) never takes over a report that named a place, a destination ("на Зміїв") is never handed to one, and its point is never a step of a track's history. Before this, the Kyiv channels' bare names — one a minute — took over every Odesa, Chernihiv and Kharkiv report within 475 km and 30 min, and those oblasts' reports were never drawn. 1.54.4, a drone's reach (the owner: "not 100 % sure, but no wild assumption when the gap between two points is so huge" — #157, "Сквира" 00:29 then "Велика Димерка" 00:53, one track 150 km across Kyiv in 24 minutes): a drone's report continues a track only within min(3.5 km/min × dt + 15, 80) km — a Shahed's 210 km/h with a tailwind — a jet drone's within min(9 × dt + 15, 120); an unstated type is a drone when it is a bare place name of a channel that tracks drones (likely) or another channel's; other unstated types and missiles keep 15 × dt + 25. The tapped target's red dots (§7.8) leave out the line between two reports a drone could not have flown between, by the same measure — the dots stay (drawSelPath, cut). 1.54.7, the physical check (the owner: "if attaching a new report to a target implies 1,000 km/h, reject the match — take the technical spec of the Shahed, the jet Shahed and the Banderol"): reach_km(dt, jet) = VMAX_KMH (350 km/h for a Shahed — 185 by its specification; 650 for a jet drone — the Geran-3 550–600, the Banderol about 500, 650 at most) × the minutes between the two reports + POS_TOL_KM = 8 km (two named places, each a few km from where the target was), never past REACH_CAP_KM (80 / 120 km; a Banderol has no cap). The minutes count from when the earlier report's position was reported (pos_ts: "500" carries a place nine minutes old). A Banderol is a jet drone here too (it had a missile's 15 × dt + 25). The same reach decides between places that share a name (§7.3b) and leaves out a line on the card (cut: d>(jet?650:350)/60*dt+8). 1.43.1, the regional clock: a report from a channel with a regional home (geo.CHANNEL_HOME) is stale after REGIONAL_STALE_MIN = 10 min and dropped after REGIONAL_GONE_MIN = 25 (an unknown after 10) — the trackers post every five to twenty minutes; it carries slow: true, and the map fades it on the same clock (grey at 10, fainter at 15, "contact lost" at 20). The oblast's all-clear still ends it at once;
  4. outcomes (down, impact, lost) close the track they belong to;
  5. 1.58.7, State._hold: a drone, a weapon not stated or a Banderol (HOLD_TYPES) whose post said only where it is heading (approach, dest_only, a place "→ Київ" — drawn at that place) is marked held while that place has no official alert (areas.under_alert with areas.alert_fill of State.active: the city, Kyiv oblast's raions, the oblasts and raions around — the shapes the pages paint; a point in no known shape is never held). The owner, of a jet drone the Air Force put "на сході Київщини, в напрямку столиці", drawn on Kyiv with no alert there: "alarm ALWAYS is better … why put a drone icon here, it's dangerous" — picked: off the map until the alert. The Tactical and Light maps do not draw a held mark; the Near list keeps it, without a distance, "position not stated — on the map once that place is under alert" (mc_held); no "near you" push is sent for it (proximity_watch). A missile is never held: its alert follows within a minute or two, and a ballistic warning must not wait for it.
  6. 1.58.41, State._approach_edge: a mark heading for Kyiv (approach or dest_only, target "Київ" or place "→ Київ") with a side the post named (heading), not an outcome, gets off_km — areas.edge_km(lon, lat, heading + 180), how far the city's outline (kyiv-city in kyiv-map.json) is on the side it comes from (the last crossing, so the point is outside), + APPROACH_MARGIN_KM = 1.5: about 11.6 km from the north, 15.7 from the south, 21.2 from the west, 23.2 from the north-east. It is where the pages and the picture draw the glyph (§10), never its position: lon/lat stay the destination for the hold, the tracks, the lists and the log. A Kyiv district, another town, a mark with no heading or a point outside the city gets none; a value from an earlier pass is always removed.

1.45: a target reported for a whole oblast is a region, not a point. A mark with place == "область" ("БпЛА на Харківщині", "з Чернігівщини курсом на Київ") still carries the oblast centre as its coordinates, for the server's windows; nothing draws it there. The Tactical map puts it on the region with the outcomes of 1.38 (OBLEVT, paintOblEvents): a dashed outline (1.51: for targets in the air, cyan — never the alerts' amber or red — over a hatching that says "an area", blinking, and a chip with the tag, the weapon, the count, a stated height and the words "somewhere in the oblast"; still under prefers-reduced-motion. 1.52.1: the hatching only on the whole-region view. 1.53.1, the owner: "still unreadable — and not this cyan": a white dashed outline (dark in daylight) slowly fading in and out, no hatching, and a compact chip — the weapon, the count, a height, "AI" when only the AI read it — right under the oblast's own name; "somewhere in the oblast" is written under the chip from the regional view in (svg.veryfar hides it), and in words on a tap. 1.54, the owner: "this tiny square marking looks really bad": the oblast's own name lights up (#obllbl .oblbl.hot, the names carry data-o), a rounded badge under it — the weapon's silhouette in its colour, ×count, a height, its border the weapon's colour — whose pieces are measured once drawn (fitOblChips: the phone's own font never runs one into the next), and the oblast's border glows from the inside (.obglow: three wide strokes clipped to the oblast, so only their inner half shows, and a thin solid line; breathing slowly, still under prefers-reduced-motion): "somewhere in here", the middle of the oblast left clear for the targets placed in it. An oblast drawn without a name (Kyiv oblast, the city, Sevastopol, Crimea: OBL_UNNAMED) carries it in the badge ("KYIV OBL.", ob_kyiv); every outline is drawn before every badge; two badges never overlap (layoutOblChips, at each zoom: the one of an unnamed oblast steps down); the town names under a lit name or a badge give way; 1.54.5, the owner: "too big on a phone" — on a phone (< 1,000 px) a fifth smaller, OBS(): 19 px high instead of 24, its text 10.5 / 9.5 px), the posts one tap away (oblEventSheet). The Near list keeps it without a distance, as "Chernihiv oblast · somewhere in the oblast" (oblWhere, nearRows vague); it never raises the level. A push never says "within N km of you" for it (proximity_watch) — only a ballistic warning, which covers the region anyway, is still sent, naming the oblast; the Light page's radar does not draw it.

Fires, ground damage and the channels' own "чисто" are not drawn (HIDDEN_STATUS on the pages).

One target, one track (1.58.29). The owner, showing another map that drew one jet drone from Pyriatyn past Borshchiv: "it would be cool to define the trajectories properly" — and "despite the channels placing it, the app did not place the drone". @chyste_nebo had followed it from Pyriatyn (03:12) through Yahotyn, Berezan ("реактивний зі сходу") and Borshchiv, then only where it went ("На любарці", "На Сошників", "На Кийлів", "На Українку"); Heimdall had three pieces:

  • a bare place name continues the tracker's drone track (State._chain, kin): "Борщів" (no type: likely drones) after "Березань реактивний" of the same channel — the families had to be equal, so a bare name never continued a drone; never a "звичайний" and a "реактивний" (regular against jet: two targets the posts told apart);
  • what an earlier report of the track said of its kind stays said (type_said): the bare report becomes the track's jet drone, its card saying "as this track's report at Berezan said";
  • a turn the post explains is allowed: "зі сходу" — it came from where the earlier report is (came_from, ORIGIN_BEARING), even off that report's course;
  • "На Любарці" is where it goes: after "на" a word ending like a locative (-і) is the locative only when it is not the place's own name — Любарці, Кулажинці end so in the nominative (geo._place_role); "на Позняках" is still there;
  • no course between two targets: the course worked out from the stream's report before is kept only if one target could have flown between them (reach_km with a jet's speed) — "Від Славутича три", then "Борщів" 20 s later, 140 km south, had given Borshchiv a course from Slavutych;
  • reported again after "lost" is found again: "Не спостерігається" closes a target reported before it, never one reported after (the pages' linkStatus, the channel's link_outcomes: 0 instead of −2 minutes for lost). The track's line is the posts' places joined (the card's path, drawSelPath) — straight between them, never a curve nobody reported.

7.6 The carriers: take-offs, launches, stand-downs (1.33)#

parse_air_report(text) reads what a post reports about the aircraft and ships that carry the missiles — mig31k (MiG-31K, Kinzhal), strategic (Tu-95MS / Tu-160), tu22 (Tu-22M3, Kh-22/32), kalibr (the ships) — as (kind, phase) pairs, phase up (airborne: "зліт", "у повітрі", "відбувся виліт", "борт Ту-22м3 в районі …"), launch ("пуски КР «Калібр»", "перші пуски … повітряного базування Х-101", "Кинджал вектор …", "Пуски Х-22", the Air Force's "імовірно здійснено пуски" included — it reads them from the aircraft) or end ("Відбій загрози МіГ-31К", "Посадка МіГ-31К", "Чисто по Х-22"). Sentence by sentence, so that the sentence that says what MAY come ("Якщо будуть пуски…", "Загроза пусків Х-22", "Може відбутись пуск", "готових до вильоту") never counts; a post about moving aircraft between airfields, a tally written after the attack, and the Air Force's summaries are not reports. Pinned on 63 real posts read by hand (tests/fixtures/air_posts_2026.json).

Every post read (before the relevance filter) goes into air_reports (Store.air_save, with a fingerprint of the text). air_sorties() groups them: several channels reporting one take-off are one sortie; a sortie stays open AIR_OPEN minutes after its last report (MiG-31K 90, Tu-95/160 12 h, Tu-22M3 6 h); a take-off more than 10 min after a stand-down is a new sortie; the same words from the same channel within 45 min count once. Each sortie has its first take-off report, first launch report and stand-down, the minutes from take-off to launch and to stand-down. Statistics only: none of it places a mark or raises anything.

Two live bugs were found on the way and fixed in 1.33:

  • A reply was read as the message it answers. On t.me/s/ a reply carries the quoted message first, in a div of the same class. The reader took it: the Air Force's "📢 Відбій небезпеки по МіГ-31К", posted as a reply to its take-off message, was stored as "Зафіксовано зліт МіГ-31К" — a second take-off at the minute the danger was called off. tg_post_text() takes the post's own text (js-message_text, never js-message_reply_text) for every t.me/s/ reader.
  • A Tu-160 airbase was drawn near Kyiv. "Також у повітрі 4х борти Ту-160 з «Українки»" (the airbase in the Amur region) placed strategic aviation at Ukrainka, Obukhiv raion. The carriers never fly over Ukraine: geo.NEVER_PLACED (strategic aviation, MiG-31K) — their posts raise their banners and place no mark. And "Кинджал", the way the Air Force and war_monitor spell it, is now read as ballistic (it was an unspecified missile, without the ballistic banner).

7.5 The Air Force's summaries (1.32)#

Twice a day kpszsu sums up the attack on all of Ukraine: "У ніч на 28 вересня (з 18:00 27 вересня) противник атакував 165 ударними БпЛА … (79 із них — реактивні) … збито/подавлено 112 …". Until 1.31 these were read for two totals (drones, missiles) and, from 25 Sep, tagged news and dropped — so the Stats tab lost whole nights, and missiles were never counted at all: the Air Force names most of them without a number ("балістичними ракетами Іскандер-М") and gives the number only for those shot down.

parse_af_report(text, ts) returns, or None for anything that is not a summary ("продовжує атакувати" is an update):

  • period (night / day) and date (the morning of the night; the day's own date);
  • launched per type — drones (Shahed type and other strike drones), jet (the "із них — реактивні" part of drones, never added to it), banderol (a jet drone, never a missile), cruise, ballistic, aeroballistic (Kinzhal), antiship, guided, decoys, and missiles when the post gives a missiles total with a breakdown in brackets. A type is counted once, by its most specific name (AF_TYPES order);
  • used: the types named without a number — shown as "used", never counted as 0;
  • down per type and total (the "збито/подавлено" part, or the headline "ЗБИТО/ПОДАВЛЕНО N");
  • impacts and debris (the number of locations), areas (where launched from, "рф" dropped), directions (the "основний напрямок" oblasts), models (Іскандер-М, Калібр, Кинджал, Циркон, Х-101 …).

tag_feed_text tags such a post af_summary (kept in the feed; not a live threat: it raises no banner). Every summary from AF_SUMMARY_CHANNELS is stored in af_reports as it is read (Store.af_save, INSERT OR IGNORE on the post id); Store.af_reports() keeps one per (date, period) — the Air Force's own post over a re-post, then the fullest, then the latest. AFBackfill fills the gaps (§5.1). The Stats tab (§10.1), the dashboard's Analytics (§8c) and the nights' figures (§8b) all read this table. parse_af_summary (the two totals) remains as a wrapper for older callers. tests/test_af_reports.py pins it on 11 real summaries of 21–28 Sep 2026 (tests/fixtures/af_summaries_2026_09.json).

1.57.9: a flash report and a model's number. The Air Force at 23:48 on 12 October: "❗️О 22.10 ворог завдав удару по столиці попередньо 6-ма балістичними ракетами … Інформації щодо пусків з території противника не поступало … Крім того, противник атакував Харків 9-ма баражуючими боєприпасами S-8000 «Бандероль»". Tagged af_summary by the feed, it was drawn all the same as ballistic missiles over Kyiv, "launch completed", 98 minutes after the strike, and stored as 8,000 Banderols. Now:

  • _marks_from_posts drops the marks of a post parse_af_report reads, from AF_SUMMARY_CHANNELS, unless it says what flies now (AF_LIVE_RX: "курсом на", "рухається", "у напрямку", "загроза застосування", "швидкісна ціль");
  • geo.SUMMARY_RX reads a strike told after it happened ("ворог завдав удару", "О 22.10 ворог атакував …"), whoever relays it; _parse_live (the Kyiv trackers) returns nothing for a post looks_like_news — it fell through to the place names when parse_post said so by returning nothing;
  • a number glued to a model's letters ("S-8000", "Х-101") is the model, and a model may stand between a count and its weapon (_AF_TYPE_RX); "завдав удару" opens the attack as "атакував" does (_AF_ATTACK_RX);
  • a report dated by its own clock time and no night or day is period: "flash": listed, never added to a date's or a window's totals (AF_TOTAL_PERIODS) — the morning's summary counts the same missiles;
  • the stored reports are read again once (Store.af_reparse, AF_PARSE_V) at start;
  • "Інформації щодо пусків … не поступало" is no launch (geo._launch_denied); "столиця" is Kyiv named and no place in it — the city is marked, not a point at its centre, and a course to it is "→ Київ" (_city_settle) (tests/test_retro_strike_1579.py, tests/fixtures/af_flash_2026_10_12.json).

7.7 The AI reader (1.50; its facts in the target tags since 1.51; retired from the map in 1.54)#

1.54, the owner: "the AI placing things on the map — we forget it". Nothing in §7.7, §7.9 or §7.10's check runs any more: no post is handed to the AI (Telegram._to_ai is gone), no AI mark is drawn (State._ai_marks), no AI fact fills a tag (State._ai_facts, the ai_live switch), and the map's context for it (ai_context, ai_map) is gone. The reading itself — prompt, schema, validators, AIReader.read / check — is kept in ai_reader.py with its tests, and the dashboard still shows what it read until then. The AI now writes bulletins (§7.11) and the night's report.

A second reader of the channels' posts: a language model, through OpenRouter (app/ai_reader.py, thread ai-reader). It never places, moves or removes a mark, and never reaches the alerts, the feed, the banners or a push. 1.51: with the dashboard's switch on (§7.8), the facts it quoted that the rules did not read — origin, height, speed — fill the target tags, marked as the AI's. It reads the same posts as the rules, its reading and the rules' are stored side by side (ai_reads), and the dashboard's AI reader tab shows where they part. What it is for, first: finding what the rules misread or miss, so that the rules are fixed — they stay deterministic, free and instant. Whether it ever does more (a fallback for a post nothing else can read) is decided on what this record shows, in a later version.

What it reads. Telegram.ingest hands it (_to_ai, off the post's path: a queue, read on its own thread) every new post of a channel on the map — never one on trial or off — that the tagger saw a target or a place in (AIReader.WANT), and, from the posts the rules set aside as not about the sky, those that name a place and are 300 characters or shorter (a tracker's line the rules did not understand, "Ціль на Бровари"; a long one is news). Not the Air Force's summaries, not a retraction. The post as the reader sees it (_clean_post).

What is sent (OPENROUTER_URL, one request a post): the post's public text, the channel's name and its oblast, the channel's previous posts of the last 40 minutes (at most 3, Store.ai_previous, context only), and the candidate places — the gazetteer's places the post's words match (geo._find_places, the regional villages of the oblasts in play) and those oblasts, at most 30, each with an id (c1…, o<uid>). Never anything about a reader, a watched place, a device or an address; a run of 13–19 digits (a card number in a fundraising line) is replaced before anything is sent or stored (mask).

The prompt is a component, versioned (PROMPT_VERSION, stored with every reading; 3 since 1.51, a p after it when the answer was asked for by the prompt alone). 1.51: it is the second reader of a deterministic parser and is told so — its job is to cross-check the rules' reading against the post, the channel's previous posts and the targets on the map from every channel, to correct it, and above all to add the targets the rules missed ("a target missed is worse than a duplicate"), never inventing: every fact still quotes the post and every place is a candidate. Each post comes with (user_message): the channel and the oblast it speaks for and the oblasts its last posts named (channel_block — a regional tracker's bare name is read in those oblasts); the PARSER READING — the rules' marks for this very post, one line each (parser_lines); and ON THE MAP NOW — the tagged targets other posts placed in the candidates' oblasts or within 80 km of them, newest first, at most 10 (State.ai_context, never the post's own marks). Its answer can say which of those a target is (same_as, a tag it was shown — anything else is dropped) and a vertical state (vertical: descending, climbing, low, high, quoted).

  1. fixed rules from SAFETY.md (SYSTEM): never infer; every fact quotes the post; places only from the candidates, by id, never coordinates; "на X" is heading to X, not at X; a city named with no place in it is the city; the weapon words (Banderol a jet drone, never a cruise missile); the status words; previous posts are context, never a source of facts; an alert or an all-clear alone is no target; 1.50.2: the origin — a direction or a country or area ("з півночі", "з Білорусі", "з Брянщини", "з моря", "з Криму", "з ТОТ"), never a Ukrainian oblast or town, which is a place it comes from — and the speed; a height or a speed belongs to the target of its own line or paragraph, a height on a line of its own to the target just above it;
  2. worked examples (EXAMPLES, six, as earlier turns of the conversation);
  3. the post's context, assembled per post (user_message);
  4. a strict JSON schema (SCHEMA: targets with kind, count, status, place_id, relation, side, distance_km, origin, heading, altitude_m, speed_kmh, each fact with its *_quote; uncertain) — response_format: json_schema, strict, and provider.require_parameters so that no provider ignores it. Nothing in it is nullable (1.50.2): what a post does not state is "", 0 or "none" on the wire (wire, unwire; the examples are written with None and sent in that form). A nullable field is a "union type" to Anthropic's structured outputs, which take at most 16 in a schema, and 1.50.0's 15 came back as "HTTP 400: Provider returned error" on every post. A provider that still refuses the schema (400) is asked once more by the prompt and the examples alone, without the schema and the caching marks (plain); if that works it is kept (AIReader.way, format on the dashboard: schema / prompt), an answer wrapped in a code fence is still read (_json_of); refused both ways, it pauses 10 minutes. A refusal shows the provider's own words (error.metadata.raw, _why), not only OpenRouter's. The request carries no other model parameter (1.50.1): with require_parameters, one the model does not take — the temperature claude-haiku-5.5 does not — leaves no provider at all (OpenRouter's "HTTP 404: No endpoints found that can handle the requested parameters"). A model set in ai_reader_model must take response_format.

The fixed part comes first and the post last, so a provider can reuse the unchanged beginning (prompt caching; for Anthropic's models its end is marked cache_control).

Checked against the post (validate): a quote that is not in the post drops its fact; a place id that was not offered drops the place; a count needs a number in its words, a distance must be written, an altitude must be 30–20,000 m with a figure in its quote, a speed 30–5,000 km/h with a figure and "км" in its quote; an origin's quote must hold a word of its class (ORIGIN_WORDS: "білорус", "брянщ", "мор", "крим", "окупов"…, or a compass word for a direction) — "з Чернігівщини" is never an origin; a target with nothing left (no weapon, no place, no outcome) is dropped. The number of facts dropped is stored (dropped) — a model that keeps inventing shows here.

Compared with the rules (compare, on geo.parse_for_channel of the same text): agree; place (the places differ); kind (another weapon at the same place — a jet drone is the rules' drone or Banderol); status (shot down / impact); ai_only (only the model found a target — perhaps a miss of the rules); rules_only; none; error. A side of a town ("на півночі від Миколаєва") matches the rules' oblast for it (_rel_settle), and Kyiv named alone is the city to both (_city_settle).

Cost and limits. The month's spend is OpenRouter's own figure for each call (usage.cost), kept in kv ai_usd:YYYY-MM; at ai_reader_monthly_usd (10) it stops until the 1st; at most 400 posts an hour; a refused key, no credit, or a model or parameter no provider serves (401 / 402 / 403 / 404) pauses it 10 minutes, "too many requests" (429) one minute; a post that arrives meanwhile is not read (counted in skipped). With the default model (anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5, $0.10 / $0.50 per million tokens) a post costs ~$0.0003–0.0005: the $10 cap is ~25,000 posts a month.

The dashboard (GET /api/ai_reads, 🔒): the state (key, model, prompt version, paused, last error), the month's readings by verdict, the spend against the cap, the median answer time, and the readings — disagreements by default, all on request — each with the post, the rules' places and the model's targets with their quotes. /api/health carries the same state (ai_reader). Readings are kept 90 days.

The switches (1.51, POST /api/admin/ai 🔒, {reader: bool, live: bool}, on the AI reader tab): reader (kv ai_reader_on) stops every call at once — AIReader.enabled() reads it; live (kv ai_live) is the kill switch for its facts in the target tags — State.ai_live(); the marks are recomputed at the next ask (≤ 10 s). Both on unless switched off.

7.8 Target tags (1.51)#

1.56.5: not shown in the app, for now — the owner: "let's disable the target IDs for now, it doesn't work and it's very unreliable". TAGS_SHOWN (i18n.js, false) keeps every number out of the pages: the map's label and its compact tag, a whole oblast's chip, the tooltip, the card's tag line and its explanation (tg_what), the replay's label, and the night's path built from a tag (loadTrail, trailLine: no /api/trail request). The server still works them out — the patterns (§7.12), the night's record and the bulletins' record, a height or a speed an earlier report of the track gave (tag.alt, tag.spd, still shown) — and /api/markers still carries tag. true brings them all back.

Every target in the air carries tag in /api/markers: {id: "#07", n, p, night, o, os, oq, oc, ot, alt, spd} — Heimdall's own number for the night. 1.53.2, the owner: "remove the direction altogether for now — it gets it wrong almost all the time" (a drone from Belarus over Chernihiv oblast was "E-82": its track had taken "з брянської" from another post's report). tag_id is the number alone ("#07"); the letter and the origin below are still worked out and kept (p, o…, targets, the night's record, the CSV) but shown nowhere — not on the map, the card, the replay, nor in the AI's view of the map (ai_map, ai_context send no origin). It is not an official or military designation (SAFETY.md).

  • The number (TargetTags): counted per night, from 12:00 to 12:00 Kyiv time (the replay's nights), the night named by the date it starts on (tag_night, as the replay names its nights — tonight is in the charts at once). _chain carries every report of a track (reports, oldest first; an absorbed approach report too); a track's tag is found by any of them, so it does not change when its first report leaves the 45-minute window, and target_reports keeps it across a restart. A new track gets the night's next number. The replay looks tags up (TargetTags.find, tg in its tracks) and never makes one.
  • The letter (TAG_PREFIX): N — north, north-west, Belarus; E — north-east, east, south-east, Russia; S — south, south-west, the sea, Crimea; W — west; ? — not said, or "the occupied territories". From geo.stated_origin on the report's own words ("з півдня", "з території Білорусі", "з Брянщини", "з моря", "з Криму", "з ТОТ"; never a Ukrainian oblast or town) — the first any report of the track stated (origin_t) — and, when the rules found none and the switch is on, the AI reader's checked origin for that track (State._ai_facts, ai_reader.match: the AI target at one of the track's places, or the only target of a post the rules also read as one). os says whose (rules / ai), oq the words, oc the channel.
  • The bottom line: the height and the speed the track's latest report stated — the rules' (alt, geo.parse_speed) or, where they read none, the AI's for that report (alt.s / spd.s = ai); and vs, its vertical state (descending…), the rules' or the AI's. Height and descent are always shown when given (the owner's priority): at every zoom, and on the chip of a target reported for a whole oblast ("↓2200", oblAlt) — 1.56.2: for 4 minutes at most (ALT_MAX_MIN in i18n.js, altNow, altFresh; the owner: "never more than 4 minutes — a drone at 900 m last reported 10 minutes ago is down, has hit, has climbed again or has turned"): the map's height and arrow, an earlier report's height with its age, the oblast chip, the card's height chip, Light's mark and row, and altTrend (a run of heights whose last one is older) all go when the report that stated them is older; the card's source line then says "reported N min ago — too old to be its height now" (alt_old), and its track keeps every report's height with its time. 1.54.9: outlined, not a pill (1.54.6 drew a filled pill; the owner: "not a filled bullet — just make the characters more visible, with an outline"): 12 px, heavy, sky blue (#5cc8ff; red #ff4d6a while it descends) on a dark outline (paint-order: stroke); the card's height chip is coloured text in a coloured frame (.chip.alt, .chip.desc), a region-wide report's height light blue (.obalt). A mark with nothing stated shows nothing. Night mode dims it (#8fb8ec / #ff8c9c on black), the day mode deepens it on a white outline, the light version is white on black.
  • targets keeps each tag's letter, origin, source and words, first and last time and type (for the analytics: targets in /api/analytics, per night by letter, and how many letters the AI read); target_reports maps each report to its tag. Both kept 60 days.

On the map the tag heads the label ("S-07 Реактивний ×2", yellow, hidden when zoomed out to the whole country), and the bottom line under the glyph adds the speed ("3500 м · 400 км/год", italic when read by the AI). The card and the hover say where it came from, in the post's words and from which channel, with an "AI" chip when the AI read it, and what the tag is. A target drawn at the town it is heading for (an approach mark with a stated side) stands off the town on that side, 30 px on the screen, pointing in along its dashed course: nobody said where it is, and the town stays clear. 1.58.41: Kyiv is a city, not a point — 30 px still put "Реактивний БпЛА курсом на Київ з півночі" over Podil (the owner: "it says heading to Kyiv, from the north, and yet the icon is placed right inside the city"). A mark heading for Kyiv carries off_km (step 6 of §7.4): the glyph stands at least that far off, just outside the city's outline on the stated side (D = max(min(30·MSK, 12), off_km) in tick()); Light (paint()) and the private channel's picture (MapShot._target: max(min(60·SS, 12·k), off_km·k)) do the same. The dashed course still runs into the centre; lon/lat stay the destination.

1.52 — only when it is sure, and "probably". A target reported for a whole oblast (place == "область") gets no tag (the owner's rule: a number only when we are sure which target it is); it gets one when a post places it. Where no post says where a target came from, the oblast its first report was in (first_uid, carried by _chain) says it probably — TAG_REGION: Chernihiv oblast n (Belarus), Cherkasy oblast s (the south or the east) — in lower case (n-07), os = region, and the card says "probably from …, first reported in …; no post said so". A stated origin, the rules' or the AI's, always wins. The height on the bottom line is the latest the track stated (alt.cur false: an earlier report's, shown with its age "2900 м (3′)" — 1.56.2: never past 4 minutes, ALT_MAX_MIN). In daylight the tag, the height and the region outline take dark colours (body.m-day).

The path of a tapped target (1.52, drawSelPath, layer #selpath): the places the posts reported it at, oldest first, joined in red dots, each with its time — the path the posts put it on, not a flight path; cleared when the card closes. 1.52.1: the tag's whole path for the night — every point of a tagged track (the report's place, time and stated height; a destination or a whole oblast is no point) is kept with its tag (TargetTags.points, table target_points, 60 days), so it outlives the 45-minute window; a tap fetches it (GET /api/trail, loadTrail) and draws the reports older than the window fainter (.old) before the ones still on the map, a time on a dot only where it does not sit on the last one shown, and the card adds "N-07 tonight: 12 reports since 19:02: Ріпки 19:02 → … → Вишгород 20:22" (trailLine, #shtrail).

1.52.1: an answer may be MAX_OUT = 2,400 tokens long (1,200 cut one reading in ten off mid-answer — "not JSON"); an answer cut off at the length is recorded as "cut off at 2400 tokens".

7.9 The AI reader's targets on the map (1.52; gone in 1.54)#

1.54: no target the AI alone found is drawn any more (§7.7's note).

The owner's decision (9 Oct 2026): a target missed is worse than a duplicate. A target the AI reader found that the rules did not read goes on the map, while the switch live is on (§7.7), with the same chaining, scope, ageing, tags and pushes ("near you", for the phones that asked) as any other — State._ai_marks, from the readings of the window (Store.ai_recent), each AI target of a post that the rules have no mark for at its place or its destination, and none when both read one target within 30 km of each other (_ai_marks_of; read further apart — "біля Сомкової Долини", which the rules took for Dolyna in Ivano-Frankivsk oblast — the AI's stands too). It is placed only at a place the post names, a candidate the server offered (ai_reader.place_of: the gazetteer, or 1.52's data/extra_places.json, the villages of Kyiv oblast, which the rules never read): at it, near it, heading to it (an approach), or for its whole oblast; never at an official alert message (geo.ALERT_MSG_RX without MOVE_RX). Its id is <post>#ai<n>, it carries ai: true, the map dashes its halo and heads its label with "AI", and its card says the AI placed it from the post and to check the post.

What the first 54 real readings showed (dashboard, 9 Oct) and 1.52 changed:

  • the prompt (version 4): two places per target — where it is (place_id) and where it is going (toward_id; only the latter → it is the place, heading_to); the channels' emoji were weapons and quotes (EMOJI_KIND: 🛵 Shahed, 🏍 jet, 🛸 drone, 🚀 missile, 💣 guided bomb — 1.58.42: no more, prompt 5: words only); a tracker's bare place name is a target at it; "н.п. X" after an oblast is at X; "повз X" is near X; an official alert message is not a target; "спускається" is a descent; a missile of no stated kind;
  • the candidates: a looser match (fuzzy_places) among the oblasts in play (Kyiv oblast for the Kyiv channels) — a village the rules' matchers do not read and a slip of one letter ("Пепеяслав" → Переяслав), by the stem;
  • a post the server set aside is compared with nothing — the rules did not draw it.

The rules' own errors it showed, fixed in geo._fix_readings (applied once, by prepare with the channel): a channel's signature on the last line ("‼️ RND | Житомир") is dropped; "з Чорного/Азовського моря" followed by a course is "from the south", not a position in the sea; an oblast named by its adjective alone after a preposition ("З Житомирської", "через черкаську", "в київську") is the oblast — except, from a Kyiv channel, going to a Kyiv place of that name ("на Житомирську", the metro station). And is_relevant drops posts about power cuts, giveaways and subscriptions that name no weapon and no strike (NOT_SKY_RX). A relay of a report already read (another channel, ≤ 5 min, the same place) is still not drawn twice, but the height, descent, speed and origin it adds go onto the mark it relays (State._merge_relay, the evidence names the channel) — "Вишеньки 2900" had lost its 2,900 m that way. A post giving a height and a descent together ("Хотів 1200 знижується") keeps both (geo._alt_full).

Two tracks never show one tag: an approach mark a track took over (absorbed in State._chain) is no longer a report a later one can continue — it made a second track sharing that report, and both found the same tag by it — and TargetTags.assign skips a tag another track of the same pass already has (taken, the tracks taken oldest first). Place names ending in "-лець" decline with a soft sign (Козелець → Козельця, Козельцем): the stem index has that form too.

7.10 The 10-minute check (1.53; stopped in 1.54) and the night's report#

1.54: the owner chose to stop the check — nothing schedules it, the dashboard has no switch for it. The night's report stays (REPORT_VERSION 2: there are no checks' notes to read any more).

The owner (9 Oct 2026): one call a post costs too much at the channels' pace — the dashboard showed ~150 posts an hour at ~$0.0004 each (≈ 8,000 tokens in: the rules and eleven worked examples, for every post), the month's $10 gone in a week. The check (ai_reader_mode "check", the default): every ai_check_minutes (10, on the clock: :00, :10…) the ai-reader thread takes the queue (the same posts as before, AIReader.wants) and makes one call (AIReader.check → _check_call): the posts grouped by the oblast they concern (the oblast of the rules' marks, else of the places they name, else the channel's own; === OBLAST o14 · Київська область ===), each block with what the map shows there now (State.ai_map: every target in the air, tagged or for the whole oblast), each post with its time, channel, text (700 characters at most), RULES: (parser_lines of the rules' marks — or "set aside") and NAMES: (its candidate ids; one numbering for the whole check). At most 30 posts a call (1.53.1: 60 made answers past 6,000 tokens on a busy night); more → several, by oblast. An answer may be 12,000 tokens; the notes come first in it, and of an answer cut off at the length what came complete is kept (salvage_check; the check says what was lost, uncertain). 1.53.4: the first real checks (9 Oct, 18:40–19:41) were cut off 10 times in 12 at 6,000 tokens — a finding in the one-post schema cost 600–1,000 tokens. A finding is now flat (FIND, 18 fields: no origin, course, side, distance, speed or tag link; find_target gives validate the one-post shape), each check records how many findings it was given (findings.returned), and a check that still fails says how its answer began (error). The prompt (SYSTEM_CHECK, version 5) reuses the reading rules 1–12 of the one-post prompt (RULES_READ) and asks only for findings — where a post's words show the rules wrong or incomplete: missed (a target no rules line has), place, weapon, outcome, not_sky (the rules drew a target from a post that reports none), facts (the right place, a stated origin, height, descent, speed, count or destination missing) — each with its target in the one-post schema (CHECK_SCHEMA) and a few words why, and a note per oblast on what its posts show together. One worked check (CHECK_EXAMPLE) is the earlier turn. Every finding's target goes through validate with that post's text and candidates; a missed target at a place the rules have is facts; a not_sky where the rules drew nothing is dropped. Each post read gets its row (ai_reads, chk, cost 0, the word from check_verdict: agree / none / place / ai_only / kind / status / rules_only / facts) and the check its own (ai_checks: posts, tokens, cost, findings, notes). A failed call is kept as a failed check and its posts go back to the queue once. ~$0.001–0.002 a check.

What the findings do on the map is what 1.52's readings did: the facts fill the tags (_ai_facts; a missed or place target is never taken for the track's own, match); a missed / place target the rules have nowhere is drawn, marked AI (_ai_marks_of; facts, weapon, outcome never are) — up to ten minutes late, so never pushed (proximity_watch skips ai marks; the owner's decision). A namesake is the place the rules read (_canon drops the disambiguation: "Козин (Прилуцький р-н)" was drawn 120 km from the Kyiv Kozyn the post meant), and of several candidates of one name the one nearest the target's other place, the post's other places, the rules' marks or the channel's own oblast (Kyiv for the Kyiv channels) is kept, whichever the AI chose (pick_namesakes, anchors_of; both modes). The dashboard: the switch "How it reads" (mode), "Check now", the checks with their findings and notes, each post's findings with why.

The night's report. State.night_summary(night) is everything recorded over one tag night (12:00 → 12:00 Kyiv): the official alerts by oblast (minutes, alerts, first start, last end, raions), every tag with its path (target_points), heights, origin and who read it, minutes followed, reports, km, oblasts crossed; the entry places by letter; heights by band; the reports by hour and oblast and those for a whole oblast; the outcomes; the Air Force's summaries; the channels; the checks (cost, findings, every note) and every post where the AI and the rules parted. Counts and the places the posts named — no reader, no watched place. State.night_text is the same as plain text (compact: a path cut to its ends, 40 partings). The night-reports thread gives the compact text, after ai_report_hour (9), to the reader's model with REPORT_SYSTEM (sections: the night in brief, routes, heights and speeds, against the Air Force, patterns, Heimdall itself — only what the record shows, "reported", no forecast as a fact, ≤ 900 words) and keeps the answer (ai_reports). Dashboard → Analytics: "the AI's checks per night" (findings by kind, checks, posts, cost; CSV).

A period of nights (1.58.37), the owner: "it would make sense that it is included within the AI tab … the main goal is to aggregate as many data as possible to make a report, so we need a wider range of dates, not only one day at a time". The report card moved to the dashboard's AI tab and takes a period: from one night to State.PERIOD_MAX (31, the latest of a longer choice), picked with two lists or "last night / last 7 / 14 / 31 nights" (remembered, localStorage.perrange). State.period_summary(first, last) adds up each night's own record (night_summary, so the figures are the same ones): a line per night — tags, those followed over 2 places or more, reports and their kinds, oblasts under alert, Kyiv city's minutes under alert, the Air Force's night summary (launched / down), outcomes, the 3 oblasts most reported — then the totals: the alerts by oblast (minutes, alerts, nights), the reports by oblast and by hour, where tagged targets were first reported (30), the outcomes by oblast, the heights, the Air Force over the nights it summed up. State.period_text is the same as text. GET /api/admin/period gives the record and the report; "write the report" (POST /api/admin/ai_report with {from, to}) asks the night-reports thread: one night is that night's own report (ai_reports), several are written with ai_reader.PERIOD_SYSTEM (the period in brief, night by night, routes and places, timing, against the Air Force, patterns — "a pattern seen on two or three nights is a hint, not a rule: say how many nights it rests on"; ≤ 1,400 words) into ai_period_reports (first, last, at, nights, model, tokens, cost, text, error). The card also downloads the period's record (JSON, text) and every night of it in full (/api/admin/nights_export with the period's from / to), and lists the periods written before.

"Not written: empty answer" (1.58.37): the model thinks before it writes and its thinking counts against max_tokens — on a busy night the 3,000 of MAX_OUT_REPORT went on thinking and no word was left. MAX_OUT_REPORT is 10,000, MAX_OUT_PERIOD 14,000 (paid only as used); an answer cut off before its first word (finish_reason length, or every token used) is asked once more with MAX_OUT_RETRY (24,000), both costs counted; any other empty answer says why (the model stopped: <reason>, <n> tokens out); an answer in parts is joined.

Tags that hold (1.53), the owner: "the ID number changes during the course — it is probably the same drone". TargetTags.assign gives a track the tag its latest shown report had (shown, kept by show for every mark drawn), then the tag of any of its reports, oldest first; and a new track whose first report sits ≤ 6 km and ≤ 4 minutes from another tagged track's report of the same kind (another channel's word for the same target, State._twin) takes that track's tag — one target, two reports, one number (twins: those reports may share it).

Nothing over a target (1.53), the owner: "do not stack things like this". The label de-collision (tick) also keeps clear of every mark's height (.dn) and compact tag (.tgc); and the town names give way to the targets' glyphs and heights as well as their labels (LBLBOX). (Until 1.54 also of the explosions layer's glyphs and names — §10.1.)

The part of the post, and the course (1.58.39). A target's hover card, its sheet, an oblast's events and Light's row quote the part of the post it was read from (postPart, §7.3) — the sheet's details keep the whole post. The course has a line of its own, above (courseHtml, .crs): an arrow turned to the heading in a ring, the compass and the degrees, "→" and the town a post said it is heading for, in the accent colour — red, with "heading towards Kyiv", for an inbound one; none said, "heading not reported", quietly. The owner: "the direction is interesting and important data — it should stand out more". Zoomed out (svg.far, the map more than 90 km wide) a target's second line ("→ Oster / Desna … from the N · 06:23") is no longer written, heading for Kyiv or not — the owner: "it must not take so much space when zoomed out — ideally the destination appears only when you click the drone"; heading for the reader's own place (tohome), or descending, it still is. The shared text names the town too.

A short card (1.53): on a phone a target's card opens at most 38 % of the screen high — the kind, height, tag, where and the post in three lines; the track, the night's path, the notes and the evidence are .sec and come with "More" (#shmore, .sheet.more). 1.54.1, on a computer (the owner: "the UI isn't that great when we click a threat"): the card is no longer a bar across the whole window under the map but a card over the map's lower left, beside the list (#sheet at ≥ 1,000 px: 420 px wide, as high as it needs up to the map's height, scrolling inside, its buttons in a bar that stays in view); a target it would hide is brought, sideways and at the same zoom, to the middle of the map beside it (sheetClear, from the card's box without its slide). 1.54.2: a tap on an oblast's badge opens its card on a computer too — the map holds the pointer from the press (pointer capture), so the badge never received its click; it is read in the map's own pointerup, like a mark (downCh).

The map opens at once (1.53): sw.js keeps the satellite tiles (/tiles/sat/, cache heimdall-tiles-1, cache-first, ≤ 5,000, the oldest dropped) — their address carries the layer's version, so a kept tile is never stale — and answers the map's drawing (FAST: the map JSON files) from its cache at once, refreshing it behind. The page asks it once per version to fetch the tiles of Kyiv and the oblasts around it ahead (warmTiles, z5–8 over the region, z9–10 over Kyiv oblast, ~200 tiles), never with saveData or on 2G. Esri's closer tiles are left to the browser's cache. The data (/api/…) is never kept.

Full-screen map (1.53): the last button of the map's column (#fsbtn, yellow) hides the header and the drawer (body.fullmap) and asks the browser for full screen where it allows it (Android); the alert status stays as a chip top left (#fmchip, mirroring the band #kyiv; a tap, Escape or the phone's Back leaves). 1.58.32: on a computer it is the map alone, the whole width — it kept a blank 360 px column where the card had been (body.fullmap #stage{left:0}).

Side panels that fold (1.58.32, a computer or a landscape tablet, ≥ 1000 px). Oleksandr Tymoshenko: "an option to collapse or hide the side panels and to give users maximum map visibility … panels can collapse into a narrow ~50 px strip: on the left only the colour status dot and the total target count, on the right only the three tab icons". A chevron on each panel's inner edge (#lcolb, #rcolb, 18 × 56 px, mid-height) folds the card column (#mycard) or the drawer into a 52 px strip (body.lcol / body.rcol, the widths --lw / --rw: 360 / 380 px open, 52 px folded; the map, the sheet, the toasts and the bars follow them). The left strip (#lrail) keeps the band's colour (.rdot R / Y / P / H / N, from #kyiv) and the number of targets on the map (not ended, not stale; syncRails, on every change of the band and every 3 s), its tooltip the band's text; a click opens the panel. The right strip (#rrail) has Feed, Alerts and Stats — a click opens the drawer on that tab. Each panel's state is kept on the device (localStorage lcol, rcol); on a 1,024 px tablet the map goes from 284 px wide to 920. A phone keeps its drawer.

Every night in one file (1.58.27). The owner: "a sort of mega JSON document with all the reports compiled … so that I can hand you the readable report for pattern analysis". The dashboard's night card has Every night, 90 days: JSON Lines · text (GET /api/admin/nights_export, State.nights_export). JSON Lines — one JSON object a line, not one huge document: it is read a night at a time, a line cut off loses one night, and a new night is one more line. The first line is a header (schema: "heimdall-nights/1", the app's version, the window, the note on what the figures are); each next line is a night: night, summary (the record, State.night_summary, exactly what Record (JSON) gives for one night) and ai_report (at, model, text, error, cost, or null). Text is the same night by night for a person or an AI to read (State.night_text, compact, then the report). For patterns, the record is the source: the tags, their paths and heights, the alerts and the Air Force's figures as recorded; the AI's report is a reading of them — useful to read, never a number to count (it can be wrong). The direction patterns' own moves are /api/admin/patterns?raw=1 (§7.12).

7.11 The bulletins (1.54)#

The owner: "instead of all that on the map, summaries of the activity of the last hour / the last 24 hours, a bit like a weather bulletin". The bulletins thread (Bulletins) writes two, on the clock: the last hour and the last 24 hours, both once an hour at :00 — 1.56.1, the owner: "instead of a bulletin every 20 minutes, really only one an hour" (the last hour was every ai_bulletin_minutes, 20, gone with it).

  • The record. State.activity_summary(since, until) reads what the server itself recorded over the oblasts the map covers (scope_uids): the official air-raid alerts by oblast (minutes in the window, on now, started, ended, whole oblast or raions; Kyiv city first), the targets the rules drew (marker_log: reports, not drones) by oblast, kind and 10-minute or hourly slice, the window before for comparison, the places most reported, the targets reported for a whole oblast, the followed tracks' paths (target_points: the places in order, at most four shown), the heights, the outcomes (outcomes: shot down, explosions), what is on the map at the end of the window, and for 24 hours the Air Force's figures. State.activity_text makes it the plain text the AI is given. Counts, place names and times only — never a post's text, a channel's name in the answer, a reader, a watched place or a device. 1.54.8, everything it knows (the owner: "those data are precious — the goal will be pattern recognition too; we need to be aware of the maximum amount of things possible"): also the raions under alert (raion_names, up to 15), the first, last and busiest slice, the kinds of the window before, the missiles and carriers reported (missile marks on the map's oblasts, air_reports anywhere: MiG-31K, strategic bombers, Tu-22M3, Kalibr carriers — up, launch), the courses the marks carried by oblast (8-point compass), every stated height (heights_all: min / median / max, descending, by band and by oblast — heights stays the followed tracks'), the speeds stated, where the posts said the targets came from ("for the record, not for the bulletin"), the reports drawn only at their destination, the outcomes' times, the followed tracks with their tag, minutes, heights and up to 12 places (20 for the hour, 30 for the day), 25 / 40 places, 25 outcome places, and for 24 hours the night's weather (Kyiv and the launch areas, weather). A weapon not stated (kind_name, unstated_kind) is "probable drone (type not stated)" when no missile and no carrier was reported in the window, "drone or missile (type not stated)" when one was — with how many of them carried a drone tracker's signs (a height, a count, its drone mark). The night's report uses the same words (kind_name(…, raw=True)). The marks it is built from keep what they stated (marker_log 1.54.8 columns, Store.MLOG_EXTRA: alt_m, alt_state, speed_kmh, origin, target, likely, tag, approach). 1.58.30, what it held, said right (the owner: "I have the impression the current AI reports don't take the data of the dashboard's 'AI' part at all" — they are written from this record; the AI tab's readings and checks are 1.50–1.53's and feed nothing). Replayed from the night of 15 Oct, the record said less than it held, and now: a followed target keeps the kind its reports said — its points joined to marker_log by report: a jet in any report is a jet drone over the whole path, and a track tagged unknown takes the weapon its reports named (the jet followed from Pyriatyn to Borshchiv was "drone"); the heights are one list, each report once — the marks' (marker_log) and the followed targets' reports (target_points, where a height alone, "6700", lands) — so the count, the range, the median, the bands and the oblasts are all of it (it counted 18 reports and gave the range and bands of the 5 the marks carried); the top band reads 3000+ m (State.band; it read "3000 m", in the night's record too); and where a target was only heading is not where it was: a report drawn only at its destination ("→ Славутич", approach) is no longer among the places most reported — "where a post put a target" — but listed apart (heading_for, heading_by_oblast: "where they were going, not where they were — for the record, not for the bulletin"; 37 of 61 reports that hour). BULLETIN_VERSION 3: the prompt names it with the origins and courses it keeps out.
  • Only while there is activity (State.activity_active: a report, an outcome, an alert, a target on the map). A window with nothing in it gets the server's own line (Bulletins.QUIET, quiet = 1), once, with no call. The same record (sig, a hash of the text) is never written twice. The 24 hours are written again only after an hour with activity, or every 6 hours.
  • One call, three languages (AIReader.ask_bulletin): BULLETIN_SYSTEM (BULLETIN_VERSION 2 since 1.54.8, 3 since 1.58.30) and a strict schema {uk, en, fr}; bulletin_clean takes out markdown, links, channel names and card numbers and cuts a text past BULLETIN_CHARS (1.54.8: 1,100 / 2,600 characters; it was 700 / 1,600) at its last full sentence; a language missing is an error. 1.54.8, "more leeway on the token cutoff": MAX_OUT_BULLETIN 4,000 / 8,000 tokens (it was 1,200 / 2,400 — three languages and a model that thinks first ran out), BULLETIN_TIMEOUT 120 s (a post's reading keeps 40). The prompt's rules: only what the record shows, "reported" (channel reports, not radar, not a count of drones), Kyiv first, alerts only from the official ones, never "safe", never shelter advice, never a forecast (a trend in the record may be said), never where targets came from or are heading (a followed target's places in order may be told), Kyiv time, 90 / 220 words a language (1.54.8; it was 60 / 140), and never "unknown" — "probably drones", or "drones or missiles" while missiles are reported, never a certainty. Within the month's budget like every call; its refusals (off, paused, budget, rate) store nothing.
  • Kept in ai_bulletins with the record it was written from, the cost and any error — 1.54.8: every one for BUL_FULL_DAYS (90), then for good one an hour (the last hour's first of each clock hour) and one a day (the 24 hours' nearest noon, Kyiv time: the night), marked kept; quiet windows and failed attempts go at 90 days (Store.bulletin_prune, once a day, whole Kyiv days). It was 30 days, all of them. About 25 rows a busy day (~150 kB), far less on a quiet one; the dashboard shows a record up to 30,000 characters.
  • In the app (GET /api/bulletin): only while the dashboard's switch is on (kv ai_bulletin_public = "1"), the latest bulletin of each kind — and only while the server still stands by it: each turn that finds the latest still true (nothing new, the same record) sets kv bul_checked:<kind>, and a bulletin neither written nor checked within 75 minutes is not shown (the hour's was 35 until 1.56.1) — when the AI cannot write (off, no key, out of budget) it goes stale and leaves the app. The Tactical page shows it over the Near list (#bul in the drawer on a phone, #bulL in the left column on a computer): "Bulletin · AI", the last hour / 24 hours, the text in the reader's language, the time it was written and "written by AI from the channels' posts and the official alerts — not radar" (loadBulletin, every 2 minutes; localStorage.bulk remembers the tab).
  • Dashboard → AI: the AI's switch and the app's, "write the last hour / 24 hours now", the month's bulletins and their cost, each bulletin in three languages with the record it was written from; the readings and checks of 1.50–1.53 are folded below ("stopped — not used by the bulletins"). 1.58.30: each bulletin says first what its record held — "Written from: 61 reports (37 of them only where heading) · 4 targets followed · 18 heights · official alerts in 2 oblast(s) (1 on now) · 3 outcomes · 22 on the map · the Air Force's figures · the night's weather" (bulFacts) — read back from the record itself by State.record_facts (so the bulletins written before say it too; a quiet window's line has none).

7.12 The direction patterns (1.55; the dashboard only)#

The owner (10 Oct 2026): "one crucial detail we need to aggregate through time and accumulate is the pattern of direction of the drones — what pattern the typical drone follows depending on its location — proper foundations so we can then calculate the % of probability that the drone goes to X, Y or Z. Those data shouldn't be seen yet inside the app, but on the admin dashboard." Nothing of it reaches the app: no page asks for it, and /api/admin/patterns is 🔒.

  • A move is one tagged target's step from a reported position to the next (§7.8: target_points, in time order): Patterns.moves_of. A report within MOVE_MIN_KM (2 km) of the last position is the target still there — the move starts from its latest sighting there; a report further than the target could have flown (reach_km, the physical check of 1.54.7: 350 / 650 km/h, + 8 km for two named places) is another target's and is left out; after a gap of more than MOVE_MAX_MIN (120 min) the track starts again. Each move keeps where it was and where the next report put it (lon, lat, place), the time of each, km, minutes, the bearing, the heights, the track's kind (move_kind: drone, jet — a mark of the track flagged jet —, banderol, unstated — a weapon not stated, a probable drone —, or a missile's own type, kept but out of the drones' patterns), where a post said it came from (origin, osrc) and the builder's version (ver).
  • Kept for good in track_moves (the points go after TAG_KEEP_DAYS, 60 days). The patterns thread rebuilds the nights of the last two days every hour (a night still going gets its new moves: its rows are replaced), and once per MOVES_VERSION (kv moves_v; moves_at, the last build) every night whose points are all still kept (BACKFILL_DAYS, 58). A night older than that keeps the moves its version built.
  • No night left out (1.58.37), the owner: "we should have thousands of drones by now, yet the number recorded is fairly minimal". The dashboard showed 2 nights of moves where 12 nights of tags were kept: a night only got its moves while it was among the last two days, so an hour the thread did not run (a deploy, a restart, an error) lost that night for good. Every hour Patterns.gaps() now also builds every night whose points are kept and that has no tag move yet; one whose tags gave no move (each reported at one place) is remembered (kv moves_empty) instead of being built again. How the last hour went is kept (kv moves_last: nights, gaps caught up, moves, the nights still to read again from their posts, an error) and shown beside "moves built" on the Patterns tab. busy() no longer waits on an alert of Kyiv or its oblast open for more than BUSY_MAX_H (12 h) — an end the feed missed would have held the reading of old nights back for ever. The words now say the scale: on the nights of the tracks, the targets tagged and those followed over 2 places or more, and what the Air Force said was launched and shot down over all Ukraine (its night summaries, dated the morning after) — coverage.scale, tag_nights_kept, builder.
  • The reading (Patterns.report): over the last days (0: all), for a kind (PATTERN_KINDS: drones — all four drone kinds —, drone, jet with the Banderol, unstated), on a grid of MOVE_CELL (0.2° × 0.125°, about 14 × 14 km) × res (1, 2, 4: ~14, 28, 56 km). For each area with min moves out of it or min stated courses in it: its moves, tracks and nights; where the next report put the target (places and areas, a share of the moves out of it; stay, the moves that stayed in the area); where the tracks that passed through it were last reported (a share of those tracks); the 8 directions of its moves, their mean bearing and how consistent they are (the mean resultant length, 0–1); the courses the posts stated there (marker_log heading, town-level positions, no approach mark; 1.56.3: not a course worked out from two of a channel's reports) and the destinations they named (target); where a post said these tracks came from (osrc rules / ai — a region's "probably" is not counted); the median speed and the median minutes to the next report. Every share comes with its 95% range (wilson, Wilson's interval): 2 moves of 3 is 67% (21–94%), 200 of 300 is 67% (61–72%) — the probability's honesty, shown beside every %. The names of an area are the places most reported in it.
  • 1.56.1: from the first night the posts are kept. The tags began on 9 Oct 2026 and the posts are kept since 18 Sep: every night before the first tagged one is read again from its posts with today's rules (Patterns.build_replay: State._replay_tracks, the replay's own tracks, 12:00 to 12:00 Kyiv time) and its moves are written with src "replay" (a tag's are "tag"; a replayed track is numbered REPLAY_N + its order: no origin, no height — the replay keeps neither). At most REPLAY_PER_TICK (6) nights an hour, REPLAY_PAUSE_S (10 s) apart, and none while Kyiv or its oblast is under an air-raid alert or a missile threat is up (busy) — the live map comes first; a night once done (for this MOVES_VERSION) is not done again (replay_nights). Two reports of one track less than MOVE_MIN_MIN (2 min) apart are no move (two places reported at once: a quarter of the first build's moves were 0 minutes); a Banderol (banderol_missiles) is counted with the jet drones (it was left out); an area is named by the places most reported in it, the marks' and the moves' together (the cell of Kyiv is "Київ", not a village one track passed); the destinations the posts named are a share of the courses that named one. MOVES_VERSION 2: every kept night is built again.
  • In plain words (1.56.1, the owner: "see whether you can identify patterns, in plain English"): Patterns.plain(report) turns the figures into sentences, English and Ukrainian (plain in the answer), only where they say something — an area with PLAIN_MIN courses stated (30), moves out of it (8) or tracks through it (8), whose main course, next place or last place holds a share whose 95% range starts at PLAIN_LO or more (30%, 25%, 25%): "Славутич: of the 526 courses the posts gave here, 53% were south and 31% north (95% range for the first: 49–57%)". Strongest first (the bottom of the range), 12 at most, after one line on what it is built from (tracks, nights, live and replayed moves, courses stated and since when, "the channels' reports, not radar"). What was reported — never a forecast.
  • These are the channels' reports, not radar: a target nobody reported between two places is one move between them, a channel that names only big towns makes big moves, and an area's shares are only as good as the tracks the chains kept together (§7.4). The foundation of a probability, not one: a next-area share is a first-order transition (where it went next from here, whatever it did before), and the moves' CSV (raw=1, every move of the window, at most 50,000) is there for any further model.
