  • Dashboard → Patterns (static/admin.html, loadPatterns / drawPatterns): the period (30 / 90 days, a year, all), the kind, the area size; the counts (tracks, moves, nights, stated courses, areas) and when the moves were last built; in plain words (the sentences of Patterns.plain, or why there are none yet); a flow map — the oblasts (static/ukraine-map.json), each area shaded by the moves out of it (one blue ramp, square root), an arrow for its mean direction (longer when more of them go the same way), and from the area chosen, lines to the areas the next report put it in (width by share, the top three labelled); the chosen area's card — the directions (its moves and the stated courses, % of each, one axis), the next places, the last-reported places, the next areas, the destinations named, the origins said, each with its range as a bar; every area in a table (click a row, or an area on the map); CSV of the areas and of every move. English and Ukrainian.

8. Translation#

The feed is Ukrainian; EN and FR readers get a translation.

  • At ingest: the offline glossary only (translate_offline) — instant. It never waits for a network call: the free Google endpoint refuses data-centre addresses, and each refusal used to cost ~3 s per post in front of the drone reports of the same poll. The glossary (G, WORDS) covers the channels' vocabulary, collected from ~1,000 real posts; anything left is transliterated.
  • Then, on demand: machine translation (Translations worker): DeepL (DEEPL_KEY), else Google Cloud (GOOGLE_TRANSLATE_KEY), else the free Google endpoint. Domain words are fixed before (PRE, PRE_FR: "мопед" → Shahed, "реактив" → jet drone, "ударні" → strike drones) and after (POST, POST_FR).
  • Only for a language somebody reads. A page in EN/FR sends ?lang= on /api/feed and /api/stream; for the hour after, new posts are translated into it, and older ones in view are queued. The result is stored (feed.text_en + en_mt = 1, or feed.text_fr) and announced with a tr event; the page reloads the feed.
  • Circuit breaker: a backend that refuses (401/403/429/456) rests 30 min, any other failure 5 min.
  • French has no offline stand-in: without a machine translator, FR shows the English.

8b. Nights and weeks (the dashboard's record) — not written since 1.58.37#

1.58.37, the owner: "the nights and weeks tab can also go away". The tab is gone and the Digests thread is no longer started: the AI's report on a period of nights (§7.10, the AI tab) does the same from the full record of every oblast. The digests written before stay in digests; GET /api/digests still lists them, POST /api/digest/run answers 503. What follows is how they were made.

A record that builds up from now on (no import of older data). After each night (18:00–08:00 Kyiv time; written up after digest_hour, 09:00, once the Air Force morning summary is out) and each Monday for the week before, State.digest_facts() computes, from what the app itself recorded, for Kyiv city and Kyiv oblast: minutes under official alert (overlaps counted once), number of alerts and the longest, alert waves (starts within 30 min are one wave), alert levels, minutes per oblast raion; the channels' reports (not a count of targets) by type, the most-named places, the reported courses (8 sectors), the busiest hours, first / last report; shoot-downs and explosions reported and where; how many waves had a mark on the map in the hour before them and the median lead; the Air Force's own figures (all of Ukraine, from af_reports: each summary of the period with launched and shot down by type, the types used without a number, hit locations, main directions). A week also gets the previous week's totals to compare with.

With ANTHROPIC_API_KEY set, the figures are sent to the model (ai_model) — counts and place names only, never a post or anything about a reader — with a system prompt (AI_SYSTEM) that allows it to describe them and nothing else: no forecast, no guessed intention, target, weapon or damage, "reports" called reports. It answers in English and Ukrainian. The figures are stored with the text (digests), shown under it on the dashboard, so every sentence can be checked. Without a key the figures are still stored and shown. The model is only ever called from the Digests thread — never in a request path. Cost: a few thousand tokens a night, well under a dollar a month.

8c. The dashboard (1.32: tabs and Analytics)#

/admin has six tabs (the last one opened is remembered, localStorage.admintab; one that no longer exists opens Live): Live (online now, the admin's message with a preview in the readers' colours, the nuclear event — §9), Analytics, Patterns (1.55, §7.12: where the drones go, area by area, and since 1.56.1 in plain words — the dashboard only), Sources (lead time, official sources, the channels and their controls, translation; a red dot on the tab when a source or a channel on the map fails), AI reader (1.50, §7.7: the shadow reader's readings beside the rules'; 1.58.37: first, the AI's report on a period of nights — §7.10) and Usage (the anonymous counters). Each tab loads its own data; only Live, Sources and AI reader refresh on their own (10 s, 60 s, 60 s while open).

Analytics (GET /api/analytics?days=7|30|90|365, State.analytics, cached 5 min) — the trends, for the owner and his partners, "Excel-like":

  • all of Ukraine, from af_reports (a date = the night that ends that morning + that day): drones launched (Shahed type / jet / Banderol) with the number shot down; missiles shot down by type (the launched numbers the Air Force gave, and the types used without a number, on hover); the share of drones shot down or suppressed and the share of jet drones (drones only — the missiles launched are not always given, and a rate over everything would read higher than it was); hit and debris locations; launch areas, main directions and models named;
  • Kyiv, from the app's own records: hours under official air-raid alert per day for the city (oblast 31) and the oblast (any raion of 14); when alerts start in the city (weekday × hour); the marks placed over the city and the oblast by hour, and the drone courses stated in posts (8 sectors; none guessed). 1.58.37: the marks per 24 h by type are no longer drawn (the owner: "mostly useless data, since it targets only Kyiv");
  • the KPIs compare the last 7 days with the 7 before; a date without a summary says so instead of showing zeros;
  • (1.33 – 1.58.36) the carriers (§7.6) are no longer charted (the owner: "vastly inaccurate, it can go"); the posts are still read for the bulletins' record (§7.11);
  • (1.33) the weather: one dot per night — the mean wind, cloud cover and rain over the launch areas (Orel, Kursk, Bryansk, Shatalovo, Millerovo, Primorsko-Akhtarsk; 18:00–06:00) against the drones the Air Force says were launched that night; Pearson's r, the number of nights and a least-squares line from 8 nights, with the reminder that a link is not a cause;
  • (1.33) the approach sides: drone positions the posts named in Kyiv oblast 20–130 km from the centre, by side of Kyiv (8 sectors), per 24 h from 18:00 (a post once per side; town-level positions only), and the total per side;
  • (1.33) hours under alert by oblast, all Ukraine: official air-raid alerts in any part of each oblast, per week (per day up to 14 days), coloured by the share of the time in the heat layer's bins; an alert row a restart left open counts only while it is really active.

Each of the new cards has its CSV (carriers, weather with the night's drones, approach sides, oblast hours). Charts are SVG drawn in the page (no library), in the validated dark categorical order (blue, orange, aqua, yellow, magenta — never by rank), one axis each, a legend for two series or more, a tooltip on every mark; the width follows the screen so a phone can read them. Every chart's table downloads as CSV for Excel (UTF-8 with its mark, ; between columns): the Air Force by date and type, Kyiv's alert minutes, Kyiv's reports by type. Nothing about a reader is in it.

9. Notifications#

  • Web Push (push.py; the VAPID key pair is generated on first run and kept in the kv table). A subscription stores the browser's coarse location snapped to a ~10 km cell (HOME_CELL_KM), never a name — 1.58.35: the phone snaps it before sending (cellOf), the server's rounding is the guard. 1.58.35: a subscription is accepted only when it is a browser's (push.clean_subscription: https, one of the push services in PUSH_HOSTS, a 65-byte p256dh, a 16-byte auth, at most 1 KB), and only {endpoint, keys} is stored; a send with no answer or a refusal is a strike (fails), the third removes the row, a 2xx dates last_ok; rows that are not a browser's are removed at start (§11.1).
  • How it is sent (1.29): all the phones at once — push.Sender, 32 sends in flight, each worker keeping one open HTTPS connection per push service, the VAPID signature made once an hour per service. It used to be one phone at a time on a new connection (~0.15 s per Android phone, up to ~0.5 s per iPhone): with a thousand subscribers the last one heard of a ballistic missile minutes late. tests/test_push_fanout.py: 400 phones against a 50 ms push service in well under 3 s, over at most 32 connections.
  • What is pushed: MiG-31K airborne (country-wide), ballistic threat (Kyiv city/oblast), and from proximity_watch: a live, non-stale target within the subscriber's radius (+ half a cell), throttled to one push per 2 min per device — except immediate types, which alert the whole region with no throttle. Siren-start and all-clear pushes were removed on purpose (nothing to act on, and they train people to swipe).
  • In the page: the Tactical page raises toasts with sound/vibration scaled by zone (near / approach / observe). The sound has a volume (the bell: slider 0–100 %, 0 = off, 50 % for a new reader, kept in localStorage as sndvol): every tone goes through one Web Audio master gain, squared from the slider (50 % = −12 dB), because an iPhone ignores an <audio> element's volume. 100 % is the pre-1.28 level; letting go of the slider or ▶ (test) plays the loudest alert at the chosen level. The zones keep their relative levels under it. Push notifications (app closed) use the phone's own notification sound and volume.
  • The escalation level (1.36, escLevel()), 0–4, from what is already known — never from a guess: 1 an official alert over the watched place's own raion (or, with no place set, over Kyiv); 2 a live target within 30 km of the place, or one a post says is heading for it; 3 within 10 km, or a target at a Kyiv bridge (with the bridges on); 4 a fresh ballistic or MiG-31K warning, or an immediate type over the region. It colours the card's edge (#mycard.lv2–lv4) and the bell's dot, and picks the tone: l1 one soft sine (an official alert starting), l2 two short beeps, l3 three rising beeps twice, l4 a 3-second two-tone warble with no gap — each with its vibration (VIB_L). At most one tone in 10 s unless the level rises (TONE), so a busy night does not become a noise people mute. The bell's ▶ plays l2, l3, l4 in turn at the chosen volume. And a shoot-down notice for a track the reader was warned about — worded so it can never read as an all-clear.
  • What makes a sound (1.58.44 — a tester: "it keeps pinging constantly … what to actually fear is unclear. Missile threats make sense — no radius there. But for UAVs, it's confusing"). Three things had been ringing: every official alert, threat and all-clear of any raion of Kyiv oblast and the city; with no place set, every post about Kyiv; and the same drone again at each new report (warned by report, not by target). Now, per target (sndFor): missiles, ballistic, MiG-31K, Kh-22, cruise missiles, KABs (SND_REGION) — anywhere within REGION_R (150 km), no radius (l4 for the immediate types, l3 for the rest; body n_imm_b / n_reg_b); a drone (a Shahed, a jet, a Banderol, a target of no stated type) — l2 once it is inside the reader's radius SND_R (the bell: 10 / 20 / 30 km / the oblast = REGION_R; 20 by default; localStorage sndr), l2 when a post names the place (hi.named) or its stated course points at it within 40 km (±20°, courseAtHome; an approach mark's course, a guessed one, none), l3 inside 10 km; a whole-oblast report, an outcome, a stale or held mark — none. Once per target: the key is its track (trackKey: the tag, else the report), with the highest rank it sounded at (1 radius, 2 heading this way, 3 inside 10 km or a missile) — it sounds again only at a higher rank (SND); homeAlerted follows the track, so its "shot down" still comes. Official alerts: a start and an all-clear of the reader's own area only (areaWatch, on every state: placeLevel(HOME) going to R/Y, l1, or back to G); a threat event for the area it covers (coversHome), a ballistic or MiG-31K one for Kyiv and its oblast for everybody. No place set: no target sound; once, silently, "Set your place to get sound alerts" (snd_noplace). Under the picker, one line says it (snd_why). Light (vibration within 10 km) and the pushes (a radius, 15 km by default, every 2 minutes at most) are unchanged. 1.58.56: "inside 10 km" is the reader's near zone, NEAR_R (10 / 15 / 20 km, 15 unless chosen), here and on Light.
  • The approach signal (1.58.56 — a reader: "a signal when entering the zones — the closer, the more alarming; in the very last zone periodic, the frequency increasing as it approaches"; the owner: by the distance reported). While a target was reported inside the near zone in the last BEEP_FRESH_MIN (5) minutes — not stale, ended, held, a whole oblast or a region-wide type (those keep their own sound) — a short tick (beep('tick'), one 70 ms sine) repeats every beepEvery(d) = 1 + 7·d/NEAR_R seconds: 8 s at the zone's edge, 1 s at the pin. d is the last reported distance (NEAR_ROWS; posOf moves nothing while the estimate is off): the pace changes only with a new report, and nothing moves between two. A chip over the map says it ("🔔 6 km · reported 1 min ago" — bp_chip); a tap silences the targets ticking now (BEEP_MUTE, by track; one that leaves the zone and comes back, or a new one, ticks again). The bell's switch "Approach signal" (#beepbtn, localStorage beep, on unless switched off) and the sound's own switch and volume govern it; not during the setup.
  • The admin's message (1.29; headed "Message from the system admin" since 1.30): the dashboard publishes one message (UK / EN / FR, info / warning / urgent, for 1 h – 3 days or until removed), stored in kv.notice and carried in the alerts state (notice), so the live line and /api/state bring it to both pages as a banner. A reader can close it (remembered on that phone, notice_x). Optionally sent as a push to every subscribed phone (the Ukrainian text). Since 1.32 it is loud: Information is bright green, Urgent bright red with a slow pulse (one cycle in 1.6 s, still under prefers-reduced-motion), the same in every display mode; Warning stays amber. The dashboard previews it in that colour while it is written.
  • The nuclear event (1.32): the one message that covers the map. On the dashboard (Live tab) it is folded away in a danger zone with three locks: Arm (the server hands back a one-time 6-character code, valid 2 minutes, one attempt right or wrong — State.nuke_arm), then the code and the phrase NUCLEAR EVENT (or, 1.42.2, ЯДЕРНА ПОДІЯ — case and spacing never matter) typed in full (the button unlocks only then), then the button held for 3 s (it keeps the pointer while held — setPointerCapture — so a drift off it does not stop the hold; Space or Enter held works too); State.nuke_fire checks the code, its age and the phrase again. 1.42.2: the arming is also kept in kv.nuke_arm, so a restart between arming and firing (every release, and the crashes of the shared-CPU nights) no longer answers "not armed"; still one attempt, still two minutes. A failed fire shows the server's answer in full. Preview draws the symbol and the banner with the text written above in a window of the dashboard only — nothing is sent or stored; it is the way to test. The text (UK / EN / FR, prefilled with shelter-in-place advice to edit) and an optional push to every phone (confirmed). It is stored in kv.nuke, logged, published as a nuke event and carried in the alerts state (nuke), so it survives restarts and reaches every open page in seconds. The pages download static/nuke.js only while one is on: the radiation trefoil over the whole map (Light: over the radar), pulsing once every 1.6 s and still under reduced motion, pointer-events: none so the map still works; a red banner with "NUCLEAR EVENT — RADIATION DANGER", the admin's text (as text, never HTML) and "Heimdall is an unofficial app, not an alert system — follow ДСНС and local authorities"; the banner can be folded, not closed; one critical tone (at the reader's volume) and a vibration, once per event per phone. End is one confirmed click and clears every page. tests/test_nuke_analytics.py holds the locks.
  • A new address (1.29): with CANONICAL_HOST set, pages opened on any other address show "Heimdall has moved to …" with a button to the new one; the phone's settings (language, display, sound, oblast, layers — the CARRY list) travel in the link's #fragment (never sent to a server) and are stored there only where the new address has nothing of its own (<script id="carry">). The watched places do not travel: they never leave the phone, not even in a link that browser history might keep; the reader sets them again. scripts/set-domain.bat attaches the domain (Fly certificates, DNS records) and sets it. 1.58.34 — done: the address is heimdall.com.ua (bought 18 October 2026 at NIC.UA; its name servers; A and AAAA for @ and www to the app's Fly addresses; Fly's Let's Encrypt certificate; CANONICAL_HOST set). One machine, one database, two doors: kyiv-air-watch-gb.fly.dev serves the same app and shows the banner — it is not closed, because an installed app, its push subscription and its pins belong to the address they were made on. The README, the manual's cover, release.bat's live check and diagnose.bat's pings use the new address (.site overrides it); the channel's link follows CANONICAL_HOST. The Android app (§10.8) needs a new build for the new address.

9b. The Kyiv bridges (1.34)#

From 1 October 2026 the Dnipro bridges are the targets — the Southern bridge hit five times in two days, the Northern bridge on 3 and 4 October, by Shaheds and jet drones, by day, with cars on them. The live trackers post just the bridge's name ("Південний міст", "🔻Зниження Північний міст", "на міст патона", "Північний міст падає‼️✈️"), and until 1.34 those posts were dropped as saying nothing, or drawn at the centre of Kyiv.

  • The eight crossings (BRIDGES, north to south): the Kyiv HPP Dam at Vyshhorod (ges, the road on the dam; 1.58.41: «Гребля Київської ГЕС», not «Дамба» — the owner: «гребля» is the hydro-dam holding the reservoir back, «дамба» an embankment along the shore; posts saying either are read, and BRIDGE_BY_PLACE and the page's placeLbl still know the old name in older records), Pivnichnyi (Northern), the Petrivskyi railway bridge (petrivskyi, "Рибальський залізничний міст" on the map), the Podilsko-Voskresenskyi crossing (podilskyi: the harbour, Rybalskyi, the arch over the Dnipro, Trukhaniv island, Raiduzhnyi — one bridge, named by "Подільський" or "Воскресенський"), Metro, Paton, Darnytskyi (also "the Darnytskyi railway bridge", 100 m from it) and Pivdennyi (Southern). Each is its line over the water, pts — two points, or more where it bends (1.38) — from OpenStreetMap; bridge_km measures to the nearest piece. They are in the gazetteer (geo.PLACES, the middle of each crossing — "Київська ГЕС" at the HPP too — with every declension and the Russian names in geo.ALT), so a post naming one places its mark on it; plus "Рибальський острів". A traffic notice about them ("рух … перекритий", "смугою", "контактна мережа", "режим руху") and a video of a strike ("Момент вчорашнього удару…") are news: no mark. tests/fixtures/bridge_posts_2026_10.json: 43 real posts of 1–4 October, each with the bridge it puts a live mark on, read by hand (24 on a bridge, 19 news / traffic / hits after the fact / "Міст" alone: none).
  • The state, bridge by bridge (State.bridges_now, carried in the alerts state as bridges — a dict by bridge, so the live line sends only the bridge that changed): at — a live mark on the bridge or within 1.2 km of its span, or a post of the last 5 minutes that names it (when its mark went elsewhere: "Повз лівий берег на Південний міст") and says nothing happened to the target; near — within 3.5 km (a Shahed's minute), 6 km for a jet drone or a missile; hit — only a post of the last 30 minutes that names the bridge and says it was hit (BRIDGE_HIT_RX: влучання, пошкоджено, зруйновано, приліт). 1.38: an explosion is not a hit — on 5 October a bare "Вибух" (the interception), placed at the tracker's last position, made "hit reported" on two bridges for half an hour. Never from an outcome (shot down, explosion, lost), a stale mark, a mark outside Kyiv city / oblast, one placed at a vague point ("Київ" alone, a bank of the river: 2–4 km from several bridges at once), or anything reported before the official all-clear for Kyiv city (State.kyiv_city_all_clears). With the distance, the type, the place named, the time and the channel.
  • The pages: Tactical — a bar in the card (red for at / hit, amber for near), one line since 1.38: the bridge that matters most, the distance, how long ago, and "+N" for the others (a tap on it lists them; a tap on the bar → the map on that bridge); the crossings drawn on the map in their state, named a few pixels past their east end (placeBrgLabels, at every zoom) — a lit one always, the others only when zoomed in under 22 km; and — the bell → "Kyiv bridges" (on by default) — a toast, a tone at the reader's volume (the level-3 tone at the bridge, louder for a jet drone or a missile; level 2 near it, §9) and a vibration when one rises. Light — one line above the places (the bridge that matters most, "+N"), a vibration when one reaches at.
  • Push: the same toggle subscribes the phone to the topic bridges (push_subs.topics, with its lang); bridge_watch sends "🌉 Південний міст: ціль за 2,3 км — Реактивний БпЛА · 17:33 · з дописів каналів, не радар. Не заїжджайте на міст, якщо можете." in the phone's language.
  • Faster reading: while a threat is within 12 km of a bridge (State.bridges_busy), the web channels are read every 10 s and the Telegram-API ones every 8 s, as in a missile threat.
  • Every rise is kept (bridge_events) for the dashboard's "Kyiv bridges" card.

Limits, said in the texts: the positions are the places the posts named (a district is ±1 km), and a post comes 1–3 minutes after the sighting — a jet drone covers 6 km in that time. A bridge with nothing reported is "nothing reported", never "safe".

9c. The owner's private Telegram channel (1.58)#

Not started since 1.58.38: the bot of §9d replaced it (the owner: "we need to completely re-think the Telegram bot"; his pick: "replace the channel"). app/chanpost.py stays — its words (the labels, the course a post stated, the height, the outcomes, link_outcomes), its Bot API client and the picture's setup are the bot's — and so does the setup script (the token). What follows is how the channel worked.

The owner: "a small private Telegram channel that posts alerts like the channels we read, with a 'screenshot' of the app where the drone is (Kyiv and Kyiv oblast only)" — a post per target, with a picture in the app's style drawn on the server. Off unless TG_BOT_TOKEN and TG_CHANNEL_ID are set; then main() starts ChannelPoster (app/chanpost.py, thread channel). Its rules are SAFETY.md's "The private channel says what the map says". 1.58.7, the owner: "instead of editing the post, we need to add a screenshot every time — it's better to keep track of what's going on", and "when the alarm is over, or it exploded or fell, or the threat is no longer here, we should mark it too".

Its sources (1.58.9, the owner: "for the Telegram bot we'll take a single source: t.me/chyste_nebo"; 1.58.12, after @chyste_nebo fell silent at 03:59 while kyiv_airdef and kievinfo_kyiv reported a drone towards Boryspil, his pick: "all the Kyiv sources"): SOURCES = ("chyste_nebo", "kyiv_airdef", "kievinfo_kyiv"), the three Kyiv live trackers — only a mark whose latest report is one of theirs is posted (a target the Air Force or a regional channel reported last waits for theirs; gone from them 5 min, "no new reports"), and only their outcomes close a target (link_outcomes over their marks alone). The official alerts and all-clears are the official data's, as before; the posts still name no channel.

What it posts. Every 5 s it takes the marks as the pages see them (State.markers_now()) and keeps (chanpost.eligible) the ones of its source over Kyiv city or Kyiv oblast (oblast_uid 31 / 14) at a named place, of a flying type (drones, the missiles, Banderol, a weapon not stated) — no outcome (status), no faded mark (stale), no merged one (superseded_by), no oblast-wide guess, no reading still waiting for its context. A target whose post named only where it is heading ("Балістика на Київ!": approach, a place "→ Київ") is posted as the app draws it — at that town, fainter, set off on the side a post named, no course ray — and its caption says "📍 Де саме зараз — у дописі не сказано"; 1.58.7: while that place has no official alert (held, §7.4) it has no silhouette there and the caption says "⚪ Офіційної тривоги там поки немає — значка на мапі немає, доки її не оголосять"; the alert starting is a new post, with it. They are grouped by target tag (tag.night + tag.id, else the mark's id): one thread of posts per target.

When Call What
a new target, reported in the last 5 min (FRESH_S), not already ended by an outcome sendPhoto the picture and the caption; the phone rings. A target closed before and reported again: a new thread, in reply to its last line
the same target reported again (a new place, course, count, height, path, minute, held or drawn) sendPhoto with reply_parameters 1.58.7: a new post, in reply to its last one, at most every 60 s (UPDATE_EVERY_S) and only for a report of the last 5 min — never an edit
an outcome a post reported, linked to it as kyiv.html's linkStatus links it (link_outcomes: down, impact, lost, clear; same family, −2…40 min, ≤ 60 km, ahead of a stated course beyond 8 km, "clear" ≤ 25 km; one each) sendMessage, in reply 1.58.7: "⬇️ Збито / Приземлено — так у дописі о HH:MM" (the app's st_* words: "💥 Вибух (влучання або перехоплення — невідомо)", "❔ Локаційно втрачено", "✅ Повідомлено: у районі чисто") and the target's line. Not where it happened, no picture. Also once after "no new reports" (a target gone from the map is linked by its last report)
the official alert of the target's area gone for 60 s (CLEAR_AFTER_S) — 1.58.24: an alert of one of its hromadas keeps a raion on (area_on) sendMessage, in reply to the area's last post 1.58.7: "✅ Відбій тривоги о HH:MM · Броварський район (офіційно)" — once per area and alert, closing the targets still open there
gone from the flying marks for 5 min (END_AFTER_S) sendMessage, in reply "⏹ Нових дописів після HH:MM немає" and the target's line

The caption (Telegram HTML, Ukrainian): the type as the app names it (Шахед, Реактивний дрон, Балістична ракета, Бандероль (реактивний дрон), "Ймовірно, дрон — тип не вказано" — as asDrone: no missile within 150 km), the count, the place; a course only when a post stated it (stated_course: a target town, or compass words with a confidence — never the bearing the app worked out between two reports, nor a destination a later post gave a carried mark); the height a post gave — 1.58.9, the owner: "I see the altitude is not shown most of the time": @chyste_nebo gives a height in a post of its own ("3800", "Бондарня другий 4600") and names only places after it, so when this report gave none, the latest one an earlier report of the same track gave (one of its sources'), with its time ("~4600 м (у дописі о 00:44)", stated_alt), only if said at most 4 minutes before this report (ALT_MAX_S, the maps' ALT_MAX_MIN); on the picture "↕ ~4600 м · 00:44" —; the time of the report (Kyiv) and of the first one; "З дописів каналів, не радар. Сирена й офіційна тривога — головне."; a link to /m (PUBLIC_URL). 1.58.7, the owner: "no need to say the source of info" — the channel a post came from is no longer named (nor in a closing line).

Areas and the all-clear (app/areas.py, 1.58.7): area_at(lon, lat) finds the shape of static/kyiv-map.json a point is in — the shapes the Tactical page paints the alerts on, Kyiv oblast's raions and the city first —, and under_alert says whether alert_fill (moved here from mapshot.py, shared with the server) paints it. A channel target's area (area_of): "kyiv-city", a raion of Kyiv oblast, or "14:*" for one placed only in a part of the oblast (the oblast is "on" while any of its raions is). An area under alert with a target in it is watched (ChannelPoster.watch); its alert gone for a minute (a flicker of the feed is no all-clear), one line says so. The watches live in memory only: after a restart the alerts are not read yet, so an area must be seen under alert again before its all-clear can be said; the kill switch clears them (no all-clear of an alert that ended while it was off).

The picture (app/mapshot.py, MapShot.render, ~0.3 s plain, ~0.5 s over the satellite): a 1080 × 1350 JPEG (1.58.10: portrait, 4:5 — the oblast is taller than wide), drawn at twice the size and scaled down. The view (1.58.10, the owner: "a change of tactics, it's too unreadable — we'll take a snapshot of the oblast and show the whole oblast on the screenshot, but ONLY Kyiv oblast and the city of Kyiv"): Kyiv oblast and the city, Slavutych included (MapShot.extent, 3 % of margin, PAD) — 1.58.13, the owner: "the image is too zoomed out, ideally a bit more focus on where the activity is": framed on the activity (MapShot.view(m, others)): the target, its last six reported places, the other targets within 60 km of it (FOCUS_NEAR_KM) and Kyiv when it is within 50 km (KYIV_NEAR_KM), ×1.3 + 15 km each side, at least 110 km tall (FOCUS_MIN_KM, ~10 px a km), the picture's 0.91 width to height, kept inside the oblast's extent; activity wider than the oblast, the whole of it —, and nothing else: the oblasts around are neither drawn nor tinted, their towns not named, the satellite picture and the rivers are cut to the oblast's shape (_oblast_mask, kept), outside it the plain background (OUT_BG). (1.58–1.58.9 framed each target, 30–220 km round it, and 1.58.7 named villages — the owner: "far too many places named, it's unreadable"; both gone.) The data the pages draw: Kyiv oblast's raions and the city of static/kyiv-map.json, the rivers of map-ua.json, the towns of kyiv.html's TOWNS inside the oblast (Ukrainian names from i18n.js), the raion labels (RAION_LBL, RAION_ADJ + "р-н"), the silhouettes of static/glyphs.js (checked shape by shape by the tests), the colours of the Tactical page. The official alerts of the moment are painted as alertMap() paints them (alert_fill: the city; Kyiv oblast's raions — oblast-wide, by raion, a hromada's raion). The target, its last six reported places (a dotted path) and the other targets of the moment (the channel's own, ChannelPoster.cur, not a held one — smaller and fainter, under the one the post is about): the whole situation over the oblast in one picture. The silhouette is 2.6 px a unit (GLYPH_SC; 3.9 until the owner: "the Shahed is far too big"), the others 1.7 (OTHER_SC), the halo 36 px (HALO), the course ray 80 px; the names (1.58.11, the owner: "a bit smaller, please") Kyiv 22 px, a town 16, a raion 14 (BIG_PX, TOWN_PX, RAION_PX). to_pixel(lon, lat): where a point is in the picture (the tests). 1.58.6: a target placed only in a part of Kyiv oblast ("на сході Київщини") is posted as that part ("Схід Київщини", "📍 Точного місця в дописі немає — лише частина області"), drawn with a dashed circle of 35 km round the point (AREA_KM). The silhouette turns as orientOf() turns it; the course ray is drawn only with a stated course. The place the post named stands in a pill beside it — 1.58.7, the owner: "display altitude on the screenshot": with the height a post gave under it ("↕ ~150 м", "↕ низько", alt_text; the caption's words). A held target (1.58.7): no silhouette, halo or course at the place it is heading for — the places it was reported at before, and the pill. Town names that would cover the target are left out; the places it was reported at before are named beside their dots where there is room. Header: HEIMDALL · Київ · Київщина · the report's time; foot: the warning and the map's credits. Without Pillow (or the font) the channel posts its text alone. The image installs fonts-dejavu-core.

1.58.2, the owner: "il faudrait idéalement la vision map satellite". Under the map, the satellite picture: the app's own Sentinel-2 tiles (SAT, the MBTiles file on the volume, §10.1 — no map provider is asked, Esri's are never used here), at the zoom as sharp as the picture (~z9–10 for the whole oblast; 10–60 tiles), each tile placed by its own corners in the map's kilometres, then dimmed as the Tactical page dims its layer (#sat: brightness .62, saturate .82, contrast 1.04 — SAT_LOOK). The areas are not filled; the alerts' tint is drawn in one layer of its own (over the picture, two shapes meeting at an edge or an enclave would otherwise be tinted twice); the rivers are the picture's. The last 200 decoded tiles are kept (~40 MB; a bad tile is not). Credits: "Знімки: ESA WorldCover 2021, modified Copernicus Sentinel data (2021) · межі: OCHA, geoBoundaries · © OpenStreetMap". No file on the volume (a local run): the plain map, as before.

Limits: 18 calls a minute (BUDGET; Telegram's is ~20 messages a minute per channel), in this order: new targets (the most recent first), outcomes, all-clears, new reports (the longest-waiting first), "no new reports"; a 429's retry_after is obeyed. State: kv chan_posts — per target, the last message id (what the next post replies to), first / last report, what was drawn (signature), its area, how it ended, a trimmed copy of the mark for the closing line and the outcome's link (place, point, course, part of the oblast); 24 h, kept over a restart so a deploy neither reposts nor forgets a post. Health: the dashboard's official sources list "telegram channel (posting)" — working or failing (the last call to Telegram), targets / new reports / closed / all-clears counts.

Kill switch (1.58.3, the owner: "il faudrait faire un killswitch"): dashboard → Live, "Private Telegram channel — ON / OFF" (POST /api/admin/channel_switch 🔒, kv chan_on). Off: from its next pass (≤ 5 s) the channel makes no call at all — no post, no closing line, no all-clear; the bot and its secrets stay. On again: it carries on with what is reported from then — nothing first reported more than 5 min ago is posted late, and the targets gone meanwhile get their closing line. Without the secrets the block says "not set up" and how to set it up.

Setup (scripts/set-telegram-channel.bat, once): @BotFather /newbot → a private channel → the bot as an administrator ("Post messages", "Edit messages of others") → a message in the channel → the script: the token asked hidden and checked (getMe), the channel found from the bot's updates (getUpdates: a channel post or the bot's own addition) — 1.58.1: or, when the bot is told nothing (no update; another program reading its updates, a webhook), the channel's id typed in (Telegram Desktop's "Show Peer IDs in Profile", or the channel's address in web.telegram.org/a; with or without -100) and checked with getChat; what Telegram answered is shown in its own words, the token masked —, the bot's rights checked (getChatMember), a test message, then TG_BOT_TOKEN and TG_CHANNEL_ID into Fly's secrets through fly secrets import's input. To stop: fly secrets unset TG_BOT_TOKEN TG_CHANNEL_ID.

9d. The owner's Telegram bot, one to one (1.58.38)#

The owner, 20 October 2026: "we need to completely re-think the Telegram bot: instead, the bot will ask the user, one to one, what location they want to be alerted on — if they write Hnidyn, the bot sends them a message only when their place is mentioned, or a threat is detected nearby". His picks: it replaces the channel (§9c); it writes when a post names the place, or a target is reported within 10 km of it (NEAR_KM; 15 km until 1.58.40, the owner: "10 km instead of 15"); up to 4 places a user (MAX_PLACES). app/tgbot.py, started by main() when TG_BOT_TOKEN is set (the channel's bot, the same token); its rules are SAFETY.md's "The bot writes about a place because it was given one".

The conversation (private chats only; a group or a channel is ignored; at most 20 messages a chat a minute, IN_PER_MIN). Its first question is the language (1.58.39, the owner: "the bot must be in Ukrainian and English — it can ask the preferred language as its first question"): a new user's first message, whatever it is, gets "🇺🇦 Оберіть мову повідомлень / 🇬🇧 Choose the language of the messages" with two buttons (CHOOSE, LANG_KB); nothing is kept before the answer, and a place name or a command written before it waits for it (in memory, 15 min — a shared location never does: it is asked again). The answer turns the question into the explanation, then answers what waited. /start (and /help, or any unknown command) says what it does, the reminder ("from channel posts, not radar") and what it keeps. A name → Gazetteer.search: the gazetteer's places (geo.PLACES and geo.REGIONAL — the villages of the oblasts read regionally and Kyiv oblast's, ~21,000), without the raions and what is not a place to live (bridges, roads, squares, plants, reservoirs, airports: SKIP_RX); a name in any letters is compared through canon (transliterated, the letters people write differently made one: "Гнідин", "Гнидин", "Hnidyn", "Hnydin" are the same); the same name first (all its namesakes), else the names starting so (4 letters or more), else the nearest spellings (difflib, 0.8). One place: added. Several: buttons with their raion and oblast (a:<i>, valid 15 min) and "Cancel". A shared location → the 4 nearest places within 12 km as buttons; the point is used for that and dropped (not kept, logged or sent on). /places: the user's places as buttons, one press removes one. /lang: Українська / English (1.58.38 had French too). /stop: everything kept of the chat is deleted. A user who blocks the bot (my_chat_member "kicked", or a 403 on a send) is deleted the same way. A button's answer edits the message it was on. The menu of commands is set at start (setMyCommands, both languages); getMe gives the bot's @name for the dashboard.

What it keeps (BotStore, in the server's database): bot_chats (chat_id, lang, since, last) and bot_places (chat_id, key — the gazetteer's key —, added). No name, no username, no phone, no message text. kv bot_offset (the next update to read: a restart answers nothing twice) and bot_sent (what each chat was told of each target, 6 h: a restart does not tell it again). The logs carry counts only.

When it writes. Every 5 s, the marks as the pages see them (State.markers_now()), the targets as the push's proximity watch takes them (TgBot.live: a flying type — the drones, the missiles, Banderol, a KAB, a weapon not stated —, no outcome, not faded, not superseded, not the AI's alone, not waiting for its context), one per track (the tag), each compared with every place (TgBot.hit):

Why When The first line
here the post placed it at the user's place (its key; or its name, within 3 km, MENTION_KM) "📍 Допис назвав ваше місце: Гнідин"
dest a post said it is heading for it (target, or "→ Гнідин") — its oblast when the name is a namesake's "🎯 Курс на ваше місце: Гнідин (так у дописі)"
near placed at a town a post named, within 10 km — not an oblast, a raion, a part of an oblast, a place it is only heading for, nor a target held off the map "⚠️ ~8 км від Гнідин (від місця, яке назвав допис)"
region a ballistic or supersonic missile within geo.REGION_ALERT_KM (150 km) — no distance (the push's rule) "🚨 Балістична ракета — у вашому регіоні (Гнідин). Негайно в укриття."

Then the channel's lines, in the user's language: the type (the app's names) × count · the place a post gave; "where it is now: not said" for a place it is only heading for; a held target's line; the height a post gave (fresh, chanpost.stated_alt, with the time of the post that gave it when that was an earlier one); a link to /m. Never the channel. 1.58.40, the owner ("the place must not be cluttered"): no course line (a course is said only as "heading for your place", the dest line; the picture keeps the stated course), no clock (Telegram shows the time), no reminder — "from channel posts, not radar" is in the first message (/start) and the bot's profile (setMyDescription, setMyShortDescription, both languages, set at start); a weapon not stated but probably a drone reads "❔ Probably a drone" ("Ймовірно, дрон"), without "— type not stated" — never "Drone": the post did not say it (SAFETY.md, "A weapon nobody named"). The owner's example becomes: "⚠️ ~9 km from Гнідин (Hnidyn) (from the place the post named) / ❔ Probably a drone · Prolisky / ↕️ Height: ~2100 m (posted at 00:27) / 🗺 Heimdall map". A target in Kyiv or Kyiv oblast comes with the channel's picture (mapshot, §9c), drawn once and uploaded once — the other chats get Telegram's file id.

A target is told once per place; again, in reply to that message, only when it comes to the place (a better reason: near → dest → here, at once) or is 3 km closer (CLOSER_KM) at most every 3 min (UPDATE_GAP_S) — never a report more than 5 min old (FRESH_S). A chat gets one alert message every 20 s at most (CHAT_GAP_S; a missile waits not): targets that came meanwhile are one message, "⚠️ Нові цілі біля ваших місць: 7" and up to 5 lines (LIST_MAX). Its end, in reply: an outcome a post reported, linked as the Tactical page links it (chanpost.link_outcomes), in the app's words and without where it happened — except a shot down (1.58.40, the owner: "on va pas mettre les confirmed shot down"): the target ends and nothing is said (TgBot._close), not even "no new reports" later; or, no longer reported for 10 min (END_AFTER_S), "⏹ Нових дописів після HH:MM немає". It says nothing of an alert or an all-clear — Telegram never starts or ends one.

Sending: one queue (TgBot.out), at most 25 calls a second (RATE_PER_S); its answers to a user go first; a 429's retry_after is obeyed; an error leaving BotAPI names the method and Telegram's words, never the token. A second program reading the bot's updates (a webhook, another server: 409) is waited for, and the dashboard says so.

Health (1.58.54, the owner: "i keep seeing telegram bot failing"): each thread — updates, alerts, sending — runs one turn at a time (TgBot._turn): a turn that raises is kept in stats["down"][name] (how many in a row, since when, Telegram's words), the next turn that works removes it (_up); a message Telegram refused is kept as message until one goes. The dashboard's line is red only while one of them is serious (FAIL_N = 3 in a row, or FAIL_S = 2 min) or another reader holds the updates: "updates: getUpdates: network — URLError (timed out) — 3 failure(s) in a row since 14:05". Its "last check" is the last answered long poll (stats["polled"]); the last alert's time is in the detail. A network error names its cause from the error's class and number (chanpost.net_why: timed out, DNS, TLS, connection refused / reset, OSError 101), never its text. BotAPI waits for a long poll its timeout + 20 s; a message lost to the network goes once more 5 s later (_failed). Before: one failed call left the line red until a message went out.

Dashboard: Live → "Telegram bot — ON / OFF" (POST /api/admin/bot_switch 🔒, kv bot_on): off, no alert goes out from its next pass; it still answers its commands, so /stop always works. The block shows its address (t.me/@name), how many users and places, the alerts sent; the official sources list "telegram bot (private messages)". Counts only — no endpoint lists a chat or a place.

10. The two pages: Tactical and Light#

10.1 Tactical (/m)#

One self-contained kyiv.html: an SVG map in a km projection around Kyiv (proj/unproj, KX, KY), level-of-detail classes by zoom, OSM tiles under the vector layers below 150 km width, on every device, and inside Ukraine only (#tiles is clipped to the oblasts' outlines, #uaclip). (1.45 laid them under the whole country on a computer, neighbours included; zoomed out, the map was unreadable, and 1.45.1 took it back.)

The satellite layer (1.47). Layers → "Satellite imagery (Sentinel-2, 2021)", under everything, inside Ukraine only (#uaclip), at every zoom, on by default on every device (1.47.1; it was off on phones). It and the OSM streets are one choice or the other (1.47.1): checking one unchecks the other — under the satellite, zooming in fell back to the streets every time. A saved choice was moved to the satellite once (layers_v3); with the satellite chosen and no imagery on the server, the streets are drawn in its place (updateOsm). The imagery is the ESA WorldCover Sentinel-2 composite of 2021 — the median of a year of cloud-free Sentinel-2 pixels, 10 m, one processing for the whole country, so the tiles join without seams; CC BY 4.0, credited on the map whenever it is drawn (SAT_ATTR in #attrib: "© ESA WorldCover 2021 · contains modified Copernicus Sentinel data (2021)"). scripts/build_imagery.py reads it once from the public bucket (s3://esa-worldcover-s2, no account) and makes the WebP tiles that touch Ukraine: 1.47's first file, zoom 5–12 from the 20 m overview (about 21,000, ~130 MB); 1.48's, zoom 5–13 from the full 10 m (each 1° cell read in four pieces with a 16-pixel margin; 80,415 tiles, 406 MB — 59,736 of them at zoom 13, ~4 KB each). Closer than the file's deepest zoom the page enlarges its tiles. The open sea is transparent: the oblasts' outlines take in coastal waters (Odesa oblast reaches Zmiinyi) and the composite has sea pixels near the coast and none farther out, so the Black Sea ended in a staircase of tiles. Water (near-infrared below NIR_WATER) connected to the open Black Sea or the Sea of Azov — found once on a ~150 m mosaic of the region after an opening of ~1 km, so that no river joins it (sea_mask) — is left out, and the map's own background is the sea; the Dnipro, its reservoirs, the Dniester, the lakes stay in the picture. The server serves them itself (GET /tiles/sat/{z}/{x}/{y}.webp, SatTiles, SAT_TILES on the volume): no map provider learns which part of the country a reader looks at, and nothing is paid per tile. The tiles never change under one address — the page asks with the file's version (?v=SAT_V) and the answer is cached a year (immutable). 1.48: that version and the deepest zoom are the file's own (its metadata: version, maxzoom; SatTiles.info() → state.config.sat_v, sat_z), not numbers in the page — a page that asked for zoom 13 or version 2 before the new file was on the volume would have kept the old pictures under the new address for a year. The page offers the layer only when the server has the file (state.config.sat); a new file put in place is read within ten seconds, no restart. 1.54.7: a tile that could not be read just then (the file going in, a busy volume — SatTiles.lookup → (None, None)) is a 503 no-store; only a tile the file does not have is the 404 the browser keeps a day — in 1.47–1.54.6 both were that 404, and a tile asked for at the wrong moment stayed missing for a day. The page asks a satellite tile that did not come again after 2, 8 and 30 s (tileDone, &r=n — an address no kept failure answers); the service worker keeps only an answer that is a picture, and its store was emptied once (heimdall-tiles-2, the warm-up flag satwarm2) after a reader's map showed pale squares; the page says color-scheme: only light, so a browser's "dark theme for sites" never recolours it or its tiles. With the layer drawn the map is "tiled" like under the OSM streets (outlines over the picture, our own roads and rivers hidden). Darkened by mode (#sat filter: standard, day, night). Why this source and not a commercial one: a paid product cannot use the non-commercial tiers, and the commercial imagery services (Esri, Mapbox, Google) bill per tile and forbid keeping their tiles — a one-off build of open data costs nothing per reader.

The closest zooms: Esri World Imagery (1.48). 10 m Sentinel pixels show a town, not a street. From zoom 14 (tileZoom(), ~1:35,000) to 18 the satellite layer draws Esri World Imagery (Maxar, Earthstar Geographics and others; ArcGIS Location Platform, which allows a commercial app) over the Sentinel tiles (#esri, between #sat and #tiles, inside #uaclip, same filters), credited "Powered by Esri · Esri, Maxar, Earthstar Geographics, and the GIS User Community" whenever drawn (ESRI_ATTR). It is part of the satellite layer, not a layer of its own. The page asks this server (GET /tiles/esri/{z}/{x}/{y}.jpg); EsriTiles asks Esri with the owner's key (ESRI_KEY, set by scripts/set-esri-key.bat) — the key never reaches a browser and is never logged (the request address carries it). Nothing is kept on the server (Esri's terms forbid storing its tiles); the reader's browser keeps a tile as long as Esri's cache header says, private. Only tiles over Ukraine (ESRI_BBOX), zoom 14–18, are asked for. Every tile counts against the free tier (2 M a month, then paid), so they are counted and capped: esri_monthly_tiles a month (1.8 M), a tenth of it a day at most (one busy night cannot spend the month), 3,000 an hour for one reader (a salted hash of the address, in memory for the hour). The counts are kept in kv (esri_tiles:YYYY-MM, esri_tiles_day:YYYY-MM-DD, saved every 200 tiles) and shown on the dashboard (/api/health → imagery). Past a cap, or with no key, state.config.esri is false and the page draws the Sentinel picture alone; a refused key (HTTP 401/403/498/499, or Esri's 200-with-a-JSON-error) pauses the asking for 5 minutes. At most 12 requests to Esri at once. A large screen that would need more than 140 tiles at its zoom gets the zoom below (placeTiles(…, zmin)), for both layers.

The background: Satellite, Map or Plain (1.49). Layers → Background is one choice of three (radio buttons; LAYERS.sat / LAYERS.tiles): Satellite — Sentinel-2, Esri World Imagery from zoom 14, and over them Esri's roads and names from zoom 10 (#lbl, updateLbl: "arcgis/imagery/labels"); Map — Esri's street map at every zoom (#emap, updateMap: "arcgis/streets", "arcgis/streets-night" in the standard and night modes), or without Esri (no key, or past the cap) the OSM streets zoomed in, as before; Plain — our own map, nothing under it. Both Esri layers come from the Static Basemap Tiles service: 512-pixel PNG tiles (asked one zoom below the 256-pixel grid for the same sharpness — a quarter of the tiles), with the reader's language (language=en|uk|fr) and the worldview that draws Crimea as Ukraine (worldview=unitedStatesOfAmerica); a request refused with them (HTTP 400, or — 1.49.3 — 401/403, as the static tiles answered "401 Token Invalid" to a key World Imagery took) is asked once more without them, then (1.49.4) with the key in an Authorization: Bearer header instead of the address, Esri's other documented way: the way that works is remembered for the kind (EsriTiles.way, no_prefs), and only when none does is the service paused, the dashboard saying "(also without language / worldview, and with the key in a header)". Same proxy, key and caps as the imagery (/tiles/esri/{img|lbl|map|mapn}/…). 1.49.2: the two services are refused and paused apart (EsriTiles.down, refused: "img" for World Imagery, "st" for the Static Basemap Tiles) — in 1.49 a key without the static tiles' privilege (premium:user:staticbasemaptiles) paused the imagery too and the satellite went back to Sentinel's 10 m; the page is told each (state.config.esri, esri_st), and the log and the dashboard give Esri's own reason ("HTTP 403: You do not have permissions…"). On the page, when every Esri name or map tile of the view fails, or three in a row, it does without them for two minutes (esriSeen, stOK()): the Map falls back to the OSM streets and our own names come back over the imagery. A tile that fails never takes the older picture away (tileSweep waits for every new tile to have loaded; a server restart or a refusal leaves the previous zoom's picture in place). 1.58.14, a reader's phone (Oppo Reno 15 Pro), through the owner: "with the Map background it crashes — solid artifacts, and it falls out to the lock screen" (not reproduced: no such phone here). The older pictures kept are counted by their memory (placeTiles(…, px): 150 of 256 pixels, a quarter as many 512-pixel tiles of Esri's map and names — 37); the night mode dims the street map and the names tile by tile (#emap image, #lbl image: opacity .55) instead of filtering each whole group as one picture; and a page that died on the screen with the Map background opens next time on the satellite (run_v1 in localStorage: what background the opening shows and whether it ended well — hidden or left, visibilitychange / pagehide, it did), with a quiet toast (map_fell_t, map_fell_b: no tone) — the Map is one tap away again, never a crash at every opening. Where Esri's names are drawn (svg.lbld, svg.mapd) our own town and village names step aside — the same names twice, a few pixels apart, could not be read. Credited "Powered by Esri · Esri, TomTom, Garmin, FAO, NOAA, USGS, © OpenStreetMap contributors, and the GIS User Community".

The towns of every oblast (1.49). TOWNS (in the page) names every town of Kyiv oblast and the oblast and regional centres elsewhere; zoomed out over Kharkiv or Poltava oblast the map showed four names in a region the size of Belgium. static/towns-ua.json (scripts/build_towns.py, from GeoNames' Ukraine dumps, CC BY 4.0) adds the raion centres and every town of 10,000+ of every oblast that TOWNS does not name (352): Ukrainian names as build_places.py gives them, with the renamed ones (Хрустальний, not Красний Луч) and city sections GeoNames files as towns left out. Drawn in #twng (loadTownsUA): tier 2 (a centre, or 20,000+) up to 520 km of map (svg.wide), tier 1 up to 230 km (veryfar); layoutTownLabels ranks them after the regional centres, and a town whose name has no room loses its dot. 1.49.1: a layer of its own (Layers → "Main towns of every oblast", LAYERS.towns), on by default on a computer and off on a phone, where it was unreadable; on a phone tier 2 shows up to 230 km of map and tier 1 up to 90 km. The file is fetched only once the layer is on.

Zoom to a kilometre (1.48). setView / zoomAt stop at 1 km across (8 km before). Over a picture (svg.tiled) the oblast and raion names are light on a dark halo like the towns (paint-order:stroke), dark on a light halo in day mode — dark slate on darkened imagery could not be read.

The layers outside Kyiv oblast (1.45). The rivers, roads and "airports / plants" layers were drawn by hand for Kyiv oblast only, and "villages and small towns" had the core gazetteer's hundred-odd Kyiv-oblast places and a couple of dozen elsewhere — on an Odesa or Lviv view they did nothing. Now:

  • static/map-ua.json (scripts/build_mapdata.py, from Natural Earth 1:10m, public domain): the main rivers cut to Ukraine's oblasts (the Dnipro with its reservoirs, the Dniester, Southern Bug, Siverskyi Donets, Desna, Prypiat, Prut, Western Bug, Seim, Danube; names in the three languages), the first-rank roads outside Kyiv oblast, and by hand from GeoNames the civil airports of the oblast capitals and the large power plants (four nuclear, Burshtyn, the Dniester and Dnipro HPPs) — no military airfield. Each checked on the satellite picture in 1.57.7: all on their runway, dam or plant but the South Ukraine NPP, which GeoNames rounds to the minute (500 m off, in the cooling pond) — now on its reactors. Loaded with the map (drawUA); it replaces the hand-drawn Dnipro, Desna and Kyiv Reservoir lines (.river.hand), whose labels stay;
  • the villages layer fetches /api/places?villages=scope the first time it is turned on (loadVillages): the regional gazetteer's hromada centres and places of 1,000+ in the oblasts the map covers, about 1,800 names, drawn like the core villages and laid out by layoutTownLabels (which now tests the screen position before the style). 1.45.1: with the layer on, names show up to 300 km of map on a computer (vilfar; the small towns too — the veryfar rule applies only with the layer off) and up to 90 km on a phone; geo.villages lists hromada centres first, then by population, so the collision pass keeps those; a village whose name has no room loses its dot;
  • the 15 / 30 / 50 km rings are around the reader's oblast capital (OBL_CAP, drawRings, redrawn by setMyOblast); Kyiv for Kyiv oblast and for an occupied capital.

The screen (1.36) — the reader's own place first, the map second, everything else one tap away:

  • Header: the two logos, the LIGHT | TACTICAL switch, the bell (#bellbtn, a dot in the escalation level's colour) and More (#menubtn). No clock, no rotating ticker. 1.39: the switch (#top .vsw) takes the room between the logos and the icons (flex:1 1 auto, at most 340 px, centred), its two halves equal, its text sized with clamp() so "ПРОСТА / ТАКТИЧНА" or "LÉGÈRE / TACTIQUE" is never cut; under 380 px the NGO logo steps aside. Checked at 320 / 360 / 390 / 1440 px in the three languages.
  • The card "My place" (#mycard), in the header's flow — never over the map; syncTop() measures it: the four places as 44 px chips, each with its own official status as a dot (placeLevel(): R / Y over the place's raion or the city, or over its hromada (1.58.16: the place in it, or within 1.5 km — the band then names the hromada), H another hromada of its raion (or one whose outline is not here yet), G none, U not known — unitAt() finds the unit on the map's own outlines, on the phone); the official block (#kyiv) — with a place set it says that place's status ("Obolon · Kyiv · since 19:08 · official") and the duration, without one Kyiv's; the preventive strip (#tstrip), whose fresh ballistic or MiG-31K warning adds one sentence ("Little time — go to a shelter now, do not wait for the siren", mc_go_now, the guide's own rule) and a What to do button to /guide#w-ballistic; the channels block (#mych): the one target that matters most for the place — one a post says is heading for it, else the nearest within 30 km — with its silhouette, place, course as stated, age, the number of channels and the distance; with none, "Nothing reported within 30 km — that is not the same as safe"; with no place set, the nearest within 30 km of the centre or an invitation to set one (1.37: "nothing reported" is two lines of text, not a box); and the foot: the data's age ("updated 4 s ago", amber past 2 min, the block greyed past 10), the sources' dot, "unofficial · not radar" (a tap opens the full notice), What to do → and ▴.
  • Folding the card (1.37, phone only): ▴ folds it to one line (#mcmini) — the place's official status, its name, the alert's duration, the targets within 30 km — remembered on the phone (localStorage.card_fold). It opens by itself when the escalation level rises above the one it was folded at (FOLD.at, at least 1), or when a fresh preventive warning or a bridge bar appears that was not there when it was folded (warnKey()), and folds back once that is over (foldShut()). While a fresh warning is on, the line carries the warning itself, in its colour. A tap on the line opens the card. Measured at 360 × 760: about 210–230 px open on a quiet night, 56 px folded.
  • The map with its controls on the right, each 44 px: Layers (#laybtn), +, −, centre on my place, the replay, full screen. 1.54, the owner: the explosions layer of the last 24 hours (#impbtn, IMP_H, its key over the map and its report by place) "does not help much for now" — gone; the live outcomes stay on the map as before.
  • The drawer at the bottom, tabs Near · Feed · Alerts · Stats (1.54: the AI's bulletin over the Near list, §7.11), three heights (tab bar / half / most of the screen: a tap on a tab opens it to half, the handle cycles, a swipe moves it). While it is up the card folds to the same one line (a tap folds the drawer back).
  • Near (nearRows(), nearHTML()): every live target with its distance from the watched place (or the centre of Kyiv), in bands — the whole region (immediate types, no distance) / within 10 / 30 / 60 km / further — nearest first, inbound ones (as a post said) before others at the same distance; a tap opens the mark's sheet. A distance between two reported points is a fact; nothing is projected.
  • The feed has no row of category chips under each post any more ("on trial" and the Air Force summary keep a label): it adds, when the map placed a mark from the post, its distance from the watched place, its type and place (.meta2, redrawn when the marks change — feedSync()), and starts with "Do not publish air-defence or impact locations during an attack".
  • Panels (#bell, #menu, #lay; a sheet from the bottom on a phone, a drop-down under the header on a computer; outside tap or Escape closes): the bell — push, warn near my position, Kyiv bridges, sound, volume and test, vibration, as switches with a line saying what each does; More — places (priority place, my oblast), view (language, normal / day / night, blackout, Light), about (What to do, Share the app, how it works, support, Telegram, about, build tag); Layers — the map layers, Kyiv's 10 districts, EST (suspended since 1.45.1: hidden, always off), zoom out to oblast / region / Ukraine, and the legend behind a button.
  • Computer (≥ 1000 px): the card is a 360 px column on the left with the Near list under it, the map in the middle, the drawer on the right opening on the Feed. The column is a flex column of fixed height that scrolls: its blocks never shrink (#mycard>*{flex-shrink:0}, 1.39 — the "nothing reported" line was squeezed under the card's foot when a warning made the card tall).
  • The footer (1.39) is one component, footerHTML(o) in static/i18n.js, the same under the notice (#discfoot), under Near (#panelfoot), in the support and help sheets and on Light (#hfoot): the "free service" line with 🔗 Support (CONTACT.donate when the owner sets a donation link; until then it opens the support sheet with the addresses — above the notice when tapped from it, .sheet.top), Share the app, the © line with the two logos (o.logos:false on Light: no images on 2G), "Built by Dread92 · version · build", and 💬 Community & feedback (CONTACT.telegram) on its own line. CONTACT (addresses, Telegram, ngoSite, donate) moved to static/i18n.js with it.
  • Share the app (1.39; the footer and More → About): shareApp() — the phone's own share sheet (navigator.share), else the link copied (navigator.clipboard, else a hidden textarea), "Link copied" on the button for 2 s. The link is the app's canonical address and nothing else — never a place, a slot or a setting.
  • Every control of the header, the card, the map, the panels and the drawer can be hit over at least 44 × 44 px (the "unofficial" line and the drawer's tab through an invisible margin); the card, panels and drawer use no text under 12 px; secondary text --ink2 #b5b3ab, tertiary --ink3 #8f8d86 (at least 4.9:1 on the panels and the drawer in the normal mode).

Main parts:

  • render() — official colours (raions, oblasts, banner band, raion chips, alerts tab);
  • 1.56.3, a drone faces into Ukraine (the owner: "in the Chernihiv region the drones must always face into Ukraine when no direction is given — it is extremely unlikely that a drone goes back to Belarus or Russia"): in an INWARD oblast (Chernihiv, "25" → towards Kyiv, where its drones go — the patterns: south-west, south) a drone or an unstated weapon with no course is drawn facing that way (inward_face, the mark's face: orientOf, Light's markOrient) — the glyph only: no course ray, no heading in the card or the tooltip, no ETA, and the card says why (face_note); a course worked out from two reports that is more than INWARD_MAX_DEG (100°) off the way in is not kept (outward; a course a post stated always is). 1.58.8, south north of Kyiv (the owner, of two drones over the Kyiv reservoir, one drawn facing north: "for those drones here, take the habit by default to make them face south — it's the most likely direction they will go anyway"): in Kyiv oblast north of 50.60° N (SOUTH_FACE: from just north of Vyshhorod — the reservoir, Ivankiv, the north of Bucha and Brovary raions) such a drone faces south (face: 180, face_by: "south", the card's face_south), the glyph only, as above; nothing is dropped there. Elsewhere a mark with no course stays upright, as before;
  • 1.57.5, a mark faces the way its reports moved (the owner, a jet drone at Obolon drawn facing north while its reported path came down from above the reservoir: "the drone should face downward when it is possible to guess"): with no course stated, track_face gives face the bearing from the last earlier report of the mark's own track (history) at least TRACK_FACE_MIN_KM (1.5 km) away and at most TRACK_FACE_MAX_MIN (45 min) older, to this one, and face_by: "track"; it comes before the way into Ukraine (face_of: track_face, else inward_face), and never points back across the border (outward). The glyph only, as above — no ray, no heading, no ETA — and the card says why (face_track). The replay keeps its own rule: a stated course or upright;
  • 1.58.42, no course, no front (the owner, of a ballistic missile "в напрямку Києва" drawn nose-up on Kyiv: "how to rotate the missile like a direction it takes usually?" — picked: no direction at all; a usual side would be a trajectory no post stated): a missile, a Banderol or a guided bomb (NOFRONT in static/glyphs.js: the ballistic, missile, supersonic, Banderol and bomb silhouettes) whose post gave no course and that has no face is the glyph nofront — a disc (r 8.4) in its weapon's colour with a "?" (.nfq) — not an upright nose that reads as "heading north" (noFront(gk, m, stated): glyphKey, before the night arrows; Light's silKey; mapshot.no_front and the picture's _glyph). Its label, the card, the lists' icons (silSvg, Light's icoSvg / wpnKey) keep the weapon's silhouette, upright. It takes its silhouette, turned, once a post gives a course or its reports move;
  • 1.58.45, the threats in 3D (the owner: "could you make some little animated threejs icon for every threat that we have" — on the marks, the card and the sheet; and of a Banderol drawn as the nofront disc although its post named it: "if the target is correctly identified like here, then display a real logo … it needs to be a 3D model"). static/models3d.js (an ES module) builds each weapon from plain three.js shapes — Shahed, jet drone, Banderol, cruise missile, ballistic, Kh-22, KAB with its glide kit, fighter, Tu-95, and the ⚠ sign — on the silhouettes' outlines, ~24 units nose to tail in their 28-unit box, and sheet(key, view, colour, px, line) photographs it into a strip of frames with one offscreen WebGLRenderer (a blob: PNG; the outline in line). Two views: top (8 frames, 0.8 s — from above and 20° behind, nose up; the propellers turn, the flame flickers, a ballistic missile rolls) and spin (24 frames, 3.2 s — from 26° above, the model turning on itself; the sign rocks instead). The page's M3 block (kyiv.html): m3Start() imports the module only once there is a mark (tick), never with "save data" or without WebGL; m3Sheet() caches the strips, made one at a time a frame apart (m3Pump), and lets the renderer go (release()) when the queue is empty; m3Mark(b, m, gk) puts <g class="m3 glyph"><svg class="m3v"> (the window on the strip; a filter on it would stop Chromium clipping it) over the silhouette, which stays underneath, hidden (.mk.m3on .rot>:not(.m3)) — its computed fill and stroke, read with its blink paused, colour the model, so every state colour stays (m3sig: again only when the mark's classes or the look change); the <g> takes the glow, fade and blink rules (.glyph; the fill blink is an opacity step, m3bl). A mark with a course shows top, turned by orientOf like the silhouette; a nofront mark shows spin of its weapon (m3Key) — it never holds a heading; with reduced motion it stays the disc. Outcomes, night arrows, blackout (body.lite): 2D. The card (showTip) and the sheet (openSheet, the oblast list) show m3Ico(m, px) — the spin strip as an animated background in the type's colour (m3IcoCol); until it is made, the silhouette. Light, the guide and the channel's picture keep the silhouettes. 1.58.55 (the owner: "update the 3D models in the threats nearby and in the cheat sheet"): the Near list's rows and the card's top threat too — rowIco(m, col, px) = m3Ico(m, px, col) (a colour can be given: the row's pink-red for a target heading for the reader's place) or the silhouette; m3Pump counts the strips made (M3.gen, in CARD_SIG) and repaints the card and the list when its queue is empty (paintCard(true)). The guide: §10.5. The strategic-aviation and MiG-31K marks had no fill rule (black on the dark map): purple like the tactical ones;
  • tick() — marks: silhouettes per weapon (G, GLYPH_BY_TYPE from static/glyphs.js), turned only to a stated course (orientOf), uncertainty ring up to 25 km, 5-minute tail, labels (1.53.3: no off-screen edge indicators any more — the owner: the map has too much on it now; a target off the screen is in the Near list);
  • threatStrip() — the banner stack, coloured by officialLevel(); idleLine() when calm;
  • the four pins (SLOTS, localStorage only — 1.54.9, the owner: "numbered pins instead of Home / Work / Kids; let the user rename them"): "Pin 1" to "Pin 4" (slotName, sl_n), or the name their owner typed in the pin's sheet (slot_names, setSlotName in i18n.js, 24 characters, on this phone only; the storage keeps its old slot ids, so no place set before is lost). Each pin's sheet (a tap on an empty one, a long press on any): its name, a search, or "📍 Put it on the map" (armPin(true, id), then a tap). The active pin is drawn as a numbered pin (POI_G.pin) with its name. Zones near / 30 / 60 km (their labels sized in screen pixels, --k), the conditional ETA band (etaBand). 1.58.56: the near zone is the reader's — 10, 15 or 20 km (NEAR_R, NEAR_RS, localStorage near_r; 15 unless chosen — it was 10 for everybody; a reader: "15 km minimum, eventually 20 — a jet drone at 450–600 km/h covers 10 km in about a minute"), chosen in the setup's third step or under 🔔 (#nearrr, setNearR). It is ZONES[0], the Near list's first band, homeInfo's near, level 3 (escLevel), the l3 sound (sndFor), the card's "near" and the texts that said "10 km" (lt_z_near, z_near, lb_nearhome, n_home_b, snd_why take the km). Light reads the same key (its near zone, the radar's highlighted ring). The bot keeps its 10 km (§9d), the push its radius. 1.58.58 (the owner: "a way to set the pins' radius, as in the tutorial"; his pick: one a pin): each pin keeps its own — r in its record (SLOTS[id].r, localStorage only, beside its place); the pin on screen sets NEAR_R (pinR, nearSync in useSlot); a pin with none takes the last one chosen (near_r, NEAR_DEF), and keeps it once another is chosen (setNearR(r, slot) gives those the default first). setSlot stores a copy of the place with its radius — a pin moved keeps it. The bell's row is the pin on screen's ("Near zone · Pin 1", nz_r_pin). The pin's sheet has a "Near zone · 15 km ›" row (#hszone): it opens the tutorial's third screen for that pin (zoneOpen, SETUP.zone) — the map alone, the pin in the middle, its zone drawn as it is chosen around it (drawZones), 10 / 15 / 20 km, the approach signal; "Done" gives the sheet the radius (HS_PEND.r, saved with the pin), "Back" changes nothing; the map comes back as it was (ZONE_VB). Light reads the pin's own (nearR());
  • the first run, in three steps (1.58.56 — a reader, after his first minutes on a phone: "a banner with the current situation and an invitation to place a marker occupied almost the entire screen … there were difficulties placing the marker … the user must complete three steps: a language, a marker on the tactical map at any zoom, a minimap with the tracking zone"; the owner picked the three steps). For a phone with no pin and no setup_done: the notice is step 1 ("1/3" over it, #dstep; its language list; its button "I understand — next", d_ok_next; its "precise place" line hidden — discStep). Its button starts setupStart(): the map alone on the whole screen (setFull(true, true) — noFs: without the browser's full screen), body.setup (the status chip, the jar, the replay and full-screen buttons hidden), a card at the top (#setup .sutop) and the buttons at the bottom (.subar). Step 2, the place: a tap puts pin 1 (setupDrop, before the armed-pin test in pointerup; no press-and-hold pin meanwhile), another tap moves it — at whatever zoom the reader chose, the view untouched; or "Where I am now" (GPS, setupView(8)), or "Find an address" (the pin sheet's search; a street arms the exact spot as before). "Next" waits for a pin. Step 3, the zone: 10 / 15 / 20 km over the map, which centres the pin between the card and the buttons with the near circle about half of that space (setupView), the line on a jet drone's speed (su3_why), and the approach signal's switch. "Done" ends it (setupEnd(true): "📍 Pin 1 — near zone: 15 km. Change it any time under 🔔."); "Skip" at step 2 ends it too; setup_done keeps it from coming back. Nothing of it leaves the phone;
  • a pin dropped on the map: press and hold on open map (a ring fills while the finger stays; a drag only starts past HOLD_SLOP_PX = 10 screen px; the browser's long-press menu is suppressed) — the fourth pin, as before; or a pin's "on the map" (PIN_FOR), banner with Cancel, a tap where it goes (dropPin). A pin put on the map is named by its pin;
  • a pin on a street (1.56, §10.6): the sheet's search finds every street and village of the oblast chosen; a street sets the pin at the street's point (hsPick), zooms the map to the street (setView, the street's reach), and arms the pin (armPin(true, id, true): "Pin 2 is on the street — tap your exact spot to move it") — a tap moves it and it keeps the street's name (PIN_KEEP); on the plain background the street map is shown for it (not saved);
  • the place search above the keyboard (1.54.9, the owner: "on a phone the keyboard overlaps the suggestions"): the page asks to be resized by the keyboard (interactive-widget=resizes-content, Android); where it is not (iPhone), vvSync keeps the visual viewport in --kb / --vvh, the sheet stands on the keyboard, and while it is up (body.kbd) the list takes the room that is left and scrolls. Light's picker fills the visual viewport the same way;
  • a shared text never says where the reader is (1.54.9, the owner: "the location of our pin must NOT be shared"): markerText is what the post said — the kind, the place, the time, the height, the course, the channel — and no more; until 1.54.8 it added "19 km — Вишеньки", the distance to the reader's own pin and its place. The first push subscription sends the browser's coarse geolocation only, like every later one (pushSync) — no pin name, no raion;
  • the heat layer (1.34; Layers since 1.36, off at every opening — never remembered): where drones and unidentified targets were reported over 24 h / 7 days / 30 days (GET /api/drone_heat: the places the posts named, town and district level, the number of posts — shoot-downs and hits left out, never an oblast's centre or "Kyiv" alone), drawn as a density whose spread keeps its size on the screen, so it is layered by zoom (out: the corridors across the region; in: the villages and the Kyiv districts); the most-named places in view written on it and listed in its legend; under every mark. (1.54.3: Layers also has Path of a tapped target — the red dots of §7.8's night path, on by default, LAYERS.selpath, svg.no-selpath #selpath; the explosions layer's two switches went with it in 1.54.) 1.40: the colour is the number of reports — pale yellow, orange, red for the most (it was violet). heatGrid() adds each place's count around it on a grid a quarter of the screen's pixels (a smooth bump of 30 px); a spot's colour is √(its sum ÷ the scale), the scale being the busiest place of the period or the busiest spot in view if that is more — so a quiet corner never turns red because it is zoomed in on; a place with few reports is faint as well as yellow. 1.54.2, the owner: "the heat map is rather bad" — every village named once painted its own pale disc and a busy week spotted the whole region: below t = 0.18 (about 3 % of the scale) nothing is painted, and above it the colour comes in with the count, so what stands out is where the reports gather. The legend's ramp reads "1 … N+" and says "not live, not alerts"; while the layer is on, the official alert colours are a faint tint (svg:not(.no-heat), 1.54.2: no more outline — "the yellow outline of every oblast when zoomed out is bad": a night of yellow-level alerts drew a yellow mesh over every raion), so the two are never read as one colour. The legend folds and moves (1.54.2, the owner: "the card can't be moved or made smaller — a problem on a phone"): ▾ / ▸ or a tap on its title line folds it to "Heat map · 7 days" (localStorage.heatfold; folded at first on a phone), and its title line drags it anywhere over the map (heatpos, as a share of the map, kept below the header where the header runs over the map: heatMinTop). On a phone it starts at the top left, below the header. Dark map: blended with screen; day: multiply. (1.33's alert hours by oblast stay in the dashboard's Analytics.)
  • tabs: Near (1.36, above); Feed; Alerts (the live tally of what is on the map, then the official alerts); Stats — the Air Force's official figures only, weapon by weapon since 1.32 (§7.5): for 24 h / 7 d / 30 d (windows[…].af) a row per type — launched and ↓ shot down, ✓ when used without a number — and the hit locations; a key under the table, one sample per line (.aflg, 1.39: it was one sentence whose end fell under the screen's edge); then each summary (night / day) with chips and a link to its post (af_days). Each type wears the app's own silhouette (gIco(): the glyph of static/glyphs.js in a small svg, the same shape as on the map), not the phone's emoji; so do the threats in the Alerts tab and the preventive strip (thrIco()). (The Map tab became the Layers panel in 1.36.)
  • approach marks (drawn at the town they are heading to): a dashed course coming into the town from the side the post named, no ray or cone ahead, never dead-reckoned (Est.), no ETA; label "→ Vasylkiv from the NW"; on Light "heading for Vasylkiv — where it is now was not said";
  • mark labels never overlap: after each tick the label blocks are placed in screen pixels — which lines are drawn is read back from the CSS (getComputedStyle), candidates right / left / a line lower or higher, clear of other labels, other marks' glyphs and the map's edge; placed by priority (to a watched place, inbound, descending, others, outcomes); an ordinary label that fits nowhere loses its second line (nolbl2), then is left out (nolbl). The boxes (LBLBOX) hide the town names under them (layoutTownLabels);
