  • an oblast's badge counts the drones reported in it (1.58.20, the owner: "how many are reported within the oblast now, with a '~' to show it is an estimate"): OBLPLACED, filled in tick(), is per oblast the drones and jet drones the map has placed there (each mark's count, as the map's totals count them; a mark's oblast_uid; 1.58.24: never a stale one — the totals put those under "?"); for a drone kind the badge shows the larger of that and the oblast-wide reports' own number (est) — never their sum — written "~12", the line under it "reported in the oblast now" (obl_now) instead of "somewhere in the oblast" once placed ones are in it, the card the sentence obl_est. Outcomes keep "×3". The badges are still drawn only for an oblast with an oblast-wide report;
  • the oblast names step back zoomed out (1.58.20, the owner: "we don't need it zoomed out, or make it more discreet"): svg.far 11 px, svg.veryfar 9.5 px letter-spaced; over the satellite or the map half-toned (fill-opacity .62 / .48, a thinner outline); a lit name (a target in the oblast: an oblast-wide report, or — 1.58.24 — one placed at a town of it, OBLPLACED) stays readable; on a phone over the whole country (svg.dense, over 2.2 km a pixel) only the lit names are drawn;
  • less on the screen (1.58.19, the owner, Kyiv oblast on a phone: "too much information on the screen"): a raion's name is the least of the names — 9.5 px, letter-spaced, quieter (.rnlbl) — and layoutTownLabels places it last, leaving it out where a town, a target, a badge, the home's name (#homeg .hl) or the map's buttons (.mapctl) are; no town name is written under those two either, and a town whose name has no room loses its dot (the big cities keep theirs). With a place chosen (svg.hashome), its 10 / 30 / 60 km zones are the only rings: the oblast centre's 15 / 30 / 50 km rings (#ringc) and the pin's own 5 / 10 km circles are not drawn;
  • a mark's size follows the zoom (1.58.18, the owner: "ideally the icons scale with the zoom"): it used to keep one size on the screen at every zoom, so the whole country on a phone was covered by 26 px silhouettes. markZoom(KPX) gives MZ — 1 at 0.6 km a pixel (MZ_REF, Kyiv oblast on a phone), (MZ_REF/KPX)^0.4 above it down to 0.55 (the whole country), ^0.15 below it up to 1.15 (close in) — and MSK = KPX·MZ sizes the glyph, its course arrow, halo, count badge, tag, height and the labels' offset (the label layout's boxes too, the replay's glyphs, the Crimea Cossack). Texts shrink less (--mzt, never under 0.8: the height, the compact tag, the count's digits), the oblast badges as a whole by --mzc (never under 0.75; their shift apart stays in screen pixels), and the tap area never under 18 px (1.58.24: it was 14 zoomed out, against the 18 of before). On a computer the whole country is about 0.62;
  • the map's own pictograms are vector shapes, not the phone's emoji (1.39): the airfields, power plants and nuclear plants of the infrastructure layer (POIS, POI_G: a plane, a lightning bolt, a trefoil; shown zoomed in, hidden at night and in blackout) and the watched place's house — the same on every phone. 1.57.7: Kyiv oblast's POIS are at the plants' and airfields' own coordinates (Global Energy Monitor, Wikipedia), each checked on the satellite picture — they had been rounded to 0.01°, so ТЕЦ-6 stood 4.6 km off in Troieshchyna's flats, ТЕЦ-5 on the Stolychne highway and the Bila Tserkva airfield 10 km east, past the town; geo.PLACES carries the same points for ТЕЦ-5, ТЕЦ-6 and the two airports, so a post naming the plant marks the plant (tests/test_poi_places_1577.py). Each name (.poilbl) stands on its icon's point and is set aside in screen pixels by the CSS (--k), as the icon is sized: offset 2 km in map units, it was 200 px away at the street view — "Київська ТЕЦ-5" in the Dnipro;
  • the OSM tile credit sits small in the bottom corner; the Crimea Cossack is drawn in #obllbl, above the raion outlines, one path per colour;
  • display modes: normal, day (inverted, WCAG-checked), night (dim, arrows only), blackout (2G);
  • the notice (disclaimer, language, oblast): shown once per opening — sessionStorage.disc_seen, shared with the Light page, so switching Light ⇄ Tactical does not show it again; always reachable from the card's foot ("unofficial · not radar") and More → About. Changing the language inside it reloads the page and shows it again in the new language (pickLang(l, true) clears disc_seen); "I understand" is a full-width button, centred, and since 1.36 it stays at the bottom of the screen while the notice scrolls (.dok, position: sticky). 1.39: the owner's text — the title is "IMPORTANT INFORMATION" alone, centred, with the language under it; the first paragraph opens with "HEIMDALL is an unofficial volunteer service"; the footer component closes it.

10.2 Light (/light)#

One place, what concerns it, nothing else. Built to load and be read on 2G (page ≈ 24 KB gzipped, map data ≈ 41 KB, fetched after the status is on screen; test_light holds both under budget).

  • Status = the place's raion, from the official data only. The raion is found on the phone: point in polygon on light-map.json's raion outlines. A place within 1.5 km of a border belongs to both raions (the outlines are accurate to a few hundred metres; a house on the line gets either side's alert). 1.43: a place in Odesa, Kharkiv or Dnipro oblast fetches their raions then (light-map-x.json, loadLMX), so a reader there gets their raion's status without everyone else downloading those outlines. Outside the covered oblasts it falls back to the oblast (ukraine-map.json).
  • States: R / Y (alert over the place's unit, official level + reason; 1.58.16: or over the place's hromada — in it or within 1.5 km, placeStatus(uid, PR, pt), the hromada named), H (another hromada of the raion is under alert, named — or one whose outline is not here yet; the small map draws an alerted hromada on its own outline), P (raion unknown, part of the oblast), G (no alert over the unit — neighbours under alert are named, never painted on the place), U (no data). "Nothing reported" never says "safe".
  • Radar: 30 km (R_KM), rings at 10 / 20 / 30 km, over a local map: raions shaded by alert, oblast borders, Kyiv's districts, rivers, main roads, towns, villages and neighbourhoods (from /api/places), labels placed without overlap. Marks are the same silhouettes as the Tactical map (static/glyphs.js): turned to a course only when the post reported one (markOrient), upright with a "?" otherwise (1.58.42: a missile, Banderol or bomb with no course and no face is the nofront disc); the ⚠ for unknown types stays upright with a small orange course arrow; a dotted tail joins earlier reports; each mark carries its list number. The list shows the same silhouette beside each row.
  • List: number, type, count, distance, zone, "→ here" (stated course within 28° of the place), place, course, height when a post stated one (descending in crimson; a run of stated heights as "↓ 2,2 км → 1,6 км → 600 м" via altTrend() in i18n.js), age, ETA band. The height is also written beside the mark on the radar. Tap a row for what the post said (original + translation), the track so far, altitude when stated, the source and time.
  • The radar zooms by itself (1× = the 30 km disc, up to 4×): pinch, double-tap, the + / − / ⟲ buttons, ctrl + wheel. Only the map zooms — never the page; unzoomed, one finger still scrolls the page (touch-action: pan-y), zoomed in, one finger moves the map. Symbols and names sit in groups scaled by 1/zoom (--iz), so they keep their size; names are placed again (more of them) once the fingers stop. Nothing on it can be selected as text.
  • Tap a mark on the radar: the nearest mark within a finger's width (tapAt, RT) opens its row as a card over the part of the map it is not on (#rcard), kept current on every repaint; a tap on open map closes it.
  • Approaching (30–100 km): listed apart, only targets whose stated course points at the place.
  • 1.36: each of the four place chips carries its own official status as a dot (placeStatus() for that place); the colour legend is behind a "?" button; the footer is two buttons (What to do, the Tactical map); every control 44 px, no text under 13 px, the same secondary-text colours as Tactical.
  • 1.39: under the two buttons, the same footer component as Tactical (footerHTML({logos:false}), §10.1) — without the two logos, which would cost 20 KB on 2G; it is drawn again when the language changes.
  • Immediate types: a banner for the whole region, no distance, no countdown.
  • The radar's background (1.57.2, the owner: "the light version should show the satellite / map too"): plain by default; the layers button on the radar (#zbg) opens Background → Plain / Satellite / Map (the Tactical words, l_base, l_plain, l_sat, l_map; "uses more data" under the two pictures), kept on the phone (localStorage.lbg). Its code is static/lightmap.js (LT), loaded only when the button is pressed or a choice is kept (ldLT): the page stays light. The pictures are the Tactical map's — the server's own satellite tiles (/tiles/sat/, their version and deepest zoom from state.config) or Esri's dark street map (/tiles/esri/mapn/, in the reader's language) — in an SVG of their own under the radar (#rtile), clipped to the disc, its viewBox following the radar's at once (a MutationObserver on #radar's viewBox); the radar's own background is a veil over them (#rwrap.tl), and the source is credited under it (#rtattr). An option the server does not have is greyed. The tiles say the cell, not the place: the radar is centred on the place, so the tiles of exactly its view would say where the pin is. The tiles asked for (LT.tiles) cover the view as seen from the ~10 km cell of its centre — the grid the push stores (HOME_CELL_KM) — plus the cell's margin (7.5 km): every place of one cell asks for the same tiles. Fewer than 50 a view (MAX_TILES: one zoom less rather than more); zoom 10–11 for the whole disc, 12–13 zoomed in.

10.2b "Instrument" — the look (1.58.33)#

The owner, 16 Oct 2026, asked for a UX audit: "design alternatives so that it looks less like a default AI asset — the buttons, the gradients, the pin management in particular" — and approved all of it. The audit's diagnosis: a 10 px radius on everything (17 rules), an orange switch with a halo as the loudest object on the screen, every button at one weight, tinted translucent fills, emoji as icons, a row of four pins three of them empty, six map pebbles with the full-screen one in the alert's yellow. The direction, Instrument — a panel of instruments, not a SaaS dashboard — keeps the three faces and the official colours and changes the chrome:

  • Tokens. --r: 2px is the one radius (:root, kyiv.html); --line2: #3a414c the outline of a control. No shadow halo, no gradient but the data's (the heat legend, the ballistic sweep). Light keeps its own 2px.
  • Buttons, a hierarchy. .b-p primary: the one yellow of a screen, Condensed capitals — the warning strip's "what to do" (.tsbtn), "choose your place" (.mych.set, unless the strip's button already has the yellow: #mycard:has(.tsbtn) .mych.set), the update bar's "now" (#updGo, #pwaGo), the pin sheet's save. .tgl / .b-s secondary: a 1 px --line2 outline; "on" is a 3 px yellow rule inside (.tgl.on, .mi2.on, .lbopts label:has(:checked), .mapctl button.on). .b-t text: close, cancel. .b-dgr danger: a red outline, never a flat. The card's "what to do" link (.mcwhat) is in ink, Condensed, no longer a blue link.
  • The switch (#top .vsw, Light .sw): two tabs on a hairline, each with its one-line meaning (sw_light_b, sw_tac_b), the current one in ink; the yellow 2 px line under it is the thumb that slides (::before, the 1.40 motion kept). No orange, no halo, no chevron. The drawer's tabs, its count badge (ink, not orange) and the layer boxes take the same yellow: one vocabulary for "chosen".
  • The map's controls (.mapctl): one bar, the buttons separated by a hairline, the active layer by the yellow rule, "centre on my place" an SVG reticle (it was the ◎ character), the full-screen button in ink.
  • The band (.band): the red and the yellow are the government app's own flats, square, a darker rule on the left; "no alert" is a green rule and a green word on the panel (.band.N), never a tinted box; the state in Condensed 800 19 px, the duration in tabular mono. Light's .status the same.
  • The rows (.mych): a list with hairlines, not a card a row; a 3 px rule inside carries the meaning (orange: a target, red: near, blue: a shelter), the glyph in a fixed 36 px column, the distance in Condensed 800 24 px with its unit small in mono.
  • The pins. The row (paintSlots) shows the pins that exist and a "+" while one of the four is free; the current one is underlined like a tab; a tap on it opens its sheet (openPinSheet(id, 'edit')), on another watches it, on "+" starts a new one — the long press that opened the sheet was written nowhere. A pin's label (pinLabel): the name its owner gave it, else the town or district; a street or a dropped point stays "Pin N" — the 1.36 rule: no address on the screen for whoever stands behind. The sheet (#homesheet) is three steps: where (#hs-where: "put it on the map" first, "where I am now" — the phone's geolocation, never sent — "find an address", and six places people pick often, HS_FREQ), find (#hs-find: the search, the list only once something is typed — renderHomeList, Light's listPick), name (#hs-name: the place with "change", the name filled in with the town, four suggestions, cancel / save; a pin that exists adds "delete" with an inline confirmation, #homeconf). Nothing is kept before "save" (HS_PEND); "on the map" arms the map and brings the sheet back for the name once the pin is dropped (PIN_NAME). On the map (drawHome) a pin is a reticle in ink — the current one white with its name and the official status of its place as a dot, the others grey — not the orange drop of every map SDK (orange is "approaching" here); a tap on one switches or opens it.
  • Icons. The interface's own strings carry no colour emoji any more (📍 🔗 🔥 🚀 🟢 🚨 …): the warning strip and a mark's tip put the weapon's SVG silhouette (thrIco) before the words; the monochrome dingbats (✕ ✸ ✓ ⚑ ⚠) stay. The channels' posts keep theirs — they are the channels' words. Notifications keep theirs: they are the phone's.
  • The Banderol (the owner: "the icon is really ugly"): drawn again in glyphs.js as the S8000 from above — a slim body, straight wings swept a touch, the turbojet block on its back, two tail fins, and a flame like the jet drone's (.mk.banderol_missiles .jetfx); mapshot.GLYPHS mirrors it for the channel's pictures.
  • The legacy /desktop page: flat cards with a coloured rule, no gradient.

The Light page's budget rose by 0.3 KB for the lot (test_light.py). Every rule has its Day, Night and Blackout version (body.m-day, body.m-night, body.lite).

10.3 The switch#

Both pages carry the LIGHT | TACTICAL switch (UK: ПРОСТА | ТАКТИЧНА, FR: LÉGÈRE | TACTIQUE): two tabs with a one-line meaning each, the yellow line under the current one (1.58.33, §10.2b; until then a two-segment orange pill).

1.40: a toggle, not a reload. The yellow line is one thumb (::before); a tap on the other half (a[data-sw], handled in static/i18n.js, swGo()) slides it across in 0.2 s, then opens the other page. Three things keep the screen from going blank in between:

  • cross-document view transitions (@view-transition{navigation:auto} in Tactical, Light and the guide): the browser keeps the old page on screen until the new one has painted, then fades one into the other (0.16 s out, 0.24 s in, a 6 px rise). Chrome and Edge 126+, Safari 18.2+; elsewhere an ordinary navigation.
  • speculation rules: Tactical prerenders /light on hover or press (moderate), Light prerenders /m only on the press (conservative — Light is the 2G page); the page is usually ready when the thumb has finished.
  • a prerendered page is not a visit until it is shown: it opens no live line and sends no ping (whenShown() waits for prerenderingchange), and the server does not count a load that carries Sec-Purpose: prefetch.

Nothing is carried between the two: both read the same places, language and settings from the phone. With "reduce motion" the thumb does not slide and the pages do not fade.

1.42: flipping, not loading. When the other version is the page just before or just after this one in the history — the reader came from it — swNav() goes back (or forward) to it instead of loading it again (navigation.back() / forward(), only where the Navigation API can say which page that entry is; the paths /, /k, /kyiv count as /m, /l as /light). The browser keeps the page it left in memory (back/forward cache), so after the first switch both versions flip at once, as they were left. To be kept there a page must hold no open connection: both close their live line on pagehide (when the page is being kept) and open it again on pageshow (persisted) — Tactical with connect() and a pulse(true), Light with connectLive() and refresh(true) — so what changed meanwhile arrives at once. The pages are served no-cache with an ETag, never no-store, which would keep them out of that memory too.

10.3e The map first on a phone (1.42.1)#

On 7 Oct 2026 at 01:20 a ballistic warning, the admin's message and a mark's sheet left the map 260 px of an 860 px phone screen — 7 px with the sheet open. On a phone (max-width:999px): the slots are 40 px; the warning's "what to do" sits beside its sentence (.tsrow); the caveat under "nothing within 30 km" steps aside while a warning shows; the card's foot is one line; the admin's message keeps to two lines (a tap opens it whole); the toast puts itself away on a tap. A mark's sheet (.sheet.mark) takes the lower half (52 vh, its buttons pinned at the foot) while the card folds to its one line (body.shmk in miniWanted()) and the admin's message steps aside. Measured: 364 px of map with the warning and the message, 460 px with a sheet open.

A ballistic report while no official alert covers the place (officialLevel() null, and not an official threat) says so — "Ballistic threat reported — no official alert here yet" — asks the reader to be ready and to go the moment the siren sounds (not "do not wait for the siren"), and turns grey after 4 minutes (TS_FRESH_BAL_UNOFF, 8 for the rest). The in-app toast says the same, one tone level lower. With an official alert over the place, the full warning at once. A mark drawn at the town it is heading for (approach) is "heading for Kyiv — where it is now was not given" in its sheet, with no distance and no zone (it said "Kyiv · 0 km from Kyiv · near zone, up to 10 km").

10.3f Closing and folding at a glance (1.58.50)#

The owner, on 22 Oct at 21:01 — an official alert, a ballistic warning in the card and the map's banner ("cruise missiles in your region") on one phone screen: "when there are several banners, how to close them must be obvious at once, so nobody has to look for it". The map's banner (#toast, one function now: toastShow(colour, title, body, ms) for notify, toastQuiet and shNoPin) has a ✕ at its top right (.tx, 40 px, btn_close); a tap anywhere on it still closes it, and it still goes by itself (7 s, 12 s for the quiet one). The admin's message and the "moved" line keep their ✕, now 40 px and as visible. The card's ▴ is a word with its arrow: "Згорнути" / "Collapse" / "Réduire" (mc_fold_s; its label stays mc_fold). The card is folded, never closed: it carries the official status.

Two faults found on the way. The folded line under a warning was a red bar with no words: it read the strip's first text, and the strip has begun with the threat's icon since the icons came — the strip now keeps its words in data-line (tstrip.dataset.line), which the line and the card's signature read. And in the day look, body.m-day .band{background:#fff} painted over the official alert's red, leaving "ПОВІТРЯНА ТРИВОГА" white on white (and the yellow white): the red and yellow bands keep their colours in daylight (not in the blackout look, which has its own); the "Near" count, dark on dark there, is white.

10.3d Daylight and night (1.42)#

The display modes recolour everything, not only the map: the bottom sheets (how it works, support, the place pickers, a mark's details), the buttons inside the panels, the cards' links, the statistics, the live-count chips and the bars. Daylight swaps the colours made for a dark screen — pale blue links, mint green, pale orange, yellow, pale pink — for ones that read on white (#14459c, #0d6b3f, #b8480a, #7a5f00, #b3140d, all ≥ 4.5:1); night's faintest grey (--ink3) is #566a80, 3.6:1 on black (it was 2.3:1). Checked by a contrast scan of every text on every panel, sheet, tab and bar in the four modes (tests/test_replay.py keeps the key values).

10.3b Motion (1.40)#

Short, eased, never bouncy, and none in blackout mode or with "reduce motion" (CALM()): a panel (bell, More, Layers) rises in on a phone and drops in on a computer (0.26 s), its rows following 14 ms apart, and leaves in 0.16 s (hidePanel() sets hidden after the animation); one orange line slides under the drawer's tabs to the chosen one (tabInd()); a tab's content fades in; switches move their knob with a slight ease; buttons give a little under the finger (scale(.95)); sheets and the drawer use the same easing. Light: the place picker and the target card slide in.

10.3c The replay (1.42)#

A past window on the Tactical map, like a weather-radar loop: the ⟲ button on the map (and "Replay — the last 7 days" in Layers) opens a bar at the foot of the map with the window (the last 24 h, or one of the last seven nights, each 12:00 → 12:00 Kyiv time), a strip of the reports per quarter-hour with the time Kyiv city or oblast was under an official alert, a slider, −10′ / ▶ / +10′ and the speed (2, 10 or 30 minutes of replay per second). Keys: space, ← / → (a minute), Esc. It starts ten minutes before the attack (the first ten minutes with at least a quarter of the busiest ten minutes' reports). /m#replay opens it at once (for a presentation). static/replay.js is downloaded only when it is opened. 1.54.7 (the owner: "an obvious loading spinner — people think the feature simply does not work"): while a window loads (up to half a minute the first time), a spinner turns over the map with the words (#rpwait, role=status, "the night is being read again from its posts") and in the bar, the slider shimmers and the controls wait greyed out (#rpbar.loading, waiting()).

  • The alerts: GET /api/replay rebuilds them as intervals from the start / end events (§13) — the alerts table cannot answer "what was on at 02:14": it keeps one row per region and alert type, and a region's second alert overwrites its first. A restart loses the alerts the process held without writing an end (the first reading after it writes a start for everything still on, and nothing for what ended while it was down), so a restart — recorded in boots since 1.41, and before that seen as a start for a region already on — closes every interval not started again within five minutes, at the restart. The page paints a moment's alerts with the live map's own function (alertMap() / applyAlertMap()), only when they change; the live colours are not painted while it is on.
  • The targets: the stored posts of the window are read with today's rules — _marks_from_posts() and _chain(), the live map's own functions — so a reading fixed since is fixed in the replay too; scope as on the map; no outcome. Each target stands where the latest post before that moment put it, until the next post moved it or its time ran out (the live clocks: a drone fades after 5 min and goes at 15, a missile at 12, an unspecified sign at 5), with its reported positions of the last 20 minutes as a trail — a leg longer than the target could have flown between the two posts is two targets joined, and the trail stops there. Nothing is extrapolated; a course is drawn only where a post stated one.
  • While it is on nothing live and nothing personal is on the map — no live mark, history, heat, bridge, watched place, pin or ring (for a screen shared in a presentation); the band and the card at the top stay live (the card folds to its line unless something near matters). The bar says "official alerts, and the targets where the channels' posts put them — not radar".
  • Server: a window of at most 26 h within the last 7 days, in whole minutes; built once an hour for a window in the past, once a minute for one that reaches now; twelve windows kept (replay_cached()). About 12 kB gzipped a day.

10.4 Languages#

static/i18n.js: every string in EN / UK / FR (t(), tn() for plurals), place, raion, river and district names, OBL_N / oblFull(). A test fails when a key is missing in one language. See I18N.md. The language is a drop-down on both pages (langSelect(): the Tactical More panel and notice, the Light header — flag and code only — and notice); pickLang() stores it and reloads.

10.5 The guide (/guide, 1.35)#

What to do, weapon by weapon. One self-contained static/guide.html; its content is the JSON block #gd (uk / en / fr, the same shape in each — tests/test_guide.py compares them key by key and list by list) and the sources in #gsrc ([publisher, title, URL]). The script draws it, so the page reads ?lang= (not stored), then localStorage.lang (shared with the two pages; the УКР / EN / FR buttons store it), then the phone's language.

The way back (1.57.4, the owner: "in the what to do section we need a back button"): the header is sticky and starts with "← Back" (#back, back in each language, 44 px): to the app page the reader came from (history.back() when the referrer is one of ours), to Light when it was Light, else to the map (/m) — the guide opened from a link or a notification. Not printed.

  • Five rules that always apply; weapon cards (shahed, jet, cruise, ballistic, kab, debris): the usual warning time as a chip, how you notice it, what to do — with the map's own silhouette and colour (static/glyphs.js, ICON in the page) — 1.58.55: and the weapon turning on itself, as in the app (static/threats3d.webp: models3d.js's spin of drone, jetdrone, missile, ballistic, bomb, a row each, 24 frames of 96 px, ~140 KB; M3G in the page, scripts/render_models.js): fetched as an image once the page has loaded (never with "save data"), played by CSS (.ic.m3, m3g, one row by --y); until then, in print and for debris the silhouette; with reduced motion its first frame. The worker keeps it once fetched (KEEP); situations (car — the bridges, out, home, kids, after, rad); the emergency numbers as tel: links; the sources, with what is not taken from them said.
  • Every rule comes from the listed official guidance (ДСНС, National Police, Ministry of Health, Poltava ОВА) — SAFETY.md. The checked date is in the footer.
  • Offline: the page registers sw.js; the worker stores /guide, glyphs.js and the logo at install and answers those paths network first, the stored copy only when the network fails. "Saved on this phone" appears only once caches.match('/guide') finds it.
  • 1.41: the two pages too. Tactical and Light register the same worker on every phone (not only when push is on); it keeps /m, /light, i18n.js, live.js, glyphs.js, the logos, light-map.json, kyiv-districts.json and the manifests (heimdall-shell-N), network first, and answers from the stored copy when the network fails or the server answers 5xx (a deploy, a restart, an overloaded host). The app then opens and says so — Tactical: a red bar at the top after 15 s without the server ("No connection to Heimdall — retrying; the sirens and «Повітряна тривога» do not depend on Heimdall"), and "connecting…" instead of "updated 5 s ago" before anything arrived; Light: its offline line after 15 s (was 60). No data is ever stored (/api/…): an old alert status shown as current would be worse than none.
  • Print: @media print — black on white, no buttons, cards kept whole, three columns; two A4 pages in each language.
  • Linked from the Tactical More panel (About, m_guide), the card (What to do →, and the ballistic warning's button), the top of the help sheet (.gdl), the Light footer (#gdl) and the nuclear-event banner (/guide#rad, static/nuke.js). The page asks the server for nothing.

10.5b A way back from everything (1.57.4)#

The owner, the shelter list open on his phone: "there is currently no way to quit this popup" — "overall we need clear return-to-home buttons in the app, simple and accessible".

  • Every sheet has its ✕ (.shx, the first thing in #sheet and #homesheet): top right, sticky while the sheet scrolls, 44 px, labelled in the reader's language (btn_close). The shelter sheets end with a Close button too (SH_CLOSE), like every other sheet.
  • The phone's back button closes what is open — a target's card, the shelters, the place sheet, the bell, more or layers panel — instead of leaving the app: while one is open the page holds one history entry (backSync, watching their class / hidden); the back button pops it and closes them all (closeAll); closed by its ✕, a tap or Escape, the entry is taken back 300 ms later (a card replaced by another stays one entry). Escape closes it all.
  • Light: the target's card had its ✕ (#rcx); the open shelter list now has one too (SH.light).
  • The guide: "← Back" (§10.5).

10.6 The streets and villages of Ukraine (1.56)#

The owner (10 Oct 2026): "for the district / village etc. it would be good to have a complete registry of the streets within the city / oblast so the user can set the pin accurately" — then "all the towns and streets of Ukraine".

  • The data: OpenStreetMap, © OpenStreetMap contributors, ODbL 1.0 (static/streets/LICENSE.md; credited in the list wherever a street or a village from it is shown). scripts/build_streets.py DIR reads the 27 regional extracts of download.openstreetmap.fr (EXTRACTS: one per oblast of the app, Kyiv and Sevastopol included; ~1 GB, not in git) with pyosmium and shapely — build-time tools, not the server's — and writes one file per oblast, static/streets/<uid>.json (format 1, described in the script): the settlements (every place = city / town / village / hamlet named in OSM inside the oblast's boundary — admin_level 4 —, streets or not, with its raion, admin_level 6) and the streets (every named way of a street's kind: residential, living street, the primary to tertiary roads, unclassified, pedestrian; a service road or a track only when its name is a street's — STREET_WORD —; never a road between towns, "Київ — Чоп", ROAD_NAME). A street belongs to the settlement whose boundary its middle is in (admin_level 9 — Ukraine's settlement boundaries — or a place area), or else to the nearest settlement node within that kind's reach (REACH_KM: 10 km for a city, 4 a town, 2 a village, 1 a hamlet); ways of one name in one settlement are one street: its point (the vertex nearest their centre), how far its ways reach, another name it is known by (old_name — the renamed streets: "Московський проспект" finds Bandera Avenue — or the Russian name where a name:uk was used, in Crimea), and in Kyiv its district (admin_level 10). The Ukrainian name (name:uk) first. 2026-10-10: 30,215 settlements, 230,814 streets; 15.8 MB, 3.9 MB gzipped (the server gzips them once, _GZ); Kyiv 2,710 streets (51 KB on the wire), Kyiv oblast 18,051 in 1,195 settlements (283 KB).
  • The search, on the phone (static/streets.js, HS): the Tactical sheet (#homesheet) and Light's picker (#pick) load it when they open (hsOpen), and it adds a "Streets & villages in" choice under the search — "Kyiv and Kyiv oblast" (31,14), or any oblast (localStorage.hs_obl; the oblast of the pin being set first). It downloads that oblast's list once (HS.load), and after the page's own places it lists the streets (up to 50) and the settlements the page does not already have (up to 20). Every word typed starts a word of the street or of its settlement, one at least of the street: "шевченка бровари" is Shevchenko Street in Brovary; an apostrophe or none (лук'янівка, лукянівка); Latin typed against the Cyrillic names (SK: khreshchatyk). The settlement named first, then the bigger settlement, Kyiv before its oblast, the shorter name. A row: the street, its old name (uk) or its transliteration (en, fr), its settlement and (a village) its raion or (Kyiv) its district.
  • What is typed never leaves the phone: the one request is the oblast's file — the server sees only which oblast's list was opened, the way it sees the map tiles a phone shows.

10.7 The shelters Kyiv city lists (1.57)#

The owner's colleague: "a layer of the official shelters — only the government's data: metro, official shelters, underpasses — baked into the app so the nearest one is found offline; no parking garages". The owner chose every shelter the city lists, each labelled with what it is — the city's own list includes 2,882 "simplest shelters" (basements, parkings), and keeping only ~520 would put the nearest one 1–2 km away for many pins.

  • The data: the city's shelter map, https://gis.kyivcity.gov.ua/shelter/ (KMDA, ІАС «Майно»), an ArcGIS web app whose layer public_protection/Укриття_редактор (MapServer/0) holds the shelters; the map shows those with actual = 1 (4,192 on 11 Oct 2026). The service states no licence: the source is credited on every list, and the owner asks KMDA. Its firewall refuses a script, so the owner saves the list from a browser — the query …/MapServer/0/query?where=actual%3D1&outFields=guid,type,kind,type_building,address,district,working_time,invalid,tel,description&returnGeometry=true&outSR=4326&f=json — and scripts/build_shelters.py FILE turns it into static/shelters.json (format 1, described in the script): [lon, lat, category, kind, ramp, address, access, phone, note], 360 KB, 69 KB gzipped. Nothing is fetched by the server; Heimdall never calls the city's service.
  • Category and kind (classify) from the city's "Тип укриття" and "Вид укриття": metro (stations, entrances), underpass, shelter (the type "Укриття" — a civil-defence structure — and the anti-radiation ones, ПРУ) — the three the pages call the strong kinds (SH.STRONG) —, dual (dual-use structures: parkings, basements, a ground floor), simple (the simplest shelters) and mobile (modular ones). A pair the rules do not know is a simplest shelter of no stated kind, never ranked higher than the city ranked it, and the build lists it. Several shelters of one category at one point and address (374 points) are one point — the one anybody may enter. 85 metro entrances are written "Вхід в станцію метро" with no station: the station within 350 m is named, as "біля ст. …" (_name_entrances).
  • Who may come in (_access), from the city's "Режим роботи" and note, never guessed: round the clock; the hours where given ("10:00–18:00" — also when the hours say round the clock and the note says otherwise); s a school's, for its pupils and staff during the school day (841); S for them only (144); r under repair (7); ? not stated. S and r are drawn dimmed and never offered as "the nearest" (SH.closed). A ramp: "з пандусом", or "В наявності".
  • On the phone (static/shelters.js, SH, its words in its own three languages): loaded by the Tactical page for a pin in Kyiv city while an alert or a warning concerns it (shCard: LEVEL >= 1, the place's status not green, or the layer on) or when the layer is on (shLoad), by Light under an alert (lShel → SH.light, which also carries Light's look, so the page stays light). The service worker keeps both files (FAST). One radius, 5 km round the pin (SH.RADIUS_KM; 1.57.1, the owner: "shelters around me, a 5 km radius based on the pin"), for the card, the list and the layer. The card (#mysh): the nearest of the strong kinds, its address and access, and the nearest of any kind when nearer (SH.line). A tap — "Shelters around me": the three nearest of the strong kinds and the three nearest of the others — basements, parkings — (openShelters, SH.listHTML, SH.near with strong / weak), one row per address, each with its access and ramp; a tap on one shows it on the map (shShow). The layer ("Shelters around me (5 km)": LAYERS.shelters, off by default; drawShelters, #shelg): the shelters within 5 km of the pin (SHNEAR, worked out again when the pin changes — drawHome), inside a dashed circle (.shr); a square per shelter — filled for the strong kinds, ringed for the pin's nearest, dimmed for a school's own or one under repair (SH.near with all) —, the strong kinds from 120 km across and every kind from 8 km (1,500 at most; 893 within 5 km of Pechersk); no pin in Kyiv city, no layer, and switching it on says so (shNoPin). A tap on a square opens its card (openShelter, SH.oneHTML: address, access, distance from the pin, phone, the city's note).
  • What it never says: a distance is a straight line ("відстань по прямій") — never a route, a walking time or a time to get there; a place is never "safe" — every list ends with "Heimdall does not check shelters: one may be closed, locked or full", the source and its date, and "Outside is dangerous during an attack. If you cannot get there in time: two walls between you and the street, away from windows." No shelter is offered outside Kyiv city.

10.8 The Android app (1.57.6)#

The owner: "an app we can really install on the phone, an APK, without going through the Play Store first — in a folder of its own, to keep things clear". It is heimdall-android, a separate folder (not in this repository): a Trusted Web Activity built on Google's androidbrowserhelper — the app shows https://heimdall.com.ua/m (1.58.34; the first build showed kyiv-air-watch-gb.fly.dev/m) full screen through the phone's Chrome, with its notifications (the library's DelegationService; Android 13+ asks the reader once), its icon, a splash, and two shortcuts (Light, What to do). Every release of this web app reaches the Android app at once; a new APK is needed only for its name, icon, address or Android behaviour. Identifier com.blackflamestudio.heimdall; Android 7 (API 24) and later; compileSdk/targetSdk 36.

  • The signing key is the owner's: made on his computer by heimdall-android\scripts\2-make-key.bat (keytool asks for the password; the key lives in %USERPROFILE%\.heimdall, outside both folders), used by 3-build-apk.bat (apksigner asks for the password, nothing stored). A test build (…heimdall.test, "Heimdall (test)", the debug key) installs beside it.
  • The site says the app is its own: GET /.well-known/assetlinks.json (android_asset_links) names the app and the fingerprint(s) in ANDROID_CERT_SHA256 (scripts/set-android-key.bat, a Fly secret like the others — public, but set by the owner). Without it, Android opens the app with an address bar on top (a Custom Tab), as for the test build.
  • No pin and no setting changes hands: the app is the web app, its storage the browser's.

10.9 As of when, and a raion named as one (1.57.8)#

  • The line under the header (#asofbar), from the government app's header the owner showed ("Станом на: 12 жовтня о 20:27 (1692 день) · Оновлення через 05 с · ⟳"): "Станом на 12 жовтня о 20:27 · 1692-й день війни" — the time of the server's last word, Kyiv's date and time (asOf), and the day of the full-scale war counted in Kyiv as the government app counts it, 24 February 2022 being day 1 (warDay); then "Наживо — зміни надходять одразу" while the live line beats, or "Оновлення через 05 с" counted down to the next ask without it (nextPulse); the stale and offline warnings as before. ⟳ (#rfr) asks everything again at once (lastFull=0; pulse(true)) and opens the live line again if it had dropped; it turns while that is under way (still, with reduced motion). In the header, so it shows with the card folded too; it replaces the card's "updated 5 s ago" (mc_upd_s, mc_upd_m removed). Light keeps its own "updated" line (its budget, §10.2).
  • A raion is named as a raion. The owner: "it's the Vyshhorod district here, not the Vyshhorod city (which is rightfully placed correctly on the map)". Kyiv oblast's seven raions were written by their centre town alone — "ВИШГОРОД" in the middle of the raion (RAION_LBL), 30 km from the town, and "Вишгород" for an alert over the whole raion. Now RAION_ADJ gives each its name: "Вишгородський район" in the place's status and the list of raions under alert (raionFull), "Вишгородський р-н" on the map (raionLbl), "Vyshhorod raion", "raion de Vychhorod" (lt_unit_r, with "raion d'Obukhiv"); the place picker lists "Вишгородський район" (tests/test_asof_raions_1578.py).

10.10 Feedback and the donation jar (1.58.46)#

The owner: "we need a clean way to collect feedback and bug reports" — "a feedback integration we can reach from our admin dashboard" (with a Telegram ping, his pick); and "a small jar with coins at the bottom right of the map, for users who want to donate to keep the infrastructure and help the devs" (a Monobank jar, his pick).

  • The form: the Tactical page's menu ("Send feedback · report a bug") and the footer's "Feedback" link open a sheet (feedbackSheet()): the kind (a bug, an idea, wrong on the map, other), the words, a contact if they want an answer, and a line saying what goes with it and that no place does. On a page without the sheet (Light) the link is /feedback (static/feedback.html, the same form, i18n.js's words). POST /api/feedback (§12): the page, its language, build and look, the screen's size; the server adds the system and browser (ua_family), never the whole string. Never a place: not the pins, not HOME — nothing in the request names one.
  • Kept (feedback table, §13): what was written (clean_text: no control characters, no bidirectional overrides, no zero-width ones), limited per address (limits.py's feedback, and 6 a day by the daily hash of the address — not of the browser, which a client writes), 300 a day in all, 180 days.
  • The dashboard's Feedback tab (admin.html, drawFB): new · open · done · spam · all, with the count of new ones on the tab (every minute); read, done, spam, delete; a contact is a mailto: or a t.me/ link only when it has that shape. Every word a reader wrote is shown as text (escA).
  • The Telegram ping: the dashboard makes a one-time code (8 characters, 10 minutes, kept as its hash; 5 wrong tries a chat, 50 in all); the owner sends /link CODE to the bot from his own Telegram (tgbot._feedback_link) and that chat (kv fb_chat) gets one plain-text message a minute at most (State.feedback_ping): the kind, the page, the language, the first 300 characters with every link and command defanged, whether a contact was given (not the contact), the count of new ones. /unlink from that chat, or the dashboard, stops it.
  • The jar: a button beside the map's controls, bottom right (#jarbtn, a jar with coins, one dropping in now and then — not with less motion; not in the blackout look), opens the support sheet; with the owner's Monobank jar set in the dashboard (POST /api/admin/donate, only https://send.monobank.ua/jar/…, checked again on the page) its first line is "Donate to the jar (Monobank)". Without it the sheet is as before.
  • 1.58.47, the jar in 3D (the owner: "make the jar better looking, in three.js — right now it's not really standing out"): models3d.js's jar — a glass jar, the lid and a band in the flag's blue and yellow, coins piled inside, one dropping through the slot with a glint, the jar rocking (VIEWS.jar, 36 frames, 4 s; not a threat: not in KEYS) — photographed once into static/jar3d.webp (scripts/render_jar.js, 112-px frames, ~70 KB) and played by CSS (.jar3d, steps(36)): no three.js is loaded for it. The button is larger (54 px), with a gold rim, a warm light and a soft glow every few seconds (not with less motion); the strip is asked for 3 s after the page opens, never with "save data" or in the blackout look — until then, and if it never comes, the drawing. The look's tag (#modetag, day / night) moved to the top right, off the jar.
  • 1.58.48, what the jar is for (the owner: "make it more obvious that the jar of coins is for supporting the app"): its word under it (jar_cap: "Підтримати" / "Support" / "Soutenir"; the button is 72 × 68), a red heart on the jar's band (jar3d.webp rendered again), and once, on a first visit, 20 s after the page opens, a line pointing at it (#jarhint, jar_hint) for 8 s — tapping it opens the support sheet; never while an alert is on, in the blackout look, or for a reader who chose "Never show this again" (sup_never); jar_hint in localStorage remembers it was shown.
  • 1.58.49, the NGO's account in the support sheet (its head, Oleksandr Tymoshenko: "only with official data" — "only the IBAN", "and ГО "07300"", and the purpose; "make it one button, or press and hold to copy"; "better with a link… or a QR"): CONTACT.iban, ibanName, ibanPurpose and ibanLink (static/i18n.js, the same as 07300.org.ua) — the recipient, the NBU payment link (a button: a banking app fills the payment in; tested by him) and its QR code (static/nbu-qr.svg, for a computer's screen), then the IBAN (shown in groups of four on one line, never broken — the copy word goes under it; copied whole) and the purpose (in Ukrainian: what the bank reads). Each row is one button: a tap copies it, and so does pressing and holding it (no menu: contextmenu copies). Below them, for donors abroad, the NGO's PayPal donation page (CONTACT.paypal, a button; "two options — the monobank link, the PayPal link"; the address CONTACT.ngo to copy only if it is missing); above, the jar once set.
  • 1.58.49, the jar on a phone (the owner: "on a phone the jar takes too much space, and it stands out too much"): under 1000 px wide #jarbtn is one more map control — 44 × 44, the controls' dark face and line (white in the day look, dim at night), the turning jar alone at 38 px, no jar_cap word, no gold rim, no jarglow; the button keeps its name (jar_aria) and the first-visit line (#jarhint, moved down to point at it). A computer keeps 1.58.48's jar.
  • 1.58.51, the NGO's fundraiser instead of the bank transfer (its head, 23 Oct: "for Heimdall we use the link, the QR code… the official bank account is too long and asks a lot of the user; the link takes them straight to the payment page"; the owner: "replace it with this, but keep PayPal"): CONTACT.fund (a PUMB payment page, checked against mobile-app.pumb.ua/… before it is drawn), sup_fund / sup_fund_for (what the fundraiser is for, in its own words: military units, mobile air-defence teams, medics — not "the servers") / sup_fund_qr / sup_fund_btn, and its QR code, static/pumb-qr.svg (segno; decoded back to the link). The IBAN, the purpose, the NBU link and nbu-qr.svg are gone; PayPal and the owner's Monobank jar (when set in the dashboard) stay. No card number is written anywhere.
  • 1.58.60, a warning for the next 24 hours is no target — §7 (tagging).
  • 1.58.59, the privacy policy and the Play listing — §11; docs/PLAY.md.
  • 1.58.58, each pin's own near zone — §10.1 (the pins).
  • 1.58.57, the bank's and PayPal's own logos (the owner, with the two files: "it's for the app and the sites — instead of just the names"): the plain names on the support page's buttons (<i class="payl">ПУМБ</i>, "PayPal") are the brands' logos — static/pumb-logo.png (PUMB's red tile cropped around its wordmark, 144×72, shown 48×24) on Heimdall's button and the NGO's banner, static/paypal-logo.png (PayPal's wordmark, its download's baked-in checkerboard keyed out, 179×48, shown 60×16 on a white chip: its dark blue on the dark page) on PayPal's; colours as given, 48-colour PNGs of 1.6 and 2.3 KB; the name stays as each image's alt. None in the blackout look (lite). The NGO's site (07300.org.ua) got the same two on its PUMB and PayPal buttons.
  • 1.58.56, the first run in three steps, the near zone chosen, the approach signal — §10.1 (the pins, the first run) and §9 (what makes a sound, the approach signal). Polish and Romanian (the same reader) are not added: some 1,500 texts, machine-translated and unread, on a safety app — and their countries' alerts would need their own sources.
  • 1.58.55, a pin put on the map keeps its view (a reader: "after zooming in to place the marker precisely, the map reverted to its original scale"): naming a pin dropped on the map (or only renaming one) no longer re-centres the map at 20 km across (homesave: same) — a place found by name or GPS is still shown.
  • 1.58.53, the jar without its box (the owner: "the jar has some black background which looks bad"): one rule after every earlier one that drew a face (1.58.47's gold rim and warm light, 1.58.49's phone control) makes #jarbtn transparent — no border, no box-shadow, no jarglow — in every look; the strip and the drawing get a drop-shadow, the word under it (a computer) a text shadow; the jar is 42 px on a phone, the button still 44.
  • 1.58.52, Heimdall first (the owner: "inside the Heimdall app the top priority should be supporting Heimdall — clean and straightforward; clear primary and secondary actions, no mismatched yellows"): supportSheet() is three things, in this order — the main card .payc (sup_hd_h, sup_hd_b: what the donations pay for; the QR code static/heimdall-qr.svg of CONTACT.fundHd; the one primary button .payb.pri, sup_hd_btn; sup_qr_pc; the owner's Monobank jar, when set, as a secondary button), the NGO's banner .payngo (sup_ngo_h + sup_ngo_b, CONTACT.fundNgo) and PayPal (sup_abroad, .payb.sec.pp). Both PUMB links are checked against mobile-app.pumb.ua/… (pumb()) before they are drawn. Each button names where it goes (.payl: "ПУМБ" / sup_pumb, "PayPal") — a plain name, not the brands' logos. CONTACT.fund, sup_fund*, sup_paypal, static/pumb-qr.svg, .supqr*, .suppp and .jarlink are gone; so is the footer under the cards (footerHTML stays on the help sheet).
  • 1.58.51, the Ukrainian wording (the owner: "some automated translations in the forms and cards sound a bit clunky", string by string): the feedback form's text box (fb_text_ph), its contact field (fb_contact) and its line on what is sent (fb_noplace: the page, its language, the version and the phone's model — "your location is never sent"); and the shelter row's "nearest of any kind" (static/shelters.js, any1: "найближче укриття:"). English and French unchanged.

11. Privacy and security#

  • The four places never leave the phone; the Light page's requests carry no place (checked in the render tests).
  • 1.56: a street or a village is searched on the phone (static/streets.js); the server sees only which oblast's list was opened (/static/streets/<uid>.json, the Kyiv pair by default) — what the map tiles a phone shows already say, and less — never what is typed, nor the street chosen.
  • 1.57.2: the Light radar's satellite or map background asks for the tiles of the ~10 km cell the place is in, never of the place itself (LT.tiles, §10.2): the server learns no more than the push already stores.
  • 1.57: the nearest shelter is found on the phone (static/shelters.js), from one file that is the same for everyone (/static/shelters.json): no pin and no position is in any request.
  • Push subscriptions: a ~10 km cell only; older stored homes are coarsened at start.
  • 1.58.59: the privacy policy (/privacy, static/privacy.html; the footer's last link, "Privacy") says this section to readers, in three languages — the pins and settings on the phone; what ordinary use sends (no address kept, the daily count's hash); what only an opt-in sends (push: the push address, a ~10 km cell; feedback: kept 180 days, FB_KEEP_DAYS, 300 characters to the team's Telegram; a misreading report; the bot); the services a phone talks to (Google Fonts, OpenStreetMap's tiles, the push services, the payment pages, Fly.io). It is the address the Google Play listing gives (docs/PLAY.md: the listing in two languages, the data-safety answers, the beta steps; tests/test_privacy_15859.py holds the page to the code's figures). A change to what is kept changes the page.
  • 1.58: the private channel's bot token (TG_BOT_TOKEN) is the owner's, created by him at @BotFather and stored by scripts/set-telegram-channel.bat straight into Fly's secrets (asked hidden, read from stdin by fly secrets import). The server reads it from the environment and puts it only in the address of its calls to Telegram: no error, log line, dashboard row or kv entry carries it (BotAPI reports the method's name and Telegram's words). The bot posts and edits in that one channel; it reads nothing. Revoke it at @BotFather (/revoke).
  • The Telegram session (TG_SESSION) is a key to a whole Telegram account. It is created on the owner's PC by scripts/telegram-login.bat and goes straight into Fly's secret store (fly secrets import, read from stdin): never on a command line, in a file, in the logs, in config.json or in the repository. Revoke it in Telegram: Settings → Devices → "Heimdall server" → Terminate.
  • Usage counters (usage, usage_seen): a daily salted hash, no IP address, no location, no per-person history. A page the browser loads ahead of a tap (prerender, Sec-Purpose: prefetch) is not counted (1.40).
  • Flags and the corpus review are behind ADMIN_KEY.
  • 1.58.21: the dashboard's sign-in log (§12.2) keeps, for each sign-in, failure, sign-out and dashboard action, the time, the browser family ("Chrome · Windows") and a network tag — a keyed hash of the address (HMAC with the dashboard key), the same network giving the same 8 characters; the address itself is never kept. 200 events at most.
  • The AI reader (1.50, §7.7) sends OpenRouter only public posts (the channel's text, its name, our place names), a card-like run of digits masked; never anything about a reader. Its key (OPENROUTER_API_KEY) is set by the owner with scripts/set-openrouter-key.bat, straight into Fly's secrets. Its readings are behind ADMIN_KEY.
  • The weather (1.33) asks Open-Meteo for seven fixed points (six launch areas and Kyiv) — nothing about a reader.
  • No secret is committed: config.json, vapid.json, .env, *.pem, databases and .flyapp are git-ignored; the history was scanned for tokens before the repository was published.
  • Static files are served gzipped with an ETag (Cache-Control: no-cache): a phone revalidates instead of downloading again, and still picks up a new version immediately.
  • 1.58.17: the abuse limits (§12.1) hold a client as a salted hash of its address, in memory only, the salt changed every day — never stored, never logged; the dashboard sees counts of refusals by kind, nothing about who.

11.1 The audit of 18 October 2026 (1.58.35)#

The owner: "a cybersecurity audit of the site, the network requests above all". It was done on 1.58.34 — the code read, the live service probed with ordinary requests, two findings reproduced on the demo server — and what it found was fixed in the same patch. Kept here so the next reader knows what each guard is for:

  • A link could plant HTML in a page. The #carry= importer (§9, the move to a new address) accepted any string for a setting; myobl is read back as an oblast, an unknown oblast's name was its uid, and the band wrote it with innerHTML — a crafted link, opened once by a phone that had not chosen an oblast, ran its script at every later opening, with the four pins in localStorage within reach and no policy to stop a fetch to anywhere. Now every carried setting has one shape (shape() in the importer: a language code, 0/1, a small integer, an oblast uid, a map of booleans), myobl is accepted only as a known uid (OBL_N), oblName() gives nothing for an unknown one, and the band's names go through esc() — the official titles and hromada names included (they come from APIs, but an API can be wrong too).
  • Every page and every answer carries the headers (Handler._sec_headers): Content-Security-Policy on HTML (default-src 'self', inline scripts and styles allowed — the pages are single files —, Google's fonts, the OSM street tiles, and connect-src 'self': a script that got into a page could not send anything anywhere), Strict-Transport-Security (over HTTPS), X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff, Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin, Permissions-Policy (geolocation for this site, nothing else), X-Frame-Options: DENY. The dashboard had them since 1.58.21; the map and the API had none.
  • A push subscription is kept only when it is one (push.clean_subscription, §9): until then any string was an endpoint, every notification waited on it (a 10 s connect timeout each, 32 at a time — a thousand of them would have put five minutes between a ballistic warning and the real phones), rows of up to 64 KB piled up at 5 a second per address, and the same field pointed the server's POST at any host, Fly's private network included. Now: https only, one of the browsers' push services (PUSH_HOSTS), keys of the right size, 1 KB at most, only {endpoint, keys} stored; the sender refuses anything but https; a send that gets no answer or a refusal is a strike and the third removes the row (push_outcome, fails), a 2xx dates last_ok; a test push goes to a stored endpoint only; /api/push/key no longer tells the number of subscribers; rows stored before the check are removed at start (push_purge_invalid). The per-address allowance (300, then 5 a second) is kept on purpose: a carrier's NAT subscribes thousands of phones in a raid, and the check, not the allowance, is what stops the abuse.
  • The whole-app lock (ACCESS_KEY) is done like the dashboard's (§12.2): a sign-in page (static/access-login.html, POST /access/login), the key compared in constant time, 5 tries then one every two minutes, a session cookie (hacc, HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Strict, 180 days — a phone in a unit is not asked twice a day, adminauth.ACCESS_TTL_S), sessions of their own (State.access, kept as access_sessions in kv), a new key ending the old sessions; scripts send X-Access-Key. The key in the address (?key=) is refused and the reader told to clear it from the history; the old uak= cookie, which held the key itself and was readable by any script, is erased on sight. Pages get the sign-in page (401); the API and the tiles get a JSON 401.
  • The address a reader is counted and budgeted by is Fly's (_peer_ip → limits.client_ip): it read X-Forwarded-For, which a client writes itself (Fly keeps the client's value and appends its own) — each value made up was a fresh Esri tile allowance (3 000 an hour; the day's 180 000 gone in minutes, the satellite and street layers dark for everyone), a fresh usage hash and a fresh daily flag allowance.
  • A slow request no longer holds a thread for ever: Handler.timeout = 20 (a body sent a byte a minute was one thread each, for as long as it liked). The live lines are unaffected: the hub makes their socket non-blocking.
  • A bad parameter is a 400, not a 500 with Python's words: Handler._int (bounds, default) on every integer parameter; the two catch-alls answer "server error" and log the traceback.
  • The dashboard's pages are served from /admin only (/static/admin.html was open to read); _file checks the path with os.path.commonpath, not a string prefix.
  • The point sent for push is the ~10 km cell, snapped on the phone (cellOf, the server's grid), not the 11-metre fix the server then coarsened: the request itself cannot say where the phone is.
  • The container runs as its own user (docker-entry.sh: /data handed to heimdall, then setpriv; as root as before if either is missing — a map that is down is worse), and deploy-fly.bat gives its keys to fly secrets import on stdin, as the set-*.bat scripts do, never on a command line.
  • Not changed, on purpose: the pages' inline scripts ('unsafe-inline' stays in the policy; the pages are single files by design), the OSM tiles' host (a layer that is off by default), the build allowances.

11.2 The audit of 22 October 2026 (1.58.46)#

The owner, before the app is promoted publicly: "a full cyber security audit — try to see whether flaws are exploitable, and fix them". The code was read in three parts (the server's routes, the pages, the bot / integrations / supply chain), each finding tried on an in-process server, and the live service probed with ordinary requests (headers, TLS, the locked routes, path tricks — all as expected). Fixed in 1.58.46:

  • An official alert's words were written into pages as HTML (high): alert_type, alert_level, location_type, a threat's threat_type / source_message, a raion's title — from alerts.in.ua / ukrainealarm, passed through as they came — reached innerHTML on the Tactical page's band and alerts tab, /desktop and /mobile. A hostile or broken upstream answer ran a script in every reader's page (and, with 'unsafe-inline', could send the pins away by navigating). Now each field has one shape where alerts come in (safe_alert: an identifier, red / yellow / none, plain text of a bounded length; in both sources' normalise, apply_snapshot and upsert_alert), and the pages escape them all.
  • Push subscriptions that never answer (high): any port and any name under a push service's was taken (fcm.googleapis.com:81 never answers: each such row cost a send its whole timeout, twice). Now the services' own names (Windows' regional ones by pattern), port 443 only, a 4-second connect (push.CONNECT_S) and no second try of a fresh connection; the rows with strikes go last in a fan-out.
  • The answers' cache could fill the memory (high): 400 copies of a 7-day history are a gigabyte. Now a byte budget as well as the count (State.RESP_BYTES, 160 MB; the longest unused go first), and /api/history's periods are the pages' (24 h, 3 and 7 days).
  • An IPv6 address was a fresh allowance each (medium): every limit, the Esri tile budget and the daily hash now count an IPv6 address as its /64 (limits.net_key).
  • A key in a header was not throttled (medium): X-Admin-Key / X-Access-Key now take a try of the sign-in page's allowance when wrong (a second's wait, logged), and are not compared once it is spent (Handler._header_key).
  • A slow body held a thread for days (medium): the body within 10 s in all (Handler._read_body, else 408), and at most Server.MAX_HANDLERS (384) requests handled at once.
  • The bot (medium): a chat past its messages a minute gets no button answer either, and the answers to users wait apart, behind the alerts (TgBot.replies, bounded) — button presses could delay a ballistic warning; the 5-second pass skips a place 30 km away that no post names (_name_c, cached); a token with spaces around it is cleaned, and an error never carries it (TOKEN_RX, configured); the chats' pending answers are dropped by age every minute.
  • Low: an alert note naming a Telegram channel ("@…") said which to readers (public rewrites it; /api/history and the events log go through public); a made-up flag token rescanned the feed under the database's lock (once in 5 minutes now); a flag's channel came from the client; feedback: per address, not per browser, its own allowance, JSON from this site only (a flag too), a ping without links; the /link code's tries per chat; a body that is not a JSON object is a 400, not a 500; new devices one address can add to a day's count (2 000); the replay's cache bookkeeping; the Server header ("Heimdall"); next= after the sign-in with a control character; the icons served before the whole-app lock by exact name only; the dashboard's esc encodes (it stripped <>& and let quotes end an attribute); /guide?lang=constructor blanked the page; the service worker kept shelters.js (code) cache-first; the channel's esc and the double quote; the Google translation key in a header.
  • Supply chain: the workflows' actions pinned to commits, permissions: contents: read, persist-credentials: false; Pillow>=10.3; build_three.sh installs with --ignore-scripts and checks the bundle's SHA-256; .gitignore / .dockerignore cover sessions, signing keys, .env.*, tiles, databases at any depth.
  • Checked and fine: every dashboard route's guard and the cross-site check; sessions (256-bit, hashed, 12 h); SQL (placeholders only); file serving; outbound requests (built from integers and fixed names); the bot's escaping and buttons; the AI's output (counts and quoted words only, escaped, never on the map); git history (no secret ever committed); the 3D code; TLS 1.3 and the headers on the live service.
  • Left to the owner: the bot is still an administrator of the old private channel (remove it); the set-*-key.bat scripts show the pasted key on screen.

🔒 GET /api/admin/patterns?days=&kind=&res=&min=&raw= 1.55: the direction patterns (§7.12) — days 0–3650 (0: all; 90), kind drones|drone|jet|unstated, res 1|2|4 (×14 km), min (3): coverage (moves, tracks, nights, first, last, stated, cells, by_kind, 1.56.1 by_src, stated_first, built, version; 1.58.37 scale {tagged, followed, af_nights, af_drones, af_drones_down}, tag_nights_kept, builder), 1.56.1 plain (en, uk: the sentences, the first on what they are built from; found), cell_deg, cell_km, cells (≤ 400, busiest first: cell, lon, lat, name, names, moves, tracks, nights, dir, dir_p, mean_bearing, consistency, stay, next, next_places, through, final, final_places, origins, stated, stated_n, said, speed_kmh, gap_min; each share {n, p, lo, hi} in %, Wilson 95%); raw=1 adds moves (≤ 50,000, not cached). Cached 5 min
🔒 GET /api/admin/period?from=&to= 1.58.37: a period of nights (§7.10; at most PERIOD_MAX, 31, the latest; by default the last night that has ended): from, to, summary (State.period_summary), text (State.period_text), report (one night: that night's ai_reports row; several: the ai_period_reports row; or null), periods (the latest 20 written), writing, max, report_hour
🔒 POST /api/admin/ai_report 1.53: {night} — asks the night-reports thread to write (again) that night's report; 1.58.37: {from, to} — a period (one night is that night's report)
🔒 POST /api/admin/bot_switch 1.58.38 (§9d; 1.58.3–1.58.37 channel_switch, the channel's): {on: bool} — the Telegram bot's kill switch (kv bot_on); answers {bot: {configured, running, on, health, status}} (status: its @name and counts), also on /api/online
🔒 POST /api/admin/region {id, on} — an oblast on or off as a whole (1.43, §7.1): its place on the map and its channels; 31 is always on
🔒 POST /api/admin/notice `{text: {uk, en, fr}, level: info
🔒 GET /api/ai_reads?limit=&all= the dashboard's AI tab: stats (key, enabled, model, prompt_v, mode "bulletins", usd_month, cap_usd, paused, last_error, 1.54 bulletins_month, usd_bulletins_month, bulletin_errors_month; and of the readings before 1.54 reads_month, by_verdict, ms_median), 1.54 bulletins (the last 30, with the record each was written from; 1.58.30 facts, what it held — State.record_facts) and bulletin (public, every_min, writing, why), checks, and reads (newest first, ≤ 300; only the disagreements and errors unless all=1): post, channel, stored, verdict, rules, ai (targets, uncertain), dropped, cost, ms, model, error
🔒 GET /api/digests the stored nights and weeks: figures, written summary (EN / UK), model
🔒 POST /api/digest/run `{kind: night
🔒 GET /api/flags, /api/corpus; POST /api/corpus/review, /api/corpus/translate flags and corpus review — no longer in the dashboard (1.19); used by scripts/corpus.py

12.1 Abuse limits (1.58.17)#

The audit of map.war-monitor.org found Heimdall's API open to anyone who wants to copy it or hammer it during a mass alert. The owner picked limit the abuse — not signed tokens, not Cloudflare Turnstile: a Ukrainian mobile operator puts thousands of phones behind one address (carrier-grade NAT), so a strict limit per address would turn real readers away in the middle of an attack. app/limits.py limits what costs the server, per client, with token buckets:

Kind What counts Allowance
build an answer computed because no copy of it is fresh: /api/replay, /api/stats, /api/history, /api/impacts, /api/drone_heat, /api/places, /api/events_log (1.58.24: one copy, rebuilt when an alert changes or after 30 s); and /api/trail (a query each time) 60 at once, 1 more a second
write POST /api/flag 20 at once, 2 a minute
push POST /api/push/* — a whole carrier's NAT subscribing in a raid stays under it 300 at once, 5 a second
stream open /api/stream lines 200 at a time (limits.STREAMS); refused, the page asks on its own
  • A usual question is never refused. State.cached(…, gate=) asks the allowance only before a rebuild — 1.58.24: by the one thread that builds, under the answer's lock, so the pages waiting for the same answer spend nothing (100 pages behind one address opening together after a restart got 40 refusals) — and a refused client is given the last copy there is, stale by seconds or minutes; only an answer that was never built — parameters nobody else asks for — is refused: 429, Retry-After, {error, retry_after}, and nothing is kept for it. Everybody asking the usual window, the usual days, shares one copy.
  • Never limited: /api/state (the official alerts), /api/markers, /api/feed (rounded up to the sizes the pages ask: 25, 80, 120, 150, 200), /api/version, /healthz, the pages and the static files.
  • The client is Fly-Client-IP (Fly's proxy sets it; X-Forwarded-For is what a client writes itself) as a salted daily hash. Not limited: this machine (no proxy header, a loopback peer), a request with the dashboard's key, and — should Fly's header ever be missing — everybody, rather than everybody as one client. 1.58.24: the header is believed on Fly alone (FLY_APP_NAME): anywhere else a client writes it himself, a new value for a new allowance. There the connection's address counts, and a private one — the proxy of another host (Render, a reverse proxy in front of Docker), every reader at once — is never limited.
  • Bounds: at most State.RESP_MAX (400) answers for parameters are kept, the longest unused go first (1.58.24: the oldest built went first — the hourly places: copies every page asks for, pushed out by odd questions); /api/places and /api/history take only known oblast uids; /api/events_log 60, 200 or 500 (the limit rounded up); a POST body at most 64 KB, and none below 0 (413).
  • The dashboard's Server panel counts the refusals by kind since the start (/api/online → limits).

12.2 The dashboard's sign-in (1.58.21)#

The owner: "we strongly need to secure our dashboard — right now it's very minimal protection" (picked: the key only, done properly; no second code). Until 1.58.20 the key was typed into the address (/admin?key=…, so it stayed in the browser's history) and then kept for a year in an ordinary cookie, uadm=<the key itself>, readable by any script of the site's pages and sent with every request to the public map. app/adminauth.py:

  • The sign-in page (static/admin-login.html, English or Ukrainian) posts the key once (POST /admin/login). A key in the address is never accepted; the page asks for it to be typed and cleared from the history.
  • A session: a correct key (compared in constant time, hmac.compare_digest) opens a random 32-byte token in the cookie hsess — HttpOnly (no page script reads it), SameSite=Strict (never sent from another site), Secure on Fly, 12 hours at the most (TTL_S); only its SHA-256 is stored (kv.admin_sessions, so a deploy does not sign the owner out); 20 sessions at most, the oldest ended first. The old uadm cookie is erased.
  • Wrong keys: 5 tries per client, then one every two minutes (limits kind login); each wrong key waits a second.
  • Same site only: a POST to /admin/*, /api/admin/*, /api/digest/* or /api/corpus/* whose Origin is another site is refused (403). The dashboard's pages go out with X-Frame-Options: DENY, frame-ancestors 'none', Referrer-Policy: same-origin (with no-referrer a browser sends "Origin: null" with the page's own POSTs), Cache-Control: no-store and noindex.
  • The log: sign-ins, wrong keys, refusals, sign-outs and every dashboard action (kv.admin_events, 200 at most), with the browser family and the network tag (§11). The Live tab's "Dashboard sign-ins" lists the open sessions (this one marked) — end one, or everyone — the wrong keys of the last 24 hours and the latest events; "Sign out" is in the header.
  • Scripts and tests send the key in an X-Admin-Key header. ACCESS_KEY (the whole-app gate of a private deployment) is unchanged.
  • Where the key is (1.58.22): only in Fly's secrets (ADMIN_KEY), which are write-only — fly secrets list shows the name and a digest, never the value — and in the owner's password manager. scripts\set-admin-key.bat makes a new one on the PC (24 random bytes from Windows' generator, 32 characters), shows it once to be saved, and stores it with fly secrets import (from stdin: never on a command line). A session keeps a fingerprint of the key it was opened with (key_print), so a new key ends every session of the old one.
  • Spaces around a key are not part of it (1.58.23, the owner: "it said wrong key while it was the correct one"): the set-*.bat scripts piped (echo NAME=%VALUE%)| fly secrets import, and cmd, when it pipes a bracketed block, adds a space before the bracket — echo wrote it, and the secret was stored with a trailing space. The scripts now pipe echo NAME=%VALUE%| fly secrets import; the key (_admin_key) and what is typed (same_key) are compared without the spaces and line ends around them, and every secret read from the environment (load_config) is too.

13. Storage#

SQLite in WAL mode (DB_PATH; on Fly, the volume). Tables:

Table Holds
alerts every alert seen (active and finished) — the normalised shape. One row per region and alert type (key ua:<region>:<type>): a region's next alert rewrites the row — since 1.58.4 its start too (before, the start stayed the region's first). Not a history: what happened over time is read from events (State.alert_rows, below)
events start / end / threat / update events — the alert history the replay rebuilds intervals from (§10.3c)
feed posts: post_id, channel, ts, text, tags, text_en, text_fr, en_mt
kv small settings: the VAPID key pair, the usage counter's daily salt, the channels (channels), the key of the channels' public tokens (pub_salt, 1.58.36), the admin's message (notice), the nuclear event (nuke), the AI's switches (ai_reader_on, 1.54 ai_bulletin_public), 1.58 the private channel's posts of the last 24 h (chan_posts: per target, the message id, when first and last reported, what was drawn — never the token), 1.58.38 the bot's switch, next update and what it told whom (bot_on, bot_offset, bot_sent: per chat, place and target, 6 h — never the token), and similar
push_subs push subscriptions ({endpoint, keys} only, 1.58.35) with a ~10 km cell, the topics asked for (bridges), the language, last_ok and the strikes (fails)
marker_log every mark as computed, with its evidence; 1.54.8: and what it stated — alt_m, alt_state, speed_kmh, origin, target, likely, tag (night#n), approach (MLOG_EXTRA). Kept for good so far: Store.prune_log(days) exists but nothing calls it — the volume's size is the limit
outcomes shoot-downs, impacts, losses
channel_stats per channel per day: posts, posts with a reading, flagged readings
digests each night / week: the figures (JSON), the summary in EN and UK, the model, any error
af_reports each Air Force summary read (§7.5): post_id, channel, ts, period, date and the reading (JSON) — kept for good, one row per post
air_reports what posts reported about the carriers (§7.6): post_id, kind, phase, channel, ts and a fingerprint of the text
bridge_events each time a target was reported on or near a Kyiv bridge (§9b): time, bridge, level, km, type, place, channel
weather each night's weather per point (date, place, JSON: mean wind, strongest gust, mean cloud cover, rain, lowest temperature)
flags readings people reported as wrong
feedback 1.58.46 (§10.10): readers' feedback — ts, kind, text, contact (only if given), page, lang, build, look, device (system, browser, screen), the daily throwaway hash that limits it (day_hash, address only), status (new · read · done · spam); kept 180 days
corpus_reviews verdicts from /admin review, merged back with scripts/corpus.py pull
targets 1.51: each target tag (§7.8) — night, n, the letter, origin, whose reading (osrc), its words, the post, first / last time, type. Kept 60 days
target_reports 1.51: which tag each report of a track belongs to (report = the mark id) — how a tag survives the window and a restart
target_points 1.52.1: where each report of a tagged track was (report, night, n, ts, lon, lat, place, alt_m) — the tag's path for the night, /api/trail; 60 days
track_moves 1.55: each tagged target's moves (§7.12) — night, n, seq, from / to (ts_*, lon_*, lat_*, place_*, alt_*), km, minutes, bearing, kind, origin, osrc, ver, 1.56.1 src ("tag", or "replay": a night read again from its posts); the direction patterns' foundation, kept for good (kv moves_v, moves_at)
ai_reads 1.50: the AI reader's reading of each post it read (§7.7) — when, the post, whether the rules stored it, model, prompt version, ms, tokens, cost, verdict, the rules' reading and its own (JSON), facts dropped, error. Kept 90 days; the month's spend is kv ai_usd:YYYY-MM. 1.53: chk, the check that read it (its ai then holds the findings: targets with f, findings)
ai_checks 1.53: each 10-minute check (§7.10) — id (its UTC time), the posts' first and last time, how many, the oblasts, model, prompt version, ms, tokens, cost, findings (by kind), notes (per oblast), uncertain, error
ai_reports 1.53: the AI's report on each night (§7.10) — night, when, up to when, model, tokens, cost, text, error
bot_chats · bot_places 1.58.38: the bot's users (§9d) — a chat's number, language, since / last; its places (the gazetteer's key, when added). Deleted by /stop or by blocking the bot
ai_period_reports 1.58.37: the AI's report on a period of nights (§7.10) — first, last (the key), at, nights, model, tokens, cost, text, error
ai_bulletins 1.54: each bulletin (§7.11) — kind ("1h" / "24h"), when, the window (since, until), quiet, sig (a hash of the record), uk, en, fr, model, tokens, cost, error, and input (the record it was written from). 1.54.8: all of them 90 days, then one an hour and one a day for good (kept, bulletin_prune); kv ai_bulletin_public (the app's switch) and bul_checked:<kind> (when the latest was last found still true)
usage, usage_seen anonymous usage counters

Columns are added in place by Store.__init__ (ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN), so an old database upgrades itself.

Next to the database, on the volume (1.47): sat-ua.mbtiles, the satellite layer (SAT_TILES, §10.1) — an MBTiles file (SQLite, tiles / metadata), read-only for the server, built once by scripts/build_imagery.py and uploaded by scripts/upload-imagery.bat. Never in git or the image (imagery/ is in .gitignore and .dockerignore). 1.48's zoom-13 file is 406 MB: the upload goes beside the old file before it replaces it, so the volume needs ~0.45 GB free for it (the 1 GB volume has it; fly volumes extend otherwise, DEPLOY.md). No Esri tile is ever stored.

python -m pytest              # ~410 tests
python -m ruff check app tests scripts
python app/server.py --demo --port 8099
Suite Pins
test_geo, test_tagging, test_misreads, test_odesa, test_news_and_banderol parsing, tags, news guard, Banderol
test_phases, test_immediate, test_marks, test_prune lifecycle, immediate types, silhouettes, staleness
test_raion_alerts, test_scope, test_banner official data by raion, channels, scope, banner

test_translation, test_i18n, test_modes translation path, three languages, display-mode contrast
